# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 2002

## Introduction by Bhakti-vikāsa Swami

Impersonalists (the pounding of whom was Śrīla Prabhupāda’s pleasure sport) consider words inadequate to describe the truth, and thus meaningless. Actually, the words of impersonalists *are* meaningless. Unfortunately, they do not shut up but continue to spout their meaningless prattle.

Devotees also admit the insufficiency of words to define the truth. Still, understanding the *ānanda* of the truth to be not simply a vague sensation but intimate exchanges of love, devotees employ the choicest of words to glorify the Supreme Absolute Truth, who is the supreme loving person. A love that is transcendental and unsullied is indeed the essence of the Absolute, and although words cannot suffice to praise Him, He nevertheless lovingly accepts sincere attempts to glorify Him.

As a bona fide *ācārya* in the line of Vyāsa, Śrīla Prabhupāda has clearly proved that the Supreme is Kṛṣṇa, the all-loving person who exists simply to love, and who instructs that love of Him can never be complete without love of His loved ones, and that the worship of His devotees is even more important than worship of Himself (*mad-bhakta-pūjābhyadhikā*).

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā festival is thus dear not only to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s followers but to Kṛṣṇa Himself, for it is solely meant for honoring this greatest of Kṛṣṇa’s ambassadors of love. Although worship of the guru may seem irrelevant to the uninformed, excessive to parochial religionists, and repugnant to the envious, profuse praise of the guru is indeed proper, fitting, and absolutely concordant with the gratefulness due one’s savior. Certainly all bona fide delegates of Vyāsa are to be glorified, but Śrīla Prabhupāda should be extraordinarily glorified, for he is a guru extraordinaire. He inherited Lord Caitanya’s gift of being able to give Kṛṣṇa immediately even to most wretched persons devoid of any spiritual qualification. And so although generally a person has to be highly qualified before even beginning to approach Kṛṣṇa (*vīta-rāga-bhaya-krodha yeṣāṁ tv anta-gataṁ pāpam bahūnāṁ janmanām ante*), by the grace of Śrīla Prabhupāda even the most unqualified wretches all over the world were given the chance to approach the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā book contains loving glorification of him who brought real love to a world so much lacking it, who was himself full of love, and who taught and practically demonstrated by his very life how to love the all-lovable Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa by being fully surrendered to Him in loving devotional service.

Within this book are offerings from devotees on various levels of maturity, commitment, and advancement. Submissions by the famous names of ISKCON, seasoned savants and generals who personally saw and interacted with Śrīla Prabhupāda, appear alongside those by devotees whose names and, in some cases, even the cities they preach in are hardly known. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā book contains many sincere prayers; a few literary gems; many personal realizations; anecdotes of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s pastimes; narrations of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s interactions with devotees even today; news of preaching successes and setbacks; and much more. And because Śrīla Prabhupāda’s movement is inextricably linked to and nondifferent from His Divine Grace, this Vyāsa-pūjā book also inevitably reflects current events and attitudes within ISKCON.

Like the Supreme Lord he represents, Śrīla Prabhupāda defies comprehensive description. Nevertheless, his direct disciples and his grand-disciples have attempted in their own ways to express what Śrīla Prabhupāda means to them. Devotees who take the time to immerse themselves in these realizations cannot but be moved by the love for Śrīla Prabhupāda and his beloved Kṛṣṇa that pervades this volume and is the very meaning of the Vyāsa-pūjā book and celebration.

Bhakti-vikāsa Swami

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Bhakti Caitanya Swami

In simple terms we understand that Vyāsa-pūjā means the worship of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the literary incarnation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who descended five thousand years ago to divide the *Veda* and make its knowledge accessible to the people of Kali-yuga. To take it a step further and acknowledge the application of the term in our Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *sampradāya,* we know that Vyāsa-pūjā means the worship, on his appearance day, of the bona fide spiritual master, who is the proper representative of Vyāsa because he has taken Vyāsa’s words and mission as everything in his life.

Let us first consider Śrīla Vyāsadeva. Ultimately Vedic knowledge is coming from Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, as He states in *Bhagavad-gītā* 15.15: *vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyo / vedānta-kṛd veda-vid eva cāham*. “By all the *Vedas,* I am to be known. Indeed, I am the compiler of *Vedānta*, and I am the knower of the *Vedas*.” However, Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s contribution to the presentation and dissemination of Vedic knowledge is so important that his position is given special emphasis, even among the *avatāras* and great *ācāryas*. Śrīla Prabhupāda explains in one Vyāsa-pūjā lecture: “Vyāsadeva is the original guru. After Brahmā, Nārada, then Vyāsa. And Vyāsa is original guru, because from his literature we understand spiritual knowledge. All these literatures, whatever we have produced, they are actually originally from Vyāsadeva.”

When Vedic knowledge was lost prior to Vyāsa’s appearance, he came to resurrect it and present it in such a way that anyone would be able to understand the message of the *Vedas* and in this way perfect their lives.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (4.2) Lord Kṛṣṇa explains:

*evaṁ paramparā-prāptam imaṁ rājarṣayo viduḥ*  
*sa kāleneha mahatā yogo naṣṭaḥ parantapa*

**“This supreme science was thus received through the chain of disciplic succession, and the saintly kings understood it in that way. But in course of time the succession was broken, and therefore the science as it is appears to be lost.”**

How that knowledge was lost is described in the *Varāha Purāṇa,* and has been summarized by Śrīla Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa as follows: The sage Gautama once received a benediction that his fields would always produce abundant rice crops. So when the country surrounding his *āśrama* was struck by a severe drought, he took it upon himself to host many learned *brāhmaṇas* and feed them. After some time the drought ended and the *brāhmaṇas* wanted to return to their villages, but Gautama had become attached to their association and did not want to let them leave. The *brāhmaṇas* created an illusion of a cow to fool Gautama. Gautama touched the false cow and it appeared to fall dead, so then his guests cleverly pretended to be struck with horror over Gautama’s killing a cow and took this as their pretext for leaving. Gautama then strictly observed the prescribed atonement for such an inauspicious act, only afterwards discovering that the cow was simply an illusion and that he had been deceived by the *brāhmaṇas*. He angrily cursed them and all their colleagues to lose their Vedic knowledge. In this way knowledge turned to ignorance, and the situation described above by Kṛṣṇa manifested.

Even though Lord Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that He is Himself reestablishing that knowledge by speaking it to Arjuna, still, the greatest part of that process is the function of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, an empowered incarnation of the Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Vyāsadeva descended by his causeless mercy at the juncture of Dvāpara-yuga and Kali-yuga to deliver the fallen souls in this material world by making Vedic knowledge available to them in a form they could understand. So it is by the mercy of Śrīla Vyāsadeva that the fallen souls of Kali-yuga can engage in the process of devotional service.

Vyāsadeva’s position as an authority is elaborated on by Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī in his *Tattva-sandarbha*. He quotes the *Padma Purāṇa:*

*dvaipāyanena yad-buddhaṁ brahmādyais tan na budhyate*  
*sarva-buddhaṁ sa vai veda tad-buddhaṁ nānya-gocaram*

**“Even Brahmā and other demigods do not know everything Dvaipāyana Vyāsa knows. He understands everything known to anyone else, but some things he knows no one else can comprehend.”**

Jīva Gosvāmī also quotes the *Skanda Purāṇa:*

*vyāsa-citta-sthitākāśād avacchinnāni kānicit*  
*anye vyavaharanty etāny urī-kṛtya gṛhād iva*

**“These others make use of small collections of ideas they have carved out from the infinite sky of Vyāsadeva’s mind. They take advantage of these borrowed ideas like people who pick up things discarded from someone else’s house.”**

Therefore among authorities on Vedic knowledge and its distribution in this world, Vyāsa occupies a unique position.

Today all the members of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness are gathering to celebrate Vyāsa-pūjā in honor of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, our founder-*ācārya*, who is the great representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva in the current age. More than anyone else in recent times, Śrīla Prabhupāda has pushed forward the mission of Vyāsa. As Śrīla Vyāsadeva came when the world was plunged in ignorance and reestablished Vedic knowledge for the world at large, similarly Śrīla Prabhupāda appeared and took the same knowledge and gave it to everyone via his Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, which he said would become famous for having saved humanity in its darkest hour.

Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s greatest contribution was *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

*kṛṣṇe sva-dhāmopagate dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha*  
*kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko ’dhunoditaḥ*

**“This** ***Bhāgavata Purāṇa*** **is as brilliant as the sun, and it has arisen just after the departure of Lord Kṛṣṇa to His own abode, accompanied by religion, knowledge, etc. Persons who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of ignorance in the Age of Kali shall get light from this** ***Purāṇa*****.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.3.43)**

However, when Śrīla Prabhupāda came, the great light of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* was almost completely obscured due to the efforts of impersonalists, professional reciters, caste *brāhmaṇas,* and mental speculators, what to speak of the overall materialistic tendencies of the people in general. Under these trying conditions, His Divine Grace took this emperor among scriptures, the crest jewel in the crown of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, and recovered it, presenting it in its purity for the upliftment of suffering humanity. As Śrīla Vyāsadeva thought deeply about how to present the complexities of the Vedic knowledge to everyone, similarly Śrīla Prabhupāda spent long hours translating and commenting on Vyāsa’s work, taxing his brain to deliver the same truths of devotional service in a form understandable to us. Therefore we are worshiping Śrīla Prabhupāda today on his Vyāsa-pūjā day, the 106th anniversary of his appearance in this world, as the perfect representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

Śrīla Prabhupāda himself said:

So this Vyāsa-pūjā means one day in a year, on the birthday of the spiritual master, because he is representative of Vyāsa, he is delivering the same knowledge which has come down by disciplic succession without any change, he is offered the respect. This is called Vyāsa-pūjā. And the spiritual master receives all honor, all contribution, on behalf of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, not for his person. Just like in our country when there was British rule, there was a viceroy, a king’s representative. So naturally, when viceroy used to go to some meeting, many people used to present valuable jewels, valuable, because just to honor him. But the law was that not a single of the jewels or contribution the viceroy could touch. It was going to the royal treasury. The viceroy could accept on behalf of the king all contribution, but it goes to the king. Similarly, this day, Vyāsa-pūjā day, whatever honor, contribution, and feelings is being offered to the spiritual master, it is Again, as we have received the knowledge from upwards, similarly, this respect also goes from downward to the upward. This is the process. So as the spiritual master is teacher of the student, he has to teach the disciple how to send back his respect and contribution to God. This is called Vyāsa-pūjā.

In these ways we can appreciate the importance of Śrīla Vyāsadeva and of Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we can understand the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā.

Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Adore Ye, Adore Ye All the Happy Day

*Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote the following poem in February 1935, on the occasion of the Vyāsa-pūjā celebration of his spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. It delighted Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta, especially the sixth stanza, which he felt captured the essence of his preaching against the Māyāvādīs. After reading this poem, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī was especially keen that Śrīla Prabhupāda preach and write in English.*

Adore, adore ye all  
The happy day,  
Blessed than Heaven,  
Sweeter than May,  
When He appeared at Puri,  
The holy place,  
My Lord and Master,  
His Divine Grace.

Oh! my Master,  
The evangelic angel,  
Give us thy light,  
Light up thy candle.  
Struggle for existence  
A Human race,  
The only hope  
His Divine Grace.

Misled we are,  
All going astray.  
Save us Lord,  
Our fervent pray.  
Wonder thy ways  
To turn our face.  
Adore thy feet,  
Your Divine Grace.

Forgotten Krishna,  
We fallen soul  
Paying most heavy  
The illusion’s toll.  
Darkness around,  
All untrace.  
The only hope  
His Divine Grace.

Message of service  
Thou has brought,  
A healthful life  
As Chaitanya wrought.  
Unknown to all,  
It’s full of brace.  
That’s your gift,  
Your Divine Grace.

Absolute is sentient  
Thou hast proved.  
Impersonal calamity  
Thou hast moved.  
This gives us a life  
Anew and fresh.  
Worship thy feet,  
Your Divine Grace.

Had you not come,  
Who had told  
The message of Krishna,  
Forceful and bold?  
That’s your right,  
You have the mace.  
Save me a fallen,  
Your Divine Grace.

The line of service  
As drawn by you  
Is pleasing and healthy  
Like morning dew.  
The oldest of all,  
But in new dress.  
Miracle done,  
Your Divine Grace.

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Anuttama dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

The auspicious anniversary of your appearance is a day when your followers and admirers pause to remember and meditate on your wonderful qualities, the inspiration you continue to provide for us, and your many gifts to us. In this way, we rededicate ourselves to your service and the spreading of the mission of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Like an estate built by a loving father or grandfather for the enjoyment and security of his descendants, your gifts surround us and nurture us in your physical absence. As I write, I sit in Māyāpur relishing the peace of the holy *dhāma,* the beauty of the Bengali rice fields, the melodious *kīrtana* floating over the evening sky, and the nearby *samādhi* glowing brighter as nighttime falls. Without you, I would know of none of this.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given so many gifts to the world. This Vyāsa-pūjā book overflows with acknowledgement of them. Today I would like to glorify you by especially appreciating just one of these gifts—perhaps your greatest gift to us—your devotees.

Without your disciples, grand-disciples, and others who worship you as *ācārya,* I would have little grasp of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is only in the association of your devotees that I am reminded Who is my real friend, and who, conversely, wishes only to deceive me. By your devotees’ friendship, example, encouragement, and correction I continue on the path; I hope against hope to someday develop *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

Lord Śiva declared that the highest object of worship is Lord Viṣṇu, but that higher still is the worship of Lord Viṣṇu’s servant, the Vaiṣṇava. Therefore, worship of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the highest form of *pūjā*. Yet, to worship you I must also worship your servants. To serve you I must also serve, and care for, your servants.

Romapāda Swami has a beautiful photograph of you set over a quotation (I believe from Gandhi) that says the way to glorify a man is not to install his statue but to build a community devoted to the ideals he taught.

In your “Seven Purposes of ISKCON” you clarify the ideals of the communities you want to build. First, your communities shall “Systematically propagate spiritual knowledge to society at large and educate all peoples in the techniques of spiritual life in order to check the imbalance of values in life and to achieve real unity and peace in the world.” Your vision was to uplift all of mankind and to leave no one behind. As you stated, “I am unable to think small.”

In your “Seven Purposes” you stress again and again service of the members of the society: “To bring the members of the Society together with each other and nearer to Krishna, the prime entity.” “To erect for the members and for society at large a holy place of transcendental pastimes. “To bring the members closer together for a simpler and more natural way of life.”

These are your members, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your disciples, and your followers. You stress so much their care, because you care for them. And we must also learn to care. As the numbers of your devotees grow, so does their diversity. Outside my door *gṛhastha* couples chat in Chinese, Swedish women discuss the morning’s class, and Bengali *brahmacārīs* rush to *kīrtana*.

If I want to love and serve you, Prabhupāda, I must first learn to appreciate, love, and serve them. They are your gifts to me. It is they who give me the opportunity to serve. It is they who remind me that I am not God, but His servant. It is they who give me hope that I can continue on the path of devotion with enthusiasm. It is they whom I must genuinely learn to serve with a sincere heart.

This year, as the Māyāpur festival closes, we mourn the loss of several of your dearest devotees. The departure of His Holiness Tamāl Krishna Goswami, Her Grace Vṛndāvaneśvarī dāsī, Her Grace Sunītā dāsī, Śrīmān George Harrison, and others reminds me how precious is the association of devotees. In remembering and appreciating you, I pray that you will help me remember and better appreciate your servants.

Devotees are a fleeting treasure. Please bless me to serve them with a humble heart.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Anuttama dāsa

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As you lay on your disappearance bed, with your disciples holding the dictaphone close to your mouth to catch your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* commentaries, you said “Let them know that I am still here and still beating on my *bṛhat-mṛdaṅga.*”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, close to a quarter of a century later you are still here, and still beating on your *bṛhat-mṛdaṅga*. Your mercy is still flowing via your ISKCON and your faithful followers. At the beginning of this year’s GBC meetings in Māyāpur, the members each gave a short summary of the preaching in their area of responsibility. What follows are some highlights from those reports.

Your *Bhagavad-gītā* has just come out in Burmese and Swahili. Ten million copies of the first edition of your Russian *Gītā* have already been sold. The second edition has now come out, with 2,500 copies selling on the very first day.

From the one hundred plus temples and farms at the time of your physical departure, your movement has grown to more than four hundred, with beautiful new temples opening in Surat, Siligiri, Dublin, Tanzania, Utah (the heartland of the Mormons), and even in the hometown of Kali, Las Vegas.

After a lecture you gave in October 1973 in Bombay, you stood on the steps of the Bharatiya Vidya Bhavan, pointed down the street, and said, “We should have a temple here.” By the inconceivable power of your merciful glance, at that exact spot there is now the splendid sandstone temple of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha, with up to two thousand guests coming for the Sunday program.

Every week two hundred guests attend the Sunday feast at your temple in Ulan Bator, Mongolia; the chief mullah of Uzbekistan, convinced by expert preaching, embraced one of your *sannyāsī* disciples; and fifty devotees from Kazakhastan came to this year’s Māyāpur festival to chant and dance—next to them in the *kīrtana* were fifty devotees from China.

Your South African Food for Life program distributed over one million plates of *prasādam* last year, while the president of the country declared at your Ratha-yātrā, “This is the real national relief and development program.”

In Murmansk, Russia, above the Arctic Circle, the devotee bakery is distributing *prasādam* cakes by the thousands, the different styles given clever names like “Shyamasundar,” “Govinda,” and “Jai Radhe.”

Every year sixty thousand to eighty thousand people attend the Janmāṣṭamī celebration at Bhaktivedanta Manor, while Oscar-winning movie stars shop at your gift store and take *prasādam* at your restaurant in Los Angeles.

Although you are the *sena-pati bhakta,* the great devotee general predicted by Lord Nityānanda to come and lead the Vaiṣṇavas, you remained a model of humility. Once you were asked, “Your Godbrothers who came to the West went to England and Germany. Why did you choose to come to America?” You replied, “They went and came back to India without accomplishing anything. So I thought, ‘If I am going to fail, at least let me fail in a different place.’”

At the same time, on rare occasions, you would offer a glimpse into your intimate relationship with Kṛṣṇa. Once you cooked some *kachoris,* and when one devotee tasted them he remarked, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have never had such wonderful *kachoris*!” You said, “Oh, yes, that’s because this is Mother Yaśodā’s recipe.”

Others advertise themselves as *jagat-guru* or *mahā-bhāgavata,* but you, although decorated with all good qualities and the deepest of realizations, would claim that you were only the humble servant of your Guru Mahārāja. As one judges a thing by the results, the whole world can now see the fruit born from your faith and fidelity to the order of your Guru Mahārāja.

On this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I pray that I may truly become your humble servant and thus be allowed to have some small role in helping you and your exalted devotees play your *bṛhat-mṛdaṅga*.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear lord and master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to you and your incomparable service to Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda!

I am writing this offering to you on the appearance day of Lord Nityānanda, and thinking how you manifest and embody so many of the qualities He is the reservoir of. That is only natural, as you are His most intimate and surrendered representative, and therefore you reflect His sublimely merciful character in so many ways.

Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura sings:

*dīna-hīna jata chilo, hari-nāme uddhārilo,*  
*tāra śākṣī jagāi mādhāi*

Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda were so kind that They delivered all the lowly and wretched people, and Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura says the evidence of this is that They delivered Jagāi and Mādhāi, who seemed impossible cases, so bad that even the devotees did not think it was possible to help them. Ultimately even Lord Caitanya was not prepared to deliver Jagāi and Mādhāi, but when Lord Nityānanda appealed to Him, the Lord agreed, and He accepted them as His associates.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* Lord Caitanya says to Lord Nityānanda:

*tāhāṅ siddhi kare—hena anye nā dekhiye*  
*āmāra ‘duṣkara’ karma, tomā haite haye*

**“You can perform a task that even I cannot do. But for You, I cannot find anyone in Gauḍa-deśa who can fulfill My mission there.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta*****,** ***Madhya*** **16.65)**

You comment in your purport to this verse:

Lord Caitanya’s mission is to deliver the fallen souls of this age. In this Age of Kali, practically cent percent of the population is fallen. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu certainly delivered many fallen souls, but His disciples mainly came from the upper classes. For example, He delivered Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, Sanātana Gosvāmī, Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya and many others who were socially elevated but fallen from the spiritual point of view. It was Śrīla Nityānanda Prabhu, however, who delivered persons like Jagāi and Mādhāi. Jagāi and Mādhāi were delivered solely by Nityānanda Prabhu’s mercy. When they injured Nityānanda Prabhu, Lord Caitanya became angry and decided to kill them with His Sudarśana *cakra,* but Nityānanda Prabhu saved them from the Lord’s wrath and delivered them. In the incarnation of Gaura-Nitāi, the Lord is not supposed to kill demons but is supposed to deliver them by preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In the case of Jagāi and Mādhāi, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was so angry that He would have immediately killed them, but Nityānanda Prabhu was so kind that He not only saved them from death but elevated them to the transcendental position. Thus what was not possible for Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu was carried out by Nityānanda Prabhu.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are carrying on that tradition of Lord Nityānanda, and you have delivered so many Jagāis and Mādhāis and others, who, if not as demoniac as them, are certainly otherwise hopeless cases, doomed to a state of perdition, completely lost to the influence of Māyā.

Your spiritual potency and magnanimity surprise everyone. So many of your contemporaries could not believe that you were really delivering Westerners. It is just like in Lord Nityānanda’s pastimes, when He delivered one of the great dacoits of His time. Undergoing an amazing and rapid transformation, the dacoit ran into the house where Lord Nityānanda was having *kīrtana* with His devotees and threw Himself at the Lord’s lotus feet, crying “Save me! Save me!” Some of the devotees were happy about this, but others remarked, “It’s a sham. At any moment he will attack us.”

Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, many of your contemporaries did not take your preaching seriously, thinking we were too fallen to deal with and not worth any serious attempt to deliver. But still you gave us Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and for this we are eternally indebted. Indeed, the whole world is indebted to you, as you have made Kṛṣṇa consciousness available to everyone, everywhere, without discrimination. This is truly the spirit of Lord Nityānanda and His mission.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura sings about Lord Nityānanda:

*den śuddha kṛṣṇa-śikṣā*  
*jāti, dhana, vidyā, bala nā kore apekṣā*

**“Lord Nityānanda gave everyone the pure teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa, not caring for their birth, wealth, education, or strength. Indeed, He did not even see these things.”**

Similarly you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, overlooked the fallen conditions of everyone you preached to and gave them pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, without reservation. In this way you have delivered the whole world, either directly or indirectly, by the mercy of Lord Nityānanda.

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī writes:

Without being empowered by the direct potency of Lord Kṛṣṇa to fulfill His desire and without being specifically favored by the Lord, no human being can become the spiritual master of the whole world. He certainly cannot succeed by mental concoction, which is not meant for devotees or religious people. Only an empowered personality can distribute the holy name of the Lord and enjoin all fallen souls to worship Kṛṣṇa. By distributing the holy name of the Lord, he cleanses the hearts of the most fallen people; therefore he extinguishes the blazing fire of the material world. Not only that, he broadcasts the shining brightness of Kṛṣṇa’s effulgence throughout the world. Such an *ācārya,* or spiritual master, should be considered nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa—that is, he should be considered the incarnation of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s potency. Such a personality is *kṛṣṇāliṅgita-vigraha—*that is, he is always embraced by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Such a person is above the considerations of the *varṇāśrama* institution. He is the guru, or spiritual master, for the entire world, a devotee on the topmost platform, the *mahā-bhāgavata* stage, and a *paramahaṁsa-ṭhākura,* a spiritual form only fit to be addressed as *paramahaṁsa* or *ṭhākura*.

That is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. There is no one like you.

On this sacred day I am remembering you and your beloved Lord Nityānanda. Please continue showering your unique mercy on this fallen soul.

*Your humble servant,*  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhakti Chāru Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. Your causeless mercy is my only hope. The more I realize that fact, the more I become fixed up in my spiritual goal.

The day you initiated me—on the day of Śrī Gaura Pūrṇimā of 1977—in the evening, the devotees who got initiated came to your room and offered their *guru-dakṣiṇā* at your lotus feet. At that time I realized with great embarrassment that I did not collect anything to offer you as my *guru-dakṣiṇā*. Since I was attending Your Divine Grace as your personal servant, I did not get a chance to go out and beg alms, nor did it occur to me that as a part of the initiation ceremony I should have done that. With a heart laden with endless remorse and guilt I told you, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave me *dīkṣā* but I did not bring any dakṣiṇā to offer you.”

With a voice ringing with compassion you replied, “You have offered yourself. That is the best *guru-dakṣiṇā*.”

Offering obeisances to you I said, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am offering my self to you. Please accept me.”

When I got up I found that your face was lit up with a beautiful smile and I felt the warmth of your compassionate glance inundating my entire being. For the first time in my life I found the meaning of my existence.

Twenty-five years later, as I recall that incident, I wonder with a heavy heart whether I have been true to my commitment. Have I really offered myself completely and unconditionally to Your Divine Grace?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am extremely unqualified. I am totally unable to do anything by myself, and on top of that my heart is so full of false ego. I am indeed hopeless. But, somehow or other, due to my inconceivable good fortune, I have offered myself to you; therefore please reclaim what really belongs to you. Please use me in whichever way you want. Please destroy all the resistance and reluctance that may stem from the *anarthas* that I have accumulated since time immemorial. Please, never ever allow me to forget that I am sold out to you and I do not have any independence anymore.

I know that the illusory energy of the Lord will always test me and try to dissuade me from the main mission of my life. But you, as my eternal Lord and Master, never allow me to drift away from the shelter of your lotus feet. Please drive away all the *anarthas* from my heart and allow it to become purified, so that I can make it into an altar, install your lotus feet there, and worship them all the time without any deviation.

*Your most unqualified servant,*  
Bhakti Chāru Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I recently traveled on a locomotive in India and reflected on some of your experiences while using this mode of travel. Those experiences (*līlā*) are, like everything connected to you, transcendental and instructive.

1\. Riding the rough train,  
The Raj Dhani Express,  
But thinking of you,  
Relieves some distress.

2\. Onboard the iron horse,  
You did share some time,  
Preparing the journey for  
Healing humankind.

3\. Outlining train venues  
In the C. C. book,  
You’re picking those places  
We should go there to look.

4\. When young, you studied  
The timing by rail,  
A trip to Purī,  
Hoping not to fail.

5\. When married and working,  
Trains were the means,  
You traveled through city  
And country scenes.

6\. Two women onboard,  
One veiled, one not,  
“Not” teased “Veiled,”  
You approved the slap “Not” got.

7\. A disciple offered food,  
Elaborately done,  
“On a train,” you explained,  
“All should simply be run.”

8\. A train through the Alps,  
Your follower thought,  
Was a cure for an ill,  
But impressed you were not.

9\. Your guru tells a tale  
Of villagers and a train,  
Three aspects of God,  
The light, the form, the humane.

10\. You declare you’re the engine,  
We the caboose,  
You pull with such power,  
Giving us juice.

11\. Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
You trained us,  
We in turn  
Train others.

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The symptoms of Kali-yuga are advancing very rapidly. Kali-yuga is manifesting in the form of terrorism as the end result of uncontrolled anger. Every thoughtful person around the world is eagerly seeking peace. By your divine blessings I have been involved in dialogue with members of many religious groups to bring peace among religions of the world.

After the terrorist attack on the World Trade Center in New York City on September 11, 2001, in which thousands of innocent lives were lost, there was a forum for dialogue with UN officials and United Religions Initiative members at the UN Plaza on October 10, 2001, on the theme “Cultivating a Culture of Peace in a Time of Crisis.” Similarly, the Asia-Pacific Summit of the United Religions Initiative, held in Bali, Indonesia, from December 13 to 15, 2001, was focused on “The Pilgrims for Peace.” On Jan 24, 2002, “A Day of Prayer for Peace” was organized by the Vatican in Assisi, Italy, and many religious leaders from around the world assembled, along with Pope John Paul II. Directly or indirectly, many religious leaders have begun to show respect for the Vedic culture. In this spirit, on that day the Pope and all the participating members of the many religions involved—Islam, Christianity, Buddhism, Hinduism, Judaism, Sikhism, African Traditional Religions, Tenrikyo, Shintoism, Zoroastrianism, Jainism, and Confucianism—became vegetarians and abstained from alcoholic drinks. This abstention was the highlight of the news coverage of the event by the Italian newspapers and TV. That all other religious groups showed respect to the Vedic culture in this way was a credit to Your Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (5.29) Kṛṣṇa says,

*bhoktāraṁ yajña-tapasāṁ sarva-loka-maheśvaram*  
*suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānāṁ jñātvā māṁ śāntim ṛcchati*

**“A person in full consciousness of Me, knowing Me to be the ultimate beneficiary of all sacrifices and austerities, the Supreme Lord of all planets and demigods, and the benefactor and well-wisher of all living entities, attains peace from the pangs of material miseries.”**

In the purport to this verse Your Divine Grace explains,

The greatest peace formula is simply this: Lord Kṛṣṇa is the beneficiary in all human activities. Men should offer everything to the transcendental service of the Lord because He is the proprietor of all planets and the demigods thereon. No one is greater than He. He is greater than the greatest of the demigods, Lord Śiva and Lord Brahmā. In the *Vedas* (*Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 6.7) the Supreme Lord is described as *tam īśvarāṇāṁ paramaṁ maheśvaraṁ.* Under the spell of illusion, living entities are trying to be lords of all they survey, but actually they are dominated by the material energy of the Lord. The Lord is the master of material nature, and the conditioned souls are under the stringent rules of material nature. Unless one understands these bare facts, it is not possible to achieve peace in the world either individually or collectively.

The divine peace formula is further explained in the following verses from the *Bhagavad-gītā:*

*nāsti buddhir ayuktasya na cāyuktasya bhāvanā*  
*na cābhāvayataḥ śāntir aśāntasya kutaḥ sukham*

**“One who is not connected with the Supreme \[in Kṛṣṇa consciousness\] can have neither transcendental intelligence nor a steady mind, without which there is no possibility of peace. And how can there be any happiness without peace?” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.66)**

*vihāya kāmān yaḥ sarvān pumāṁś carati niḥspṛhaḥ*  
*nirmamo nirahaṅkāraḥ sa śāntim adhigacchati*

**“A person who has given up all desires for sense gratification, who lives free from desires, who has given up all sense of proprietorship and is devoid of false ego—he alone can attain real peace.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.71).**

Your Divine Grace further explains,

To understand one’s actual position as the eternal servitor of Kṛṣṇa, without falsely claiming this material body to be oneself and without falsely claiming proprietorship over anything in the world, is the perfect stage of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One who is situated in this perfect stage knows that because Kṛṣṇa is the proprietor of everything, everything must be used for the satisfaction of Kṛṣṇa. A materially desireless person certainly knows that everything belongs to Kṛṣṇa (*īśāvāsyam idaṁ sarvam*), and therefore he does not falsely claim proprietorship over anything. This transcendental knowledge is based on self-realization—namely, knowing perfectly well that every living entity is an eternal part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa in spiritual identity, and that the eternal position of the living entity is therefore never on the level of Kṛṣṇa or greater than Him. This understanding of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the basic principle of real peace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, at this time of great world crisis all sincere people of the world will greatly admire your transcendental message. On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, please bless me so that I can humbly deliver your divine message to whomever I meet.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

*namo mahā-vadānyāya kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te*  
*kṛṣṇāya kṛṣṇa-caitanya-nāmne gaura-tviṣe namaḥ*

My dear eternal father, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Just as we know that Lord Caitanya is the most merciful of all incarnations, and that Lord Nityānanda is even more merciful than He, we are all experiencers of this great mercy, which is being passed down to us through yourself.

Last year I went on a thirty-five-country world tour, and I particularly tried to visit many temples and projects that I had never visited before. During that time I did many seminars and workshops, and I was totally amazed to see so many wonderful devotees all around the world. Yes, there are many increasing problems, but nevertheless there are so many determined souls who go on year after year trying to serve you.

Thank you so much for giving us this very diversified and powerful family. If it were not for you, most of us would have very little in common. But because of you, the most important thing about all of our lives is the same. You have so mercifully given us so much to work with. Now, of course, we have a duty to maintain its essence while we find more ways to spread your mission. Of course, it is a very difficult time on this planet, in the spiritual as well as secular communities. But it is these difficulties that can help us grow as never before. Thank you for all the wonderful opportunities, experiences, and associations, and let me thank you just as much for the wonderful challenges.

I have just undergone surgery. I was overwhelmed by the hundreds and hundreds of devotees all around the world who offered constant prayers and special *pūjās* for my protection and well-being. I was amazed and impressed, but at the same time I was somewhat saddened. I was thinking how, by your mercy, I am a *sannyāsī* and guru who has hundreds of disciples around the world. I have so many Godbrothers, Godsisters, nieces, and nephews who are genuinely concerned about my well-being and very ready to facilitate me in whatever way necessary. However, at the same time, the majority of your disciples, grand-disciples, and others who have been touched by you, directly and indirectly, do not have anywhere near this kind of support system.

So on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, one of the main things I would like to offer you is a vow of commitment to double, or even triple, my efforts in trying to reach out with more caring, in all of my interactions with all devotees. I know that if I can genuinely offer you this, it will allow me to be a better representative of yours. In this way I can start gradually trying to pay you back a small amount of what you have so mercifully given to me. I thank you once again for always giving me more than what I am worthy of. I pray like anything, with effort, to try to connect more with this great mercy that has been passed down from Lord Caitanya, through Lord Nityānanda, to yourself, to all of us.

*Your most grateful servant and son,*  
Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble and prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet!

Your Divine Grace taught us the difference between *vāṇī* and *vapu*, that words are more important than the physical presence of the spiritual master. I thought I understood. Now I am in a dilemma. I find it difficult to make this distinction in value. The *vapu* of Your Divine Grace is the embodiment of Vedic injunctions and the perfection of my eyes. Śrīla Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura prays that attachment to the lotus feet of the spiritual master is the perfection that fulfills all desires. A spark of that attachment developed in my heart, especially when you once allowed me to touch your soft lotus feet to place them in your shoes. Sitting close to your lotus feet freed my mind from all anxieties. They are the very object of my shelter.

When your lotus eyes occasionally glanced at me, I felt as if I were returning back to life. I experienced the greatest encouragement, love, compassion, and chastisement as well. In 1977 in London, after I returned from preaching in the Middle East, Your Divine Grace mercifully glanced at me in such a way that I thought, “Here is the object of my surrender and shelter. Here is my lord and master, who knows and fully reciprocates with the great struggle and *tapasya* I underwent in my service to him.” It was too overwhelming for me and I could not check my tears. I cursed these tears, as they obstructed my vision of your divine form.

In 1973 in Paris when I tried to place a garland around your neck, you gently obstructed me and whispered into my ear that the garland was a little too wet. How much I treasure these words, I cannot describe. Were do I find these words now? Other times you sent me loving and encouraging words by mail when I sent you some gifts and a report about my preaching activities in far distant places. These words were written with your own lotus hand. Where do I find such life-sustaining words now?

I think my dilemma can only be solved when I do not have to distinguish between your *vāṇī* and *vapu,* when I am again placed at your lotus feet, when I am again blessed by your merciful glance, when I again come back to life by hearing your loving and affectionate words wherever, in whatever form, and in whatever circumstances it might be. Your *vāṇī* and vapu are my very life and soul. Now I have realized that I strictly have to follow your *vāṇī* in order to attain your *saumya*(beautiful)-*vapu* again.

*Your eternal insignificant servant,*  
Dāsa A.C. Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhaktivijñāna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances prostrated in the dust at your lotus feet.

Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī says that there are fourteen principal *uddīpanas,* which stimulate the attachment of a devotee to the Lord. They are Kṛṣṇa’s qualities (*guṇas*), His activities, His dress and ornaments, His smile, His bodily fragrance, His flute, His buffalo horn, anklets, conchshell, and footprints, His holy places, the Tulasī plant, His devotees, and holy days like Janmāṣṭamī and Ekādaśī.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, what was your *uddīpana* when you were walking in Manhattan and on the Bowery? Syringes used by drug addicts? Drunkards lying on the street? Half-naked women on the billboards?

You left Vṛndāvana, where everything reminds one of Kṛṣṇa, and came to New York, where everything is meant to make one forget Him. But you left Vṛndāvana to bring Vṛndāvana to the whole world.

Your son Vṛndāvana Candra came to Calcutta Harbor to see you off. He was the last one who saw you before you boarded the *Jaladuta* steamship, not knowing whether you would ever return to the holy land of Bhāratavarṣa. When he was asked about what he thought at that moment, he answered, “I was proud of my father.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I cried today when I listened to the story about your journey to the USA (how many times have I heard it before?), I cried when I listened to how you went to Jaipur to get the blessings of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, the Deities of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. I cried when I listened to how you spent a day in Māyāpur praying at the *samādhi mandira* of your Guru Mahārāja. I cried when I listened to how in Butler, Pennsylvania, everyone was very polite toward you but nobody wanted to help you. And I cried when I listened to how you wrote letter after letter to your Godbrothers begging them to help you, how a crazy hippy wanted to kill you in a Bowery loft.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I do not know Kṛṣṇa. I do not fall unconscious when I see a peacock feather or hear the sound of a bamboo flute. I love Vṛndāvana, but I never cried there as much as I did today. Today the words *Jaladuta,* “Butler,” and “Bowery” became my *uddīpana*. What can I do to repay you for everything you have done for me?

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktivijñāna Goswami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Recently I’ve been listening to recordings of your morning walks, one after another. I’ve heard about seventy within the past month and a half. You are awesome.

These walks seem to catch you in a specific mood, that of a lionlike *ācārya* refusing to tolerate anything but the highest standard of purity, knowledge, and love for Kṛṣṇa—God.

Materialists are condemned as hoglike, for both hogs and materialists become fat due to their satisfaction and happiness with stool and filth. You denounce the changing schemes of the politicians and decry Māyāvādīs, other false religionists, and followers of bogus gurus claiming to be God. “Challenge,” you say, “that ‘I shall kick on the face of this rascal, and if he is God, let him punish me. If he is God, let him punish me. I am prepared.’ In public, say, ‘I kick on his face, and let him do whatever he likes. I am prepared. If he is God, let him kill me by his mantra or by his will. Then I will accept him.’ And you are so fool that you are accepting this rascal as God?”

Deep and true wrath was also directed at abortionists: “Kick simply on their face, on their nose.” And at materialistic scientists—“shameless atheists”—whose true goal was collecting a large salary. “What good they have done?”

Once, however, when a disciple had offered an over-criticism, you honored the accomplishments of the scientists, but softly added the core of your discontent: “No, no. We don’t condemn the scientists. We say ‘Take credit as much as you can. But why do you defy the existence of God?’ That is our protest. But why do they claim that there’s not a God?”

Your strong preaching words minimize all but the highest achievement—freedom from death and disease—as useless. You demand surrender to Kṛṣṇa, and when asked to define surrender, you reply, “Everything for Kṛṣṇa.” Your voice, as you pronounce these words, is expansive, emotional, and realized.

I also heard a youthful disciple proclaim: “Often the devotee thinks that he’s more unhappy than the *karmīs* because he knows he’s unhappy.” Your reply was “That means he is not a devotee. Devotee means the first sign will be happy, *brahma-bhūtaḥ prasannātmā*. If he’s not *prasannātmā,* he’s a rascal. He has not entered even devotional life. He’s outside. That is the test. Just like Dhruva Mahārāja. When he saw Viṣṇu, he said, ‘Everything is all right. I don’t want anything.’ *Svāmin kṛtārtho ’smi*. That is Vaiṣṇava. And if he is still in want or in unhappiness, that means he has no spiritual life at all. He is simply making a show.”

When I heard that, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my heart trembled, pierced to the core. Your uncompromising words that had previously exposed the pseudo-civilized, the pseudo-scientists, and pseudo-religionists had now exposed me—the pseudo-devotee.

What can I say? A dream I had of you several days ago sums up the only hope of one in my forlorn condition: I was sitting on the floor and you walked near me and crouched directly before me. I felt an imminent test. You spoke strongly to me, in the mood of a guru wishing to pull a realization from his disciple. “Which is better” you demanded, “the way of the cat \[the mother cat holds her kitten in her mouth\] or the way of the monkey \[the baby monkey clings to the mother\]?” When I was thus questioned under pressure, my mind first tensed, then froze. But I suddenly understood what to say—and do—and what you actually wished from me. I quickly replied “The way of the monkey” and circled my arms around you in a tight embrace.

Thank you for kicking me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, yet not kicking me away. I beg you to please accept both my humblest obeisances and my warmest embrace. Though I will not attempt to imitate you from my own lowly position, please allow me to act appropriately, preach on your behalf, and please you.

*Your lowly aspiring servant,*  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

As I write this letter I am feeling very deeply the separation of your dear son Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja. I am not able to comprehend the great loss that your ISKCON society, society at large, and I have suffered as a result of his departure from our mortal vision.

For the last few years I have been blessed by his merciful association on a regular basis. Every time I was confronted with some perplexing situation, I would call him to get practical advice and a spiritual perspective. I grew to be dependent upon that guidance. During the last few years I was sometimes calling him several times a week.

I knew that you were speaking through him. He would tell me how happy he was to render the service of advising his fellow devotees, as he understood that you wanted him to serve ISKCON in this way. It is so unfortunate that more devotees did not take advantage of his wisdom.

His voice was laden with deep love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Whenever he talked about you, I was overawed with the devotion in his voice. At the GBC meetings this year, during a discussion with him, he made this simple comment: “Śrīla Prabhupāda is my guru.” Hearing the love in his voice and seeing his facial expression as he made this comment, I became overwhelmed with emotion. I had difficulty fighting off tears.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I know that you have called Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja to assist you in your preaching. That is the actual reason for his departure. He is your loyal *saṅkīrtana* general and your right-hand man.

I can only pray that I will be able to associate with Tamal Krishna Mahārāja in the future. I want to remain eternally in your entourage. Please bless me in this way.

*Your servant,*  
Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

When cosmic complexities bewilder the demigods, they assemble at the shore of the milk ocean and offer majestic yet emotive prayers to the Lord. Then they await His broadcast of the solution.

Similarly, when inevitable institutional conundrums baffle your disciples, we can amass in the holy *dhāmas*. Whether known as conservative or liberal, *adhikārī* or *sannyāsī,* we can close ranks and approach you, the father, with humble entreaties for guidance.

In celebration of the Vyāsa-pūjā of the founder-*ācārya,* allow this useless soul to pose a question that both glorifies you and desperately begs an answer.

What do we do about all those wonderful books you have left us? You told us that if we want to know you we should read your books, that your books are better than you, that they are the law books for thousands of years to come, and that while anyone can translate, your purports contain your devotional ecstasies. In Australia you said that even if someone doesn’t purchase your books but merely takes one in hand, just feels some appreciation, this person becomes blessed with a human form in the next life. In Los Angeles you told us that your books have everything any level of spiritual aspirant requires—whether neophyte or most advanced.

Here in Vṛndāvana, I sit in front of Govardhana Hill, attempting to compose praise to you. I am convinced that just as Lord Kṛṣṇa saved the residents of Braja from Indra’s wrath by His lifting Govardhana and inviting the Brajavāsīs to take shelter under it, similarly you have saved us tormented denizens of Kali-yuga from a storm of horrors by your erecting the shelter of your books and asking us to live within them.

Last night Dhanañjaya dāsa reminded us of your confidence in your literary solution to the problem of spiritual institutions surviving in the material world. You told him that even if all the GBCs, temple presidents, and *pūjārīs* leave your mission, your books are everywhere, and consequently some sincere soul will read them and know exactly how to start the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement again. You urged that your disciples strive to get the reward for assisting you, but then you declared that even if we fail you, someone else will certainly arise in the future, just by reading your books, and get the credit.

Obviously we are fortunate to have the first chance at your mercy—and also, obviously we are expendable.

Since your divine disappearance, twenty-five years have passed. I am wondering if times have changed, how, and to what degree. Thus I am begging for guidance. During your physical presence your books were our be-all and end-all. Now, have we entered, consciously or unconsciously, a new era of ISKCON known as “PPB”—that is, “post–Prabhupāda’s books”?

More than a few seasoned devotees and supportive academics opine that indeed it’s time to move on. Maybe, while maintaining our eternal gratitude to you, our founder-*ācārya,* it’s now necessary to evaluate what you have left us and distill whatever part of that legacy should accompany us into the future of a different world and a different ISKCON.

Therefore, amidst the perennial onslaught of time and change in the material world, I clutch at your feet and seek your clarification, since you are the most expert strategist in Lord Caitanya’s contemporary army. And because you cling most tightly to the lotus feet of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, you are the most divinely pragmatic.

1\. What are your expressed desires for your books in relation to the future of ISKCON?

2\. How do you see your books in relation to both training future ISKCON devotees and enlightening the present nondevotee population?

3\. Amidst the burgeoning potpourri of Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism outside of India, do you still assert that your books are sufficient for bringing us to the zenith of *bhakti*?

4\. What should we do and feel about purports that seem to clash with current scientific, gender, sociological, and political notions and niceties?

5\. How are we to commingle the previous *ācāryas’* literatures with your books, especially since you certainly mention other *śāstras* in your purports?

6\. Can your books withstand time? That is, are the purports and language dated—if not now, then in the future?

7\. Is emphasis on distributing your books, by whatever method, outmoded? Isn’t ISKCON without emphasis on mass distribution a breath of fresh air for us?

8\. Without a doubt you urged your disciples to write books. What is the relation between our books and yours, within your ISKCON society and beyond?

9\. Should our senior devotees take the lead in demonstrating lifelong dependence on your books for their spiritual sustenance and delight? Is there a danger that by doing so they could inhibit their spiritual maturity?

I can’t stand on the shore of the ocean of milk, yet for the sake of ISKCON’s future and my own well-being I beg you for clarification: Is it pie-in-the-sky and reactionary to pine for a time when we will all be on the same page (into the same books) again, “like the good old days”? Would we gain anything significant and lasting if ISKCON’s leaders, in both word and example, rallied to exalt your books as the main basis of our cultural unity? And if you deem such voluntary austerity a laudable goal, then how can we avoid setting off a wave of insensitivity and brashness to attain it (like the good old days)?

Maybe there is no one answer to these questions. Perhaps, for your practical pleasure, we should accept the reality of a highly pluralistic ISKCON society, and learn to live with it. If that is your desire, I am ready to accept it.

What shall we look for: unity within the diversity or diversity within the unity? Meanwhile, please allow me to put forth my own desire. Prostrating my sinful body on the ground before you, pressing my nose at your holy feet, I beg for the fulfillment of my private dream. In Atlanta, 1975, you told devotees that distributing your books with the goal of going back to Godhead is self-gratificatory, but when a devotee aspires to take birth again for assisting you in *saṅkīrtana*, that is pure love of Kṛṣṇa.

Tripurāri dāsa: Sometimes the devotees ask if they can take birth again distributing books for you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda: Very good. That is real devotion. A devotee does not want to go to Vaikuṇṭha or any liberation. They are satisfied with the service. That is pure devotion. And distributing books for the benefit of going to Kṛṣṇa, that is selfishness. But “I want to simply distribute the books without any remuneration, without any my personal” that is pure devotee. Just like Prahlāda Mahārāja says that “I do not wish to go to Vaikuṇṭha unless I take all of these rascals with me.” That is pure devotee.

Yes, sometimes I am an author, and the BBT publishes my books. I try to write in a way that if people happen to read my books they will then seek out yours—the real benediction—with appreciation and submission.

But crying in the dust of your lotus feet, I shamelessly present to you my deepest personal desire: I want to go where you go. Let me find you quickly in my next birth and pass out your books as a member of your *nirmala saṅkīrtana* party.

Praying to be an unconditional servant,  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Girirāj Swami

*kṛpā kari’ kṛṣṇa more diyāchilā saṅga*  
*svatantra kṛṣṇera icchā,——kailā saṅga-bhaṅga*

**“Being merciful upon Me, Kṛṣṇa gave Me the association of Haridāsa Ṭhākura. Being independent in His desires, He has now broken that association.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya*** **11.94)**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You said the duty of the disciple is to inquire of the spiritual master. You have called your beloved servant to your lotus feet. By your divine grace, he was also our dearest friend and brother. How shall we live without him?

Now, having asked the question, I feel you have given the answer: by the same divine grace.

Thank you. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

*Your eternal servant—and his,*  
Girirāj Swami

## Gopāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, I would like to offer my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. I also want to beg your forgiveness for not having the ability to adequately glorify your transcendental qualities. Twice, each year on your *āvirbhāva* and *tirobhāva,* we have the opportunity to hear other Vaiṣṇavas’ glorification of Your Divine Grace. This gives us an opportunity to better understand and appreciate the transcendental *līlā* that you revealed during your stay on this planet.

You appeared 106 years ago with a message of the absolute world for the benefit of entire humanity. You appeared in order to help realize Lord Caitanya’s prediction that the holy name would be chanted in every corner of the globe. At the recently concluded Māyāpur festival we had the opportunity of meeting devotees from almost every corner of the globe, including a big delegation from Taiwan. You are the real motivating force behind the entire effort of ISKCON. Millions of fortunate individuals who were formally suffering in ignorance have found real happiness and enlightenment because of your books. It is due to your desire and purity that temples are being maintained and built. The preaching is expanding and festivals are being held. Your devotees in North India recently concluded a historic Ratha-yātrā, which started from Kurukṣetra and ended in Vṛndāvana. Thirty-one Ratha-yātrās were held in thirty-one different cities in thirty-one days. The *ratha* carrying Jagannātha, Subhadrā, and Balarāma was moving from one city to another in the day, and huge public festivals were held in the evening. A few months ago, we also opened a major section of our Delhi museum. This section features the philosophy of the *Rāmāyaṇa, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Mahābhārata,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. It also has a Scientific Section and a Quadroscope. This presentation is being very much appreciated by both devotees and guests. Earlier, we had opened the *Bhagavad-gītā* animatronics show, which features a lecture by Your Divine Grace and Lord Kṛṣṇa’s revealing His universal form to Arjuna. More and more educational institutions are bringing their students to see a state-of-the-art presentation of the Vedic philosophy. It was you who inspired the FATE museums in Detroit and LA, and this Delhi museum is also a realization of your instruction for the former city of Hastināpura.

Therefore, on this auspicious day, I, your unworthy servant, pray for forgiveness for my many failures and shortcomings. I pray that I always remember you and your instructions and never deviate from the auspicious path that you have given. I pray that I may always remember you and serve your order. I pray that I may always remain a part of your spiritual family—ISKCON. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Gopāl Krishna Goswami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. May your glories be spread throughout the universe. And may we help to spread them. Doing so will be to our eternal benefit, as well as that of all living creatures who have the good fortune of contacting your unlimited compassion.

The *śāstra* is replete with descriptions of pure devotees. You have indicated in your books that one actually becomes a pure devotee when one fully manifests the twenty-six qualities of a Vaiṣṇava. Thus I would like to make those qualities the subject of this and subsequent offerings to Your Divine Grace, to illustrate and elaborate on how you have manifested them to the highest degree.

First, Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Goswami mentions *kṛpālu,* merciful. Lord Kapiladeva indicates that mercy is an essential characteristic of a pure devotee—*titikṣavaḥ kāruṇikāḥ*. Who can be more merciful than you? And *akṛta-droha,* one who tolerates others, who never becomes inimical. I have seen so many devotees who display many divine qualities, but they seem to become a bit intolerant as they become older. Yet at seventy years old, you tolerated being amongst the most untamed and undisciplined group imaginable. Most of us cannot even conceive of such a sacrifice. You weathered heat, opposition, lack of interest, and many other obstacles to bring your books and Lord Caitanya’s mercy to the West. Driven by an incomprehensible degree of mercy, you demonstrated the level of compassion attributed to Prahlāda Mahārāja, King Rantideva, and Vāsudeva Datta. You sacrificed a spiritually comfortable life in Vṛndāvana (although you are always in Vṛndāvana) so that we and every living soul had the maximum opportunity to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your translation work was an act of mercy. Most of us are satisfied to study your books, but you wanted them to reach every nook and corner. Even your schedule was born of sympathy, always catering to the needs of the devotees and the preaching. You rose at midnight or 1 p.m. to translate all night, so that the conditioned souls would have the means to free themselves from Kali’s grip. Indeed, every one of your actions can been seen in the same way: no place for even a drop of personal consideration. In that sense, you are made of mercy, and every breath exuded benevolence.

O my lord, Śrī Guru Karuna-sindhu, please be mercy toward me, and be tolerant of my ignorant self, as you have always been. I continue to request it, since I am so unfortunate that, having the greatest fortune at my fingertips, the holy name brought by you, I have no attraction. Thank you for your boundless grace.

*Your most unworthy servant,*  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Hridayānanda dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances at the dust of your feet. As always, the annual call to write an offering to you awakens a deeper desire to fulfill your desires. You wrote to me once, asking that I read your books and explain them in my own words. You challenged us in your Los Angeles garden by saying, “What is the use of your being American devotees if you do not do something wonderful to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness?”

Your pure desire, which you so often fervently declared, is that America take Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously. It was not your desire that ISKCON and its devotees be a laughing stock in America or in any other country. You have given us a perfect philosophy with a perfect practice. Now it is our sacred task to present this Kṛṣṇa consciousness in an appropriate way that earns the respect of intelligent, reasonable people in America and throughout the world. We beg you to make us your pure instruments as we proceed in this most important mission.

*Your servant,*  
Hridayānanda dās Goswami

## Kavicandra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my most humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

I am writing from Śrīdhām Māyāpur, at the beginning of the 30th annual Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana Festival. In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 11.96, you write in your purport:

The members of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness should go to India during the birthday ceremony of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu at Māyāpur and perform *saṅkīrtana* congregationally. This will attract the attention of all the important personalities in India, just as the beauty, bodily luster, and *saṅkīrtana* performance by the associates of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu attracted the attention of Mahārāja Pratāparudra. The associates of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu were unlimited during the Lord’s presence on this planet, but anyone who is pure in life and devoted to the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is to be understood as a *nitya-siddha* associate of the Lord.

Of course, all the millions of members are not coming, but thousands are. By your divine influence, Māyāpur has become a very busy place. So many pilgrims are coming that we are always needing new facilities to accommodate them. The government has become obliged to upgrade the road to accommodate all the buses.

Those of us who attended the festival in your physical presence feel intense separation when we remember how everyone moved around you, like iron filings move around a magnet. Now it sometimes seems that there is no one at the center. But we can recognize your presence when we see so many book tables displaying your books. Everything here is a result of your mercy We hear so many of your followers presenting *bhāgavata-dharma* so nicely. Beautiful *kīrtanas* resound twenty-four hours a day. Again we are reminded of *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 11.96):

**I have never before seen such ecstatic love, nor heard the vibration of the holy name of the Lord chanted in such a way, nor seen such dancing during** ***saṅkīrtana*****.**

Because the temple of Lord Jagannātha is situated at Jagannātha Purī, many devotees from all parts of the world came to perform *saṅkīrtana* in glorification of the Lord. All these devotees were certainly seen and heard by Mahārāja Pratāparudra, but he herein admits that the *kīrtana* performed by the associates of the Lord was unique. He had never before heard such *saṅkīrtana* nor seen such attractive features manifest by the devotees.

Also by your order, the members of the GBC are still meeting here every year and doing their best to carry out your orders. This is another time for intensely feeling the weight of your absence, since we cannot just go to your room for approval or disapproval of our resolutions. But that makes us think more carefully.

The plans for the construction of the *adbhuta mandira* are certainly *adbhuta,* and many preparations are going on for the construction. I am astounded by the *adbhuta* accomplishments of so many of your followers, and it is only by your causeless mercy that such a fallen soul as myself can be in such sublime association. I hope to develop a genuine *seva-vṛtti,* or service attitude, which you have explained many times is the key to pleasing guru and Gaurāṅga.

In many of the remote places where I am trying to serve, the devotees do not have the funds to travel to Srīdhām Māyāpur, but somehow they are inspired to keep preaching your message. I pray that I will be able to make arrangements for them to visit the holy *dhāma*. Your rooms are preserved, and daily many pilgrims from all kinds of backgrounds are worshipping Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava and visiting your Samādhi Mandira and pastime museum. Now, by the grace of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, modern technology allows thousands of souls from all corners of the planet to pay a virtual visit to Śrīdhām Māyāpur.

So many devotees are giving their lives for your mission, and despite the intense efforts of Kali-yuga to stop Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, it is expanding rapidly. Others will give you more reports, so I will stop here.

Aspiring to serve you and your followers,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your divine appearance!

After the Māyāpur Festival in 1974, you stayed in Bombay for some time, and I had a chance to see you and hear you every day. Anirdeśya-vapu and I were given the service of cleaning the brass utensils for the *pūjā,* and that we used to do right after *maṅgala-ārati,* sitting on the floor of the small temple while Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsa-bihārī were being dressed in the Deity room. The sky was adorned with the dawn, and a cool gentle breeze caressed the rare silence of the early morning.

You would always appear at that time, so transcendentally beautiful, composed, shining kindness and gravity. Wearing a fresh *sannyāsa* cloth, with your *japa-mālā* in your hand, you used to patiently stand there and wait while Girirāja dāsa Brahmacārī would run in anxiety to gather devotees to accompany you on your morning walk.

Spellbound at your sight, I tried to finish my service as fast as I could so I could also follow you. When that was possible, I was always amazed at the incredible amount of energy you had in walking so fast, chanting, and then stopping all of a sudden to offer deep insightful comments on what Girirāja was reading aloud while walking; compared to you, we were all like old stumbling men trying to follow a young energetic sportsman.

I cannot but deeply regret how much I underestimated the value of your association. It is with shame that I remember how you would be standing there waiting for us, how sometimes one would choose to rest instead of avail himself of your mercy, how I took everything for granted and lost numberless chances to hear you and serve you while you were present amongst us.

And it is not that with your *vāṇī* form I have such a better record. I still see you standing there in the ever-youthful morning dawn of your compassion, while I immaturely oscillate between spellbound admiration and indolence in service.

May you be so kind, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to patiently wait some more time for me so that I may be able to at least clean this brass-coated steel heart and, even though stumbling, follow in your footsteps.

*Your humble servant,*  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my head at your lotus feet eternally. I am yours, with nowhere else to go and no desire to go elsewhere.

Something unexpected happened to me on the way to the GBC meetings this year. I became very ill and was not able to attend the meetings as a result. Instead, I was taken to the wonderful facility named and operating in your honor, Bhaktivedanta Hospital. Actually, I had been struggling with my health for several months. In fact, I had been thinking that I might not even make it back from Māyāpur. I thought often of the Gaṅgā and the possibility of a final bath. Now I was so weak I couldn’t walk unaided. I was brought by wheelchair into the lobby of the hospital, where you are presiding in your most beautiful loving *mūrti* form. Suddenly I was filled with your divine presence, and I felt washed over by your mercy. I felt an immense relief that was very intense and that completely relieved me from any doubts or anxieties. Although it was Rādhānātha Swami who had ordered me to the hospital, I felt it was by your personal directive that I had actually arrived. During my stay here, I have met doctors, nurses, and patients whose lives have been transformed as a result of your mercy that is being distributed through this facility. It is without a doubt amazing proof of your extraordinary presence and ability that you are still attracting conditioned souls and guiding them, through your various agents, back home to Godhead. Each day brings a new testimonial. This is true healing!

In America I had been misdiagnosed and was therefore not receiving the proper treatment. The result was that I was literally wasting away. The medical people seemed unconcerned about this fact, and this was very discouraging for me. I felt my only hope was to go to India, although devotees felt that I was not well enough and should give up my plans. But I felt that since the problem began in India during Kārttika, I might find a solution in India. I flew on stand-by, without a proper reservation, and it was only your beautiful face that captured my thoughts during the journey. I thought of you traveling alone to the USA on that huge, impersonal cargo freighter, deathly ill and unattended by any mortal hand. But somehow you were spared by the direct hand of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and by His actions, all of us have also been spared.

Since I am here to recuperate and recover, I have taken the opportunity to read more. I was reading a magazine from a Godbrother with a variety of articles in it by various devotees. It was well done. There was also an article by you, which I saved for last. As I began to read your article, I was overwhelmed by the strength of your power to preach the Absolute Truth so clearly and precisely. It was as if a giant halogen lamp had suddenly been turned on, illuminating each word. You were out-shining everyone by trillions of universes. You are the Jagat Guru! You are the *ācārya* for this age! We can only attempt to understand this by humbly serving your mission with every ounce of our lives. Truly, you are inconceivable. And truly, it is inconceivable that we continue to have the fortunate mercy of your transcendental presence. Although separation is there, you have not left us stranded and alone. As you wrote to a Godsister of mine in December of 1972, “As you are remembering me, so I am also remembering you. In this sense, we are never separated.” I beg to be allowed to remember you without cessation. It is true solace in this desperate *yuga*.

*A fallen and grateful daughter,*  
Mālatī-devī dāsī

## Mukunda Goswami

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“Devotees, they are *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī*. The symptom of a devotee is they are unhappy by seeing others unhappy.” (from a Śrīla Prabhupāda lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.1, given in Ahmedabad, 6 December 1971)

Feeling the whips on others’ backs has been referred to romantically as the milk of human kindness. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 22.78–80), you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have translated *karuṇa* as compassion, *kṛpālu* as mercy, and *vadānya* as magnanimous. Elsewhere you render *dayā* as “mercy,” “intolerance of others’ unhappiness,” “sympathy to everyone’s suffering,” “distributing all transcendental mellows,” and “compassion.”

*Pathos,* the now Anglicized Greek root for “sympathy” and “empathy,” also connotes suffering—suffering, that is, in sympathy for, or resonance with, others. This is what we hear Vaiṣṇavas are supposed to be all about. “He’s actually feeling others’ distressed condition” (from a lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.15, given in Hyderabad on 21 November 1972). You once said, “When I think of how everyone in the word is suffering, it makes me cry. I actually cry.”

Your desire to “die with your boots on,” and the fact that you were translating and writing purports to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* almost to the very moment of your leaving your body, are superhuman.

There is an urgency to preach Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message of love of God. And the pressure seems to come from a feeling deep within the Supreme Lord. A feeling of empathy for the suffering conditioned souls, who you said were “rotting in this material world.”

But can Kṛṣṇa Himself be unhappy? Is he *sad* about humankind’s suffering condition? You once put it this way: “The father is very sorry: ‘Oh, my son is living like a wretch.’ So, the father is not happy. Similarly, the conditioned souls in this material world are suffering so much, living like wretches and rascals. So Kṛṣṇa is not happy.
