As long as one is not compassionate to people in general in his devotional service to the Lord, he is a third-class devotee. The first-class devotee gives assurance to every living being that there is no fear of this material existence: “Let us live in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and conquer the nescience of material existence. .

To award fearlessness to the common man is the greatest act of charity. A *sannyāsī,* or one who is in the renounced order of life, should wander from door to door, from village to village, from town to town and from country to country, all over the world as far as he is able to travel, and enlighten the householders about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. A person who is a householder but is initiated by a *sannyāsī* has the duty to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness at home; as far as possible, he should call his friends and neighbors to his house and hold classes in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Holding a class means chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa and speaking from *Bhagavad-gītā* or *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. There are immense literatures for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and it is the duty of each and every householder to learn about Kṛṣṇa from his *sannyāsī* spiritual master.

From these instructions of yours, it is clear that every devotee in ISKCON has the responsibility to somehow or other become an instrument in making others Kṛṣṇa conscious. Otherwise one will remain a third-class devotee, on the lowest platform of devotional service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when I reflect on my situation, I can see my heart is steel-framed and devoid of any real compassion. Therefore, on this auspicious day of your appearance please kindly appear in my mind and heart and teach me how to truly be considered one of your devotees. My case is virtually hopeless, but you are invested with the unlimited mercy of the reservoir of all mercy, Lord Nityānanda and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Therefore I have hope.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Candramauli Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The many things that I had held were loosened from my grip.  
One day you came and chose to be the captain of my ship.  
So many times I thought that I had changed my life for sure,  
But came to find that things were as before.

So many people walking through this world with things to do,  
Convinced that life is yielding all good things, as it should do,  
Smug, unaware that there is so much more to find within.  
You came and showed me where I might begin

Amazing, when you think of it—the things that we go through:  
What we hold dear, what we reject, the partners we recruit.  
And yet it seems, for all those schemes, no lessons do accrue.  
Unknown, we wait for someone just like you.

Buying our happiness, making our plans,  
Staking our claims and making demands,  
So many people we meet every day,  
And nothing is said that is real.

Lives which are shallow, a few inches deep,  
Lost in our ignorance, soundly asleep,  
So many items we do every day,  
And nothing is done that is real.

You’ve given us a plan,  
Now we can understand,  
Now we can reach God’s land,  
It’s a simple thing to take my stand.

You’ve mapped out the practical life of a saint,  
You’ve made clear how to live with restraint.

When the path is spoken, when the bonds are broken,  
Everything is clear.  
We may fall and stumble, but if we just stay humble,  
We will carry through.

You were there to guide me, you have stood beside me  
When I looked away; it happens every day.

Help me be above lust, help me to gain your trust,  
Help me be aware and have the heart to care.

What am I without you? There is nothing I do.  
You gave me my chance, now just let me dance.

So many things that I had planned that never came to be,  
Desiring all those useless things that were no good for me,  
And now I sit and marvel at the wisdom of a man  
Who dared to live according to God’s plan.

The *Gurv-aṣṭakam,* it states at least three times a day  
That I should meditate upon the mercy that you gave.  
You are the link, you are the strength, the message and the plan.  
I just want to do all that I can.

Afresh I want to pledge myself to do my little part,  
To walk the talk and toe the line and demonstrate some heart,  
Improve each day a tiny bit on what has come before,  
Hear your voice each day a little more.

*Your servant,*  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Dānavīr Goswami

To His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktivedanta Svāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda My dear beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Please accept my most humble repeated obeisances at the sacred dust of your lotus feet.

You are the learned, fearless, unfathomable Jagat-guru,  
Lord Caitanya’s commander-in-chief for Kali-yuga.

You have destroyed all misconceptions conceivable;  
You have taught how to feel separation from devotees;  
You have won the hearts of the steel-framed, coal-hearted;  
You have composed the quintessential symphony, ISKCON, where everyone plays  
wonderfully.

You have given the *mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare /  
Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

You have given *Veda* to the ignorant  
and delicious sweet, bitter, sour,  
astringent, pungent, and salty *prasādam* to all.

You have given .

peace better than sleep,  
a Friend better than dog,  
excitement better than winning a championship;  
happiness better than sex life.

You have given .

meaning to life,  
speed beyond light-years,  
mercy to untouchables,  
love beyond time,  
and tickets to Goloka.

Please, please let me remain your servant eternally.

*incompetent yet thankful,*  
Dānavīr Goswami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Another year has passed since your last Vyāsa-pūjā, & here I am again with the same old report card. I didn’t think I could even face you again with it, but delinquent *sannyāsīs* stand out. How shameless!

Another year has passed, & a few more of your books have been distributed, a few more plates of *prasādam* too, & a few more people chanting the holy names on beads. How insignificant!

Another year has passed, & I am still prodding along in the background, trying to assist your mission amidst all the controversies, in a way that I hope will bring you some solace. How conceited!

Another year has passed, & your mercy is still allowing me to play a trivial part in Mahāprabhu’s grand scheme, although I am lazy, faithless, & offensive. How truthful!

Another year has passed, & although the wake-up bell was rung, America slept right through it: “Give a snake good advice & he simply becomes angry.” How predictable!

Another year has passed, & the world is still under the rigid control of *asuras*. You told us that Kṛṣṇa will kill them through this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, but “how it will be done you cannot know now.” How inconceivable!

Another year has passed, & I ponder (as you warned us), “If you yourselves are suffering from the very ills \[fighting\] we are trying to remove, how can the people be influenced favorably?” How irresponsible!

Another year has passed, & I am not sure how many years I have left to reflect in this way. Please allow me to contribute something of value before my time is used up. How irreplaceable!

*Your fallen servant,*  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious  
day of your divine appearance in this world.

As the years go by, my appreciation for you grows and grows, especially .

When I wonder at your astounding accomplishments worldwide,  
As I struggle to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness inch by inch,

When I think of your perfect vision,  
As I lament over all my mistakes,

When I think of your free and ever-blissful demeanor,  
As my mind locks me in the grip of fruitive desire,

When I meditate on how dutiful and devoid of the enjoying spirit you were,As I balk at doing the needful.  
My appreciation grows and grows, especially .

When I marvel at your thousands of purports,  
As I struggle to write even a few words,

When I remember your all-embracing compassion,  
As I find fault even with the devotees,

When I relish your bold and fiery preaching,  
As I try to shy away from challenges,

When I think of how you inspired us to follow your every word,  
After I achieve such meager success at getting others to do so.

My appreciation grows and grows, especially .

When I meditate on your deep absorption in Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
After finding myself focused on everything but His holy name and fame,

When I think of how regulated and fixed you were in service,  
As I daily bow to the call of whimsy,

When I see how undaunted you remained within your failing body,  
As I cower in fear at the approaching miseries of old age.

Every time I have the opportunity to tell someone that I am your disciple, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my heart swells with immense pride. At those times I also become increasingly aware of the need for me to demonstrate to others what it means to be your disciple. That is the challenge I face day after day; that will be my success; and that is how your legacy will be passed on throughout the ages. In the centuries to come, how gratifying it will be to see millions of devotes offering their homages to you, reveling in your divine qualities and accomplishments, and continuing to endeavor, as I am now, to gain strength from remembrance of your glorious words and deeds.

Thank you again and again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for continuing to accept me as your eternal son. I cry within when I think how, for thirty-three long years, you have been so patiently trying to teach this cripple to walk. On this most auspicious occasion of your appearance, this lowly disciple prays fervently that one day your patience will be rewarded as you watch me finally stand up and then race swiftly toward Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Hanumatpresaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your name alone is sufficient to glorify you. Anybody who knows even a little of your service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara becomes moved just by hearing your name. If someone wants to distort and exploit the “house of *bhakti*” that you have begun in this modern world, then he is thwarted by your books, which remain solid foundation stones and pillars establishing the shape and character of your movement.

Next week is “our Vyāsa-pūjā,” and our disciples are arranging a festival in our small temple in San Jose. You identify yourself as “just a dog at the lotus feet of your spiritual master.” We cannot estimate your relationship with Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, but we feel put in that position of being some kind of animal at your lotus feet. What do we know if you don’t guide us? What can we do if you don’t empower us?

All we can do is try to follow your instructions more and more scrupulously. This year we’ve been able to chant twenty-five rounds every day, follow the four principles strictly, always keep a full morning program, and preach to the best of our ability. We are your atomic dust particles. Any disciples “we have” are only such under your personal authority and order. We follow you as you teach us.

*Your servant,*  
Hanumatpresaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories unto you!

In August 1976, after visiting our temple in Paris, you expressed your satisfaction with your visit and said you looked forward to coming back the next year. We worked hard that coming year in the expectation of seeing your lotus feet again. In those days, with your annual visits and with regular updates of your travels, we remained always absorbed in thoughts of you. Despite your busy schedule, you also remembered us, often writing and encouraging us in our devotional service to the Lord. When my *saṅkīrtana* party wrote to you asking if we could install and travel with Gaura-Nitāi Deities, you wrote back to us that you felt such worship might interfere with our most important service of distributing your books, and you recommended that we worship a picture of the Pañca-tattva. You said that the Pañca-tattva are the proper Deities for Kali-yuga, as They are the most benevolent and merciful of all the Lord’s incarnations. Following your instructions, we worshipped a simple picture of Their Lordships with all our heart and soul, and as we did so our *saṅkīrtana* results increased—along with our faith and attachment to you.

Although you had thousands of followers, and we were separated from you by many more thousands of miles, we felt (as did all your disciples) your immediate concern, care, and love for us. Anxious for your return to Paris, our expectations of seeing you again increased as each day passed.

Then in November 1977 came the unbelievable and unbearable news: you had performed your final pastime and left this world to rejoin your beloved Lord in the spiritual sky. Śrīla Prabhupāda, on that day we were suddenly plunged into feelings of separation unlike anything we had ever experienced. In the initial stages of our devotional service we had come to know so many facets of spiritual life. One left unknown to us, however, was the pain of separation we have had to endure until this very day.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, Kṛṣṇa sent Uddhava here to Vṛndāvana to give hope to His *gopīs;* please send me a sign that I will indeed, by faithful and loving service, be able join you again in your pastimes so sublime. I know that *vāṇī* is more important than *vapu,* but if I can be assured that one day I’ll be with you again, then I can take any risks required in my preaching, knowing that even death itself, in the line of duty, will serve my desire to be with you.

In 1971 I was feeling separation from you after your brief visit to our temple in Detroit. I expressed those feelings in a letter to you. You kindly wrote back: “Yes, I am also feeling separation from my Guru Mahārāja, but I always feel that he is watching over and protecting me.” Please bless me with the same ability to see you constantly in my service to the Lord.

The responsibilities you have entrusted to all of us, your followers, are generally reserved only for the most advanced devotees of the Lord. We need your guidance and protection to be able to faithfully execute those orders, meant to spread your teachings throughout the entire world.

No doubt your guidance and protection come in many ways. Often I see you in the mercy you bestow upon the devotees around me. As a single ray of light shining through a piece of crystal causes a myriad of colors, so your mercy, expanded through the hearts of your disciples and grand-disciples, brilliantly shines throughout their service to you. By your decree their service is done, and just as one sees the personality of an artist in his painting, so one can see you in the activities of your sincere followers.

My humble prayer on this auspicious day is that you continue to give me your association and guidance through the mercy you bestow upon these loving and loyal devotees, until that day comes when we can again join you in your “ISKCON in the spiritual sky.”

Until then I remain  
*Your servant,*  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“I am so much grateful to you .” Śrīla Prabhupāda would say.

It’s not that he was stereotypically grateful. He was not soupy, sentimentally gratified by whatever came his way. Often, he would let us know that whatever we had done was incompetent, it was foolish, it was nonsense, it was not enough. We had cow dung for brains, or no brains at all.

Yet so often when we tried to serve him, even when our service was meager, he responded with gratitude. I hesitate to give examples of what I mean—what service do we want to call meager?—but from what you know of Śrīla Prabhupāda, either from personal experience or from reading about him, I think favorite examples of your own will come to mind.

And if he was grateful for even small offerings of service, then what to speak of his gratitude toward disciples who did great service, and over many years and years. When they dedicated their full energy, made his orders their heart and soul, and did wonderful things for Kṛṣṇa, how appreciative Śrīla Prabhupāda was, how grateful.

“I accept you all as representatives of my Guru Mahārāja,” he sometimes said. Imagine!

To his own Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Prabhupāda—his life showed it—was most profoundly and everlastingly grateful. And he was grateful to us for helping him carry out what his Guru Mahārāja had desired.

As Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote to Brahmānanda Prabhu in 1968,

I am so much grateful to you for your kind sentiments for me. It is all Kṛṣṇa’s Grace that He has sent such a nice assistants to me, for executing the mission of my Spiritual Master. Personally, I am nonentity; I have come here on the order of my Spiritual Master, and He has kindly sent you all boys to assist me. So whatever is being done, there is no credit for me, but all the credit goes to my Spiritual Master, because He has arranged everything, and I am simply to abide by His order.

And out of gratitude to Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are simply to abide by his.  
Jayādvaita Swami

## Krishna dāsa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, the greatest spiritual revolutionary leader this world has ever known, on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

As I write this offering I am approaching my seventieth birthday. I cannot but help reflect on the fact that at this tiresome age, when my body is giving me so many problems, you actually began the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and thereafter spent eleven more years spreading the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu all over the world with great vigor and enthusiasm. Can we, who were entrusted to carry on your movement and push it even further, ever show the same vigor and enthusiasm? I think not. We can simply try to follow in your footsteps and attempt to do as much as we can despite our aging bodies. With the current onslaught of Kali, if we can just maintain what you left us, that will be our gift to you.

As I write this offering, I am also looking forward to fulfilling a long-cherished desire—to clothe Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā at the main temple in Śrī Jagannātha Purī. I am hoping to travel to Śrī Jagannātha Purī in March, and by your blessings and the mercy of Their Lordships, hopefully They will accept the clothes that have been lovingly stitched by your grand-disciples in the Coventry *yātrā*. I beg for your mercy so that Their Lordships will accept these gifts from us on your behalf.

With great delight I am also remembering taking *darśana* of Your Divine Grace at the London Ratha-yātrā many years ago. I still remember that moment vividly. I was struck with wonder at the way you were dancing, with so much enthusiasm and spiritual energy. Even when a police officer thought you might be tired and lovingly offered you a lift, you equally lovingly declined, smiled at him, and said that you were feeling fine, and then carried on dancing. I will never forget this lovely moment, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*rathāruḍho gacchan pathi milita-bhūdeva-paṭalaiḥ*  
*stuti-prādurbhāvaṁ prati-padam upākarṇya sadayaḥ*  
*dayā-sindhur bandhuḥ sakala-jagatāṁ sindhu-sutayā*  
*jagannāthaḥ svāmī nayana-patha-gāmī bhavatu me*

**“When Lord Jagannātha moves along the road on His Ratha-yātrā car, at every step large assemblies of** ***brāhmaṇas*** **loudly chant prayers and songs for His pleasure. Hearing their hymns, Lord Jagannātha becomes very favorably disposed toward them. He is the ocean of mercy and the true friend of all the worlds. May that Jagannātha Svāmī, along with His consort Lakṣmī, who was born from the ocean of nectar, be the object of my vision.” (*****Jagannāthāṣṭakam*** **5)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, for historical reasons many people from around the world cannot take *darśana* of Śrī Jagannātha Svāmī at Jagannātha Purī. However, you then did something wonderful—you took these beautiful forms of Their Lordships to the people of the world instead, thus ensuring that nobody was deprived of Their beautiful *darśana* and Their favorable disposition toward them.

You often used to say that your success was actually due to the kindness of your Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Gosvāmī Mahārāja. May I pay the same homage to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda—that whatever successes I have achieved and may achieve, all have been and will be by your mercy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on September 11 of last year the world experienced the shock and horror of terrorism on an unimaginable scale. The security and economic situation of the whole world was destabilized. This is the first sign of the reaction to the slaughter of innocent animals and the turning away from Lord Kṛṣṇa by the world. The storm of Kali is now raging, and we are very much in need of shelter.

Please continue to shower your mercy upon me and the whole world, and give us the shelter of your divine lotus feet.

I remain your humble servant in the service of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa,  
Krishna dāsa Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion of your appearance day please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Recently I visited your *samādhi maṇḍira* in Vṛndāvana *dhāma*. After offering obeisances, I began circumambulating you, and as I did so I had a strong, clear vision that from where you are sitting in the *samādhi* all the instruction, or *vāṇī,* that you spoke is radiating, floating, spreading, and filling the whole sky. You also keep appearing in my dreams. Thank you very much for these amazing *darśanas,* Śrīla Prabhupāda.

I am fifty-three years old now, and I estimate that the balance remaining in my life is about fifteen to twenty years. Thirty at the most. Of course, death might come at any moment, but we hope that there are still twenty to thirty years remaining at our disposal. I am always reminded of your encouraging words: “Prepare to go back to Godhead in this one lifetime.” You used to say that we should join the dancing party of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa at the end of this life. Then you used to add, “We should be like the businessman who does not wait for the next birth to collect his profit, but rather strives to acquire his profit in the same lifetime.”

Time is short, and the journey back to home, back to Godhead, is a long one, full of obstacles. How am I going to achieve this goal? I know that going back to Godhead is not child’s play. I am a dwarf trying to reach the moon. *Mūkaṁ karoti vācālām* however, by your mercy anything and everything is possible. I know the goal cannot be achieved by going into seclusion, but rather by working hard for you, being active, and continuing my *sādhana* and service for your pleasure. I know that I have lots of shortcomings, but I am willing to endeavor for perfection. As Mother Yaśodā attempted to tie the Lord to the mortar, she found the rope always two fingers too short. Finally, the Lord allowed her to tie Him. This became possible by the determined effort of Mother Yaśodā and the kindness of Kṛṣṇa toward His devotees.

I am maintaining the desire to study *śāstra* more deeply—your books—and to become well versed in Sanskrit and in music for singing, so as to fill my whole life with *kṛṣṇa-kathā* and share that with as many persons as I can. When you awarded me *sannyāsa* more than twenty-five years ago, I had an idea to concentrate on just studying and preaching. However, you did not encourage me then to do just that, but rather to combine my studying and preaching with administrative duties. At this stage in my life I would like to take up studying and preaching more as full-time assignments. You did not want me to do that then, and I hope that my doing it now is not going against your will. You gave me a direct instruction to do *padayātrā,* which I will continue to do with all my strength and heart.

My life is full of many imperfections and shortcomings. I have committed lots of offenses at your lotus feet. I beg, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your forgiveness and for the spiritual strength to work on eliminating the balance of *anarthas* in my heart. On this occasion I would like to also beg forgiveness from all the devotees that I may have offended, knowingly or unknowingly. Vaiṣṇava *aparādha* is the biggest stumbling block on the way back to Kṛṣṇa. Once again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless my endeavor of attempting to go back to Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Many, many thanks for everything you have done for me. Thank you for putting me on the right track, on the path back home. Hoping to see you there alongside Śrī Kṛṣṇa, I remain

*Eternally and sincerely yours,*  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Today it seemed that time stood still here in Māyāpur while we have been hearing and chanting the glories of your servant His Holiness Tamāl Krishna Goswami. His disappearance has certainly served to remind me how I should be serving you. Your wonderful disciple was so powerful because he was always fixed in your service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because your deeds are so glorious, our attempts have also become glorious. His Holiness Tamāl Krishna Goswami *kī jaya*!

Navadvīpa Maṇḍala Parikramā, 21 March 2002. It is 6:30 a.m. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are passing your samādhi dome on our left side. We are in about twelve boats leaving Śrī Koladvīpa and heading for Śrī Ṛtudvīpa and Śrī Samudra-ghāṭa. We pass the modern Navadvīpa on our right.

This morning I awoke early, worried about finishing your Vyāsa-pūjā offering and processing so many outstanding GBC reports and letters. While I am happily chanting and dancing in the *dhāma,* all these responsibilities seem to be gathering dust. I feel ashamed that I cannot do as you did and keep my mail up to date. You had no laptop or e-mail either.

Before *maṅgala-ārati* I asked Jayapatāka Swami for his advice: “Should I return to Māyāpur to do my writing, or stay on the *parikramā*?” He suggested a compromise, because I was inspiring 800 devotees on the *parikramā*. One of your grand-disciples agued that by performing the *parikramā* of Navadvīpa, I was helping to liberate the whole world.

When you entered *samādhi* thirty-four years ago, you left us the responsibility of continuing this movement successfully. I am so incompetent, yet I wish that one day I can become a worthy son of yours. You achieved so much by strictly following the order of your Guru Mahārāja. Surely a son of yours should also be wonderful, but I cannot even answer my letters. Besides that, there are many other failures.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, try to forgive me here in Koladvīpa, where offenses are forgiven. Please bless me and let me start a new year afresh, with a new resolve to be your worthy disciple.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, through your instructions and ISKCON teach me how to do the right thing to please you and to fulfill Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu’s mission to save the whole world by successfully performing the *saṅkīrtana-yajña*.

*Your fallen and incompetent servant,*  
Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami

## Navayogendra Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, today is your divine appearance day, a most blessed day for me. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the true representative of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, the saviors of the fallen. Please bless me so that I can become your true follower and attain *kṛṣṇa-prema*. As your *gurudeva* said, this material world is not a place for a gentleman to live. Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy I have lost all taste for this material world. Kindly bless me that I become more and more attached to your lotus feet, which are the only shelter for fallen souls like myself.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything you have given us.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your humble servant,*  
Navayogendra Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You have revealed to us that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the perfection of life and have also shown us the methods to achieve it. The absorption in pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness is possible for one who has completely dedicated his life to pure devotional service. Such pure dedication is possible to achieve by service to the Lord’s disciplic succession of devotees. In this age, the disciplic succession descends from the *yuga-avatāra,* Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, whose *saṅkīrtana* mission is concerned with freely distributing pure love of Kṛṣṇa, *kṛṣṇa-prema,* to others.

Therefore, if we want to achieve absorption in Kṛṣṇa consciousness we must also become absorbed in service to the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You stressed that this mission of distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness is especially concerned with the distribution of transcendental literatures and the establishment of a cultural revolution to convince people that Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Therefore, the secret of advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is that through the development of a service attitude to the mission of Lord Caitanya we can become qualified to experience the happiness of the sweet exchanges between the Lord and His associates.

Lord Kṛṣṇa’s desire is to make everyone happy, and thus He uses His abilities and energies to benefit others. This is part of His unlimited renunciation. Thus one who is absorbed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and dedicated to preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness will certainly obtain whatever is necessary for his service from the unlimited fountainhead of everything, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement does not require anything more than utilizing what we already have and applying this to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission. To a dedicated and enthusiastic devotee, the Lord certainly gives all the resources and spiritual intelligence needed to properly engage His energies in His transcendental service. However, if devotees misuse the Lord’s energy, to teach them how to use His energies more wisely He may sometimes take away their source of material attachment. The conclusion is that there is nothing more auspicious than trying to preach the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Lord Kṛṣṇa will give His servants everything required to carry on His mission and at the same time elevate them to the spiritual platform of awareness and expertise. For success in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and pure devotional service, we need only add to our present activities the sincerity and enthusiasm to engage in the mission of the Lord under the guidance of His bona fide representatives.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rasānanda Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, all glories to your mission!

I cannot help but lament to see around me a lack of distribution of your books. The books are conspicuous by their absence around me. I am afraid to be called a fanatic or an anachronism. My simple understanding is that you wanted to see a flood of book distribution all over the world. My understanding is that you came to us for translating, publishing, printing, and distributing your books. I came back to my country to help spread the glories of your books, but I am powerless and fallen. I beg you to give me the strength and dedication to push on your mission of publishing and distributing books.

We have plans of our own, leaving aside the distribution of your books as a second plan or even no plan. Our plans are indeed good plans, but we should always remember that without your book distribution all plans are useless. Please give me the strength and inspiration to ignite the fire of book distribution around me; otherwise I will feel that my life is useless. We have to understand that without blindly following your instructions we will never be able to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, as you explain so nicely in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 12.8. There you explain that the key to success in our spiritual life is to follow the order of the spiritual master with firm faith. In your purport you stress the order of publishing and distributing books. Please empower me with this message so I can spread it wherever I go.

*prathame ta’ eka-mata ācāryera gaṇa*  
*pāche dui-mata haila daivera kāraṇa*

**At first all the followers of Advaita Ācārya shared a single opinion. But later they followed two different opinions, as ordained by providence.**

\[PURPORT\] . We followed the principles especially explained by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura in his commentary on the *Bhagavad-gītā* verse beginning *vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana*. According to this instruction of Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, it is the duty of a disciple to follow strictly the orders of his spiritual master. The secret of success in advancement in spiritual life is the firm faith of the disciple in the orders of his spiritual master. The *Vedas* confirm this:

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“To one who has staunch faith in the words of the spiritual master and the words of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the secret of success in Vedic knowledge is revealed.”**

The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is being propagated according to this principle, and therefore our preaching work is going on successfully, in spite of the many impediments offered by antagonistic demons, because we are getting positive help from our previous *ācāryas*. One must judge every action by its result. The members of the self-appointed *ācārya’s* party who occupied the property of the Gauḍīya Maṭha are satisfied, but they could make no progress in preaching. Therefore by the result of their actions one should know that they are *asāra,* or useless, whereas the success of the ISKCON party, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, which strictly follows guru and Gaurāṅga, is increasing daily all over the world. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura wanted to print as many books as possible and distribute them all over the world. We have tried our best in this connection, and we are getting results beyond our expectations.

These are the words I live my life by, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please empower me to inspire others to do so also.

*Your servant,*  
Rasānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances into the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

You are always active for the elevation of the whole world—and especially of those who seek your shelter.

Even by your divine appearance day you give us the opportunity to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, for on this day you draw out our devotion to you and receive our expressions of gratitude.

Lord Kṛṣṇa glorifies the spiritual master with these words:

*nāham ijyā-prajātibhyāṁ tapasopaśamena vā*  
*tuṣyeyaṁ sarva-bhūtātmā guru-śuśrūṣayā yathā*

**“I, the Soul of all beings, am not as satisfied by ritual worship, brahminical initiation, penances, or self-discipline as I am by faithful service rendered to one’s spiritual master.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.80.34)**

*guror anugraheṇaiva pumān pūrṇaḥ praśāntaye*

**“Simply by the grace of the spiritual master a person can fulfill life’s purpose and attain eternal peace.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.80.43)**

And Śrī Kṛṣṇa gave a glorious personal example of what it means to render faithful service to the guru when He and Sudāmā obtained firewood for Sāndīpani Muni’s *yajñas* under great personal inconvenience caused by a raging rainstorm. After spending the whole night shivering in the cold jungle, surrounded on all sides by rainwater, they still had the bundles of firewood on their heads! While Kṛṣṇa and His friend were in this difficult situation, Sāndīpani Muni came personally to comfort his wonderful disciples and rescue them.

**Service in Separation from You**

In my own little way I was fortunate to be allowed to have similar experiences in your service during the years when you were physically with us. Whenever I tried to surrender to you, you came and took care of me—rescued me from difficult situations or simply comforted me, whether during tense moments in book distribution, while preaching behind the iron curtain, during massive public preaching programs, in shaky devotee relationships, at difficult moments in my Kṛṣṇa conscious growth, etc. Whenever I turned to you during the whirlwind of life and service, you came to my rescue.

Even since your so-called disappearance this has not stopped. In some ways it has magically increased, since your disappearance is in reality a chance for your disciples to let you appear in their own hearts.

Last year, when you kindly gave me another chance to surrender to your and Kṛṣṇa’s plan, I became so grateful and forever bound to your lotus feet when you again came to my rescue. Although the external circumstances of the once so glorious North-European zone have not always been 100% encouraging in the last few years, last year you showed me that if I simply give my best, the Lord and you are satisfied and willing to help.

When the spiritual master leaves his body, he is called *nitya-līlā-praviṣṭa*—meaning that he has joined Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in Their eternal abode. (I know that at times you said you would just continue your preaching service, but a great personality like you can do both—simultaneously be with Kṛṣṇa and preach His glories). From this it is clear that Their Lordships will accept the disciples’ devotion to Them only when the disciples continue to give their service to the spiritual master, Their Lordships’ dearmost servitor. If one even slightly ignores the wish of such a servitor while at the same time making a big show of affection for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, that will not impress the Divine Couple in the least.

Over the years it has become obvious that I can devote myself to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa only as much as I devote myself to you.

For instance, every day I may chant lakhs of holy names, but if I should neglect you and your service the Lord will not listen to me. On the other hand, if I consider myself fully dependent on you in every situation, I will stay under the full protection of the Lord.

On this auspicious day I am happily reflecting on your divine presents to me. The *nāma,* the Gāyatrī mantras, the holy *dhāmas,* service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana, and full-fledged preaching service in your wonderful mission of delivering Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western countries.

You once said we are “packed together” with you when we chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. This every sincere chanter can experience. In the *Vāmana-kalpa* Lord Brahmā explains, *yo mantraḥ sa guruḥ sākṣād / yo guru sa hariḥ svayam:* “One should understand that the mantra one has received from the guru is identical with the guru and that the guru is identical with Lord Hari.” (cited in Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī’s *Bhakti-sandarbha, Anuccheda* 237.8)

While not ignoring your other gifts, I beg to realize this divine secret more and more. Still, my mind is unfortunately absorbed in countless illusory activities, and it has more attachment to the concepts of *ahaṁ mameti* (I and mine) than your divine service. Māyā is always behind me with the rope, trying to catch me like a lowborn criminal. I am totally dependent on your divine protection and on your merciful glance, which will help all the wonderful conclusions of *bhakti* to prosper and grow in my heart. Helplessly I am throwing myself at the dust before your lotus feet and requesting your mercy.

*Your humble servant,*  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Trivikram Swami

**To the Savior of the Modern World**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

He was determined to such a degree,  
That Māyā herself could not possible see.

And although she tried to break his resolve,  
His plans went unchecked and quickly evolved.

In his vision he saw temples and more—  
Farms and guesthouses, devotees galore.

No one could imagine in this foreign land,  
How pure devotion would be in such demand.

But somehow or other he inspired us all  
To give up our nonsense and answer his call.

And what was that call, as clear as a bell?  
“Society without Kṛṣṇa is going to hell!”

In their foolish pursuits they fiddled like Nero,  
But because Kṛṣṇa was lacking it all came to zero.

By Lord Caitanya’s unalloyed mercy divine,  
You delivered to us this mantra so fine—

Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare /  
Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare

Just chant it, you said, without an offense,  
And Kṛṣṇa will kindly make His descent.

When Kṛṣṇa is pleased with our undertakings,  
Then never again will we be forsaken.

From strength to strength we will certainly grow.  
All by your mercy, please keep us in tow.

Thank you, and thank you, and thank you again,  
What else can we say, except thank you again!

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Trivikram Swami

## Umāpati Swami

**Śrīla Prabhupāda: No Mistakes**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Last year, while browsing the Internet, I wandered onto a website that I had helped to create a few years earlier. It had been some time since I had gone onto this website, and this time I was shocked by what I found there. I saw an article called “Can the Guru Make Mistakes?” written by a member of ISKCON. What shocked me was the statement that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s views on some subjects were antiquated, and that Śrīla Prabhupāda had made mistakes.

This assertion goes against the foundations of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda displayed the symptoms of a *mahā-bhāgavata,* one who has transcended the modes of material nature. To say that even a *mahā-bhāgavata* can have antiquated views, that he can be misled by the fashionable views of a mundane era, is to say that no one can transcend material nature.

Some people say that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s views on women and on moral subjects are influenced by his Victorian education, yet Śrīla Prabhupāda constantly criticized the education in mundane universities. Shall we now say that all the while Śrīla Prabhupāda was criticizing mundane education he was under its influence?

Here is passage that proves that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s views on morality are not conditioned by any mundane period or any university:

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Yes. You do not know what is Kṛṣṇa. That is the Kṛṣṇa must have everything. Otherwise how He is God? God’s definition is that everything is emanating from Kṛṣṇa. *Janmādy asya yataḥ*. He’s God, the original source of anything. Anything and everything. Then He’s God. So if sex life is not in Kṛṣṇa, then wherefrom it comes? Does it drop from the sky? You rascal, you do not know the science.

**Hari-śauri:** And with Kṛṣṇa’s sex there’s no inebrieties.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Hmm?

**Hari-śauri:** When Kṛṣṇa has sex life there’s no inebrieties. There’s no abortion and contraception. With our sex life there’s so many bad things.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** No, first of all, wherefrom the sex life comes if it is not in God? You make God impotent. But we do not make. Why He should be impotent? Then He’s imperfect. If He’s not potent, then He’s imperfect, and God is all-perfect.

**Satsvarūpa:** They take it that this is an example of anthropomorphic, that we are saying .

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Whatever it may be.

**Satsvarūpa:** “God has sex.”

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** They are luscious(?) that \[break\] Just like a tuberculosis patient. To him doctor says that “You don’t have sex life. That will bring your death.” Does it mean sex life is bad? The tuberculosis person, for him it is bad, not for the sane man, not for the healthy man. So when sex life is advised to , forbidden, that is for the diseased condition. But who is never diseased—He is perfect—for Him there is no forbidding of sex life. So you do not understand that in this material condition you are suffering only. You have no brain. Therefore morality, immorality, good, bad, there are so many things. But when one is perfect, healthy, for Him all the activities of life is perfect. Just like a physician advised me, “You don’t take salt.” Does it mean salt is bad? I am in a particular condition of this kidney trouble or liver trouble. For me salt is bad. But does it mean salt bad?

**Gurukṛpa:** No. It’s very good.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Similarly, sex life for you is bad, not for Kṛṣṇa. You are thinking Kṛṣṇa like you, *mūḍha*.

**Gargamuni:** Yes. They try to equate themselves.

**Gurukṛpa:** Sex-monger.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Yes. You are taking Kṛṣṇa on your level. You are such a rascal. You do not know Kṛṣṇa. Therefore Kṛṣṇa says, *manuṣyāṇāṁ sahasreṣu kaścid yatati siddhaye yatatām api siddhānāṁ kaścid*. You do not know what is Kṛṣṇa. You are taking Him on your level. Therefore you are trying to criticize Him. You do not know what is Kṛṣṇa. That is your ignorance. What is food for one is poison for others. So for you it is poison, not for Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is God. He has everything. He can use everything. That is God. He is not restricted by anyone. *Mattaḥ parataraṁ nānyat*. Who can restrict Him? Then He’s not God. If by a third-class man like you He’s restricted of His sex life, then He’s not God. He becomes under your control. But Kṛṣṇa is *īśvaraḥ paramaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ*. He’s the supreme controller. How He can be controlled by your so-called goodness and badness? So the conclusion is you do not know Kṛṣṇa. You want to bring Kṛṣṇa in your level of understanding, and that is your foolishness. So we don’t care for the fools like you. This is our conclusion. *Avajānanti* That is replied. *Avajānanti māṁ mūḍhāḥ:* “Rascals, fools, they criticize Me, think Me that I am as good as human being.” *Mānuṣīṁ janma. Paraṁ bhāvam ajānantaḥ:* “The rascals do not know what is My actual position.” So you have to preach in that way. \[evening conversation, 25 January 1977\]

In short, Śrīla Prabhupāda says that sex is not a bad thing, that God can have sex but we cannot because we are diseased. This is not Victorian philosophy, nor is it 19th- or 20th-century philosophy, and I doubt that Śrīla Prabhupāda learned this at his university. It is pure spiritual truth coupled with a clear vision of the material world, and all of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s moral teachings are extensions of this principle.

Concerning women, Śrīla Prabhupāda says that we are not these bodies. Again, this is not an antiquated view, and Śrīla Prabhupāda knew how to engage each person according to the person’s particular situation. Some people say that Śrīla Prabhupāda was engaging women according to Victorian concepts, but do we find women worshiping Deities in the Victorian era?

Again, some people say that Śrīla Prabhupāda made a mistake by establishing *gurukulas* and giving *sannyāsa* to people who later fell down, but establishing the system of *gurukula* and the order of *sannyāsa* in our society was not a mistake. It had to be done. The mistakes were made by those who did not live up to the ideal.

Unfortunately, some people in our movement are so attached to what they consider to be the enlightened conceptions of modern society that they want to relegate Śrīla Prabhupāda to some by-gone mundane era. Not only that, but I have been told that this spiritual sickness is spreading throughout our ISKCON society.

These people, however, admit that Śrīla Prabhupāda has perfect knowledge of the spiritual world. If that is so, then Śrīla Prabhupāda is certainly influenced by a culture and an era, but it is the eternal culture and the eternal era of the spiritual world, and if we want to enter into this world, then we have to accept Śrīla Prabhupāda’s vision of perfect culture and strive to establish it in this world. Vṛndāvana is identical to Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, and if we prefer the mundane culture of the twenty-first century to that of Vṛndāvana, how can we hope to attain love of Kṛṣṇa?

Therefore, I call on every guru, *sannyāsī,* and other teacher in ISKCON to put Śrīla Prabhupāda back in the center and make everyone understand that everything about Śrīla Prabhupāda is perfect. I urge every teacher to impress on everyone the importance of accepting Śrīla Prabhupāda’s vision of things in its entirety. Otherwise, we will remain hopelessly stuck in this material world.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Umāpati Swami

## Vedavyāsapriya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

For a sincere devotee, the material tribulations are just a token of lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s mercy for his spiritual progress. He does not complain. He welcomes them and faces them for his further purification. This is devotional service. When I compare my nothingness in front of the excellent example of one of the most exalted, intimate, and pure devotees of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa—Queen Kuntī-devī, who asks the Lord to give her the same troubles over and over again so she will remain totally dependent upon the mercy of the Lord—it seems I expect too much from myself.

To sustain my devotional service, who else can I turn to other than your followers and your institution? When you left after giving me my spiritual birth, I was raised by your disciples, my Godbrothers. I am made of their mercy. Governed by them, I can sustain my devotional service in your institution. I simply cooperate the way they want me to. Otherwise, I could not have survived the bitter taste of the many political situations I’ve experienced, faced the various emergencies imposed upon me, or tolerated all the inconveniences and obstacles that could have deviated me from your service. Amazingly, I am still surviving in your house despite all these obstacles, simply due to the mercy of my Godbrothers. So, how could I ever afford to leave such a shelter?

Please rest assured that these tribulations give me more and more opportunity to realize the absoluteness of Your Divine Grace’s mercy. Therefore I beg you on this auspicious occasion of your divine appearance day, following Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, *vedavyāsapriya koy, nā ṭheliho rāṅgā pāy, tomā bine ke āche āmāra*?

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*A servant of your servants,*  
Vedavyāsapriya Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

**Is It Okay?**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I wonder if this is okay. Another day, another year. We who are living still, you who are living still in sound.

I wonder when I will see you again, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I mean in the flesh. I mean with my eyes, three dimensionally. When will that day come when I can sit with you again? When will that day come when I can ask you a question and watch you turn your lotus eyes in my direction? Is that okay?

I miss you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, even though I know very well you are always with us. Is it a crime to miss you even though you are still with us? I don’t know. I just know that I miss you. I yearn for the time when you would visit the temple and the devotees would ecstatically greet you with a *kīrtana*. Is that okay?

I remember the times you would sit on the *vyāsāsana* while we would ecstatically jump up and down in front of you, and you would nod approvingly as you played the gong. I remember the struggle I went through to buy you a hammer for that gong, and how you seemed to appreciate it so much when you played it. Is that okay?

I miss the time you came into the temple room in New York and I didn’t have the “Govindam” tape ready, and you asked me, “Is it ready yet?” I miss those little things the most. Is that wrong? What about in Vṛndāvana when I used to give you the garland every morning after I gave you *caraṇāmṛta*? Oh, those good old days. I miss them.

Anyway, I’m still here, waiting. Holding the fort. Giving classes. Encouraging others. Listening to your voice on tape. Growing older. Chanting, still. Missing you. Struggling to survive in spiritual life. Trying to please you.

Hope it’s okay. I don’t have much to offer. I don’t know how to offer much.

*Your servant,*  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Adelaide

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

1\. Through myriad forms of life I came,  
Thinking that the human form was the same.  
Just to rise to the crest of the wave,  
Only to be plunged to another grave.

2\. Distressed by the life I was leading,  
For relief my real self was pleading.  
The years of suffering kept growing,  
The tide of my karma kept flowing.

3\. Then you entered into my life,  
Miraculously ending all my strife.  
By your intervention a plan took shape,  
This human form was meant for escape.

4\. Somewhere in the sands of eternity,  
We met by chance inconceivably.  
Who could predict our paths would meet,  
And give me shelter at your lotus feet?

5\. Though you didn’t know me,  
And your real self I could not see,  
To me you still opened your heart,  
And by your affection I made a new start.

6\. Trying to enjoy this body as permanent,  
We submitted to you for treatment.  
Like an expert surgeon with a knife,  
You cut the false ego from this diseased life.

7\. It’s been many years since those first days,  
The only pain now—I’m slow to change my ways.  
But there really isn’t any other choice,  
As I strive in my intelligence to hear your voice.

8\. Every year on this day most divine,  
I consider your mercy on a soul like mine.  
How can a dog so fallen and lame  
Still have some taste for the Lord’s Holy Name?

9\. Your benediction mercy is like the rain,  
Which will neither discriminate nor refrain  
From falling on the land and sea,  
Blessing every living entity equally.

10\. Thus you always show me your kindness.  
Yet I would be steeped in spiritual blindness  
If by me to whom so much is given little is returned.  
Help me to give to others what I have learned.

*Your aspiring servants in Adelaide, Australia.*

## Agartala

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*jaya śrī-kṛṣṇa-caitanya prabhu nityānanda*  
*śrī-advaita gadādhara śrīvāsādi-gaura-bhakta-vṛnda*

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare /*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I am not qualified to glorify you properly. Still I am happy to take the opportunity of this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day to think of you and glorify you.

I feel very happy in my heart when I think how everything connected with you is in the mode of transcendental goodness. I personally experienced this two years ago when I was at the Chaitanya Chandrodaya Mandir in Māyāpur for a couple of days. At that time I was very unhappy because of many difficult problems, and I was feeling very useless due to some mistakes I had made. I was not able to collect sufficient money for paying the debt for the Agartala temple and for the new temple construction project. I prayed to your *mūrti*. Then I began chanting my rounds while walking in front of the dioramas depicting the life of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. When I saw how Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu gave some *prasādam* to a dog who had traveled with Śivānanda Sena, I was little consoled, because if Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu could care so much for a dog, I thought I certainly have some hope to be cared for also. During *ārati* in the temple, I stood near the altar, speaking with His Grace Hari Śauri dāsa. I requested him to pay a visit to our temple in Agartala. Just as the conch blew and the garments were being offered, Hari Śauri Prabhu asked me to hold the small *mūrti* of you during the daily *parikramā*. This unexpected mercy melted my heart, and tears filled my eyes as I realized how much you are loving and encouraging me. Finally, I also performed *ārati* for you and was filled with intense emotions and happiness.

Later Hari Śauri Prabhu gave the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, and he related that you often instructed the devotees, “Never abandon your devotional service, even if it is very hard, even if it is imperfect, and never become discouraged, because Śrī Kṛṣṇa is always present and ready to support you, and sometimes He is testing you.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed the perfect example of how a devotee should be determined when faced with great difficulties. Whenever I am in trouble, I think of your austerities, and then immediately I understand how small are mine compared with yours. This realization gives me a lot of courage to continue my duty.

Hari Śauri Prabhu gave a nice example of how you are constantly guiding your devotees, even after your disappearance from this planet. He spoke about the promise you gave to personally accompany to the spiritual world anyone who would go to China or Japan to distribute Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One devotee accepted that challenge and went to China. After a year of preaching, not a single new devotee had turned up. So the devotee went back to his house in America, started to drink alcohol, and got involved with many other bad things. Finally, he contracted AIDS and was living on the street. One day a devotee *mātājī* recognized him and brought him to the temple. After some time he changed. He became very eager to chant the *mahā-mantra,* and he left his material body surrounded by Vaiṣṇavas. From this episode and many others like it, we can understand that you are still living with us.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for your wonderful books. They give the extraordinary universal world of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to my mind and heart. Therefore I want to please you through distributing a minimum of one million of your *Bhagavad-gītās.* Please help me by giving me the special blessings of strong determination and book-distribution *śakti*.

And finally, a big thank you for sending me a wonderful devotee—your faithful disciple Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami—as my spiritual master. He is a very expert guide. I feel completely protected by him, and I have no doubt that he will be able to send me to the spiritual world.

Finally, I want to ask you for one special blessing: Please give me your *darśana* at least once more in a dream like the previous ones. Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your servants in Kṛṣṇa consciousness,  
*The devotees of ISKCON Agartala, India.*  
(written by Premadata dāsa)

## Amravati

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this most auspicious appearance day of Your Divine Grace, we simply bow down at your lotus feet, hoping with great humility for your causeless mercy.

It is by your mercy, and that of your dear disciple Lokanāth Swami Mahārāja, that we have opened an ISKCON center at Amravati, in India’s Vidarbha province. This is a most holy place because the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa kidnapped Rukmiṇī from this place.

We are always begging for your divine mercy and divine instruction so that we are able to serve your mission better.

All Glories to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga!

*Your grand-disciple servants at ISKCON Amravati, India.*

## Arizona

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

How does one measure success?  
The total books gone out, some would guess,  
Or is it new *bhaktas,* shining and bright,  
Or *lakṣmī* collected at the end of the night?

Beautiful temples, the mortgage paid,  
Lord Jagannāth beaming in a public parade?  
Opulent Deities and a *rāj-bhoga* feast?  
Six offerings a day at the very least?

Strict *sādhana,* chanting sixteen rounds on our beads,  
The ISKCON sandwich—is this what we need?  
Is all this pleasing you? Tell us the fact.  
Or are devotee relationships something we lack?

We’ve made some progress toward the spiritual sky,  
But not without eating quite a bit of humble pie.  
After thirty-six years we still don’t have things quite right—  
Instead of cooperation, we still tend to fight.

Gurudev, do you expect more than the external result—  
*Anartha-nivṛtti,* looking within to get rid of the fault?  
We find mercy and humility are things we lack;  
When true love is shown we are taken aback.

But that is what you did to draw us near,  
Showing so much concern—that made you so dear.  
Can we show that example to our own,  
And not act as if our hearts are of stone?

To become genuine and free from false pride,  
Taking your instructions as our life to imbibe,  
Learning to put into practice what we teach,  
Then we can begin to actually preach.

Your guidance is there when we stumble and falter,  
And Kṛṣṇa’s loving glance looking down from the altar.  
You’ll keep us encouraged right up to the end.  
You’re our father, our savior, our dearmost friend.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us in our attempt to serve you and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Our only qualification is your mercy.

*Your fallen servants in Arizona, USA.*  
written by Sandāmiṇī-devī dāsī

## Austin

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. For this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā offering from your center in Austin, Texas, I humbly submit the following song I have written, entitled “Emissary from the Kingdom of God.” I hope you like it.

You sailed on the ship *Jaladuta* in 1965,  
From Calcutta to New York City,  
To make this dead world come alive.  
Down in the heart of Manhattan’s Lower East Side  
At 26 2nd Avenue to reside.  
Drumming and singing Hare Kṛṣṇa, what a dare!  
To start a world revolution in the park called Tompkins Square.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Emissary from the Kingdom of God.  
Your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement  
Spread like blazing wildfire,  
Beaming the light of pure wisdom  
On a world trapped in hopeless mire.  
Clad in a simple saffron monk’s robe,  
Traversing each and every corner of the globe,  
Insurmountable opposition you overcame  
To deliver to us the mercy of Kṛṣṇa’s name.  
My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Emissary from the Kingdom of God.

You exposed the deluded scholars  
And the rascal scientists,  
Whose bogus propaganda  
Makes us doubt that God exists.  
With love you adeptly uprooted our confusion  
By distinguishing reality from illusion.  
You revealed to us wherefrom everything does flow:  
The Supreme Person Kṛṣṇa, whom by love we all can know.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Emissary from the Kingdom of God.

He reasons ill who tells that Prabhupāda is gone  
Or that his Hare Kṛṣṇa movement is now dead,  
While he’s living still in his books  
And in the hearts of those who remember what he did and said.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray for the mercy that I can please you unlimitedly,  
For I’ve got to try to repay the debt for the unlimited bliss you’re giving me.  
In spite of my hopeless nonsense disqualification,  
I beg to serve in your movement’s global inundation.  
Yes, the history books will all solidly declare,  
Hare Kṛṣṇa saved the world from its hour of darkest despair.  
My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Emissary from the Kingdom of God.

*Your eternal servants at the ISKCON center in Austin, Texas, USA.*  
(written by Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa)

## Baltimore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. As the child Dhruva Mahārāja, by the touch of Lord Viṣṇu’s conch, was empowered to offer suitable prayers, please bless me to write a suitable offering. May you be pleased, I purified, and the readers benefited, and even enlivened. We shoot for the white rhino!

By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our lives have been transformed with meaningful goals, endeavors, and activities beyond this temporary, superficial material life. In the material world the Lord’s powerful manifestation as time rules our lives by setting limiting parameters and reminding us of the conditions imposed on us and of the fact—a fact you often reminded us of—that time is precious and can’t be recovered even with gold. Though my practice of devotional service is embarrassingly meager (as exemplified by my sometimes harsh, disrespectful, and mean dealings with other devotees), at least I can say I have found the right path of life.

I have begun this offering three times, and have expressed different moods each time. How shall I utilize this most important time? Should I write of how fallen I am and how incapable of offering proper praise and glorification? Should I find proper quotes glorifying you as the ideal pure devotee, or proving how important the guru is to the life of the disciple? Should I cry to you in disappointment at the in-fighting of my Godbrothers and Godsisters? Decisions, decisions!! I will express a bit of all those moods, perhaps, yet most importantly let me be personal, honest, and relevant.

So many emotions flow over me as I think of my years of being a devotee and how my relationship with you has changed and deepened. How different it is to be a nineteen-year-old neophyte with your physical presence, and to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness now, as a fifty-something, slightly advanced neophyte bereft of your physical presence yet forced to see you in your instructions and disciples and sometimes within. As death approaches, I often think, What is there really of value in this world?

Certainly we should always reflect on our relationship with you, on who you are to us, and on how we are following or failing to follow your instructions. Yet certain days like this, or other yearly festival days, are like signposts that specifically beckon us, on our sojourn of life, to slow down and stop, take a few breaths, and take an especially hard look at our lives, our spiritual progress, and what we can do to improve. It is so easy to get caught up in the current of life and forget where we should really be heading, and what is truly important. If we are not careful we can be very busy externally (doing all the “right” things) yet lack the proper mentality and spiritual motivation. Somehow we can forget the magic, focus, and intensity of our first days in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and just get caught up in the routine, even labeling it “same old, same old,” just as the nondevotees talk about their work. So on this day, dear Prabhupāda, let us remember the higher taste of Kṛṣṇa consciousness we have experienced, taking shelter of you and our dear “prabhus” to really determine what is favorable for our advancement over the “long haul” of our life.

I remember how, as I grew up, my miserable, unfulfilled life was the perfect background to lead me to you and devotional service. Kṛṣṇa is the perfect mystical arranger (*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya*), and you are the perfect fulfiller of His will! When I became a devotee I was “out of my mind,” which means I was out of my conditioned mind, which made taking up devotional service not at all radical but the most natural thing. How miraculous and fabulously fortunate for me—and for all of us who have taken to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which is the true medicine for our suffering condition.

How even more miraculous to have lasted over years and years of tests, ups and downs, discouragement and cynicism, and to still be positive about Kṛṣṇa consciousness in spite of all the horrendous things that have happened, and all those very difficult and even embarrassing dealings between devotees! It is a challenge to us all to see beyond externals, abuses, and falldowns in order to see eternal Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to believe it is real, possible for us to attain, and good for everyone.

We must always look long-term (you told us, dear Prabhupāda, to “Give this one life to Kṛṣṇa”), and see Kṛṣṇa’s hand in everything and see everything as meant for our ultimate welfare. Thank God (Kṛṣṇa) for all my mature, thoughtful, loving devotee friends who help me weather all the storms and difficulties in life by their good council and association. They give me hope about what devotees and our communities can and should be.

How can we live without the shelter of the devotees, who are your mercy to us, Prabhupāda, and who help us be Kṛṣṇa conscious and live in your physical absence? Truly, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us everything, even food (*prasādam*), spouses (or *daṇḍas*), friends, practical advice, work, worship, the kingdom of God, and love. What more could we ask for (other than the blessings to obtain Kṛṣṇa)?

In spite of our lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, can we not see that because we’ve met you and become your followers, our lives will never be the same? Though we may have other desires, can we not appreciate that we have not only learned what our true desires, goals, and aspirations should be but have had them fulfilled as well—at least to some extent? That is progress! Though we may not always associate spiritually with other devotees, do we not have so much in common and appreciate when we do share Kṛṣṇa consciousness?

Don’t we really aspire, or at least aspire to aspire, to be happy by Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to be Kṛṣṇa conscious, and to return to Kṛṣṇa? For most of us not born into Kṛṣṇa consciousness, isn’t that really an amazing turn of our lives when we look back at our upbringing? Who would have thought, growing up, that we would have become devotees—however neophyte we may presently consider ourselves? Or for those born in devotee families who experienced less than ideal upbringings but who have remained devotees, or who have given up and again taken up Kṛṣṇa consciousness, is that also not a great wonder and Kṛṣṇa’s mercy?

Although we need to take a cold hard look at our lives to discover our *anarthas* and faulty thinking patterns so we can improve, we also must rejoice at our good fortune in coming in contact with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, either personally or through your teachings and disciples. Śrīla Prabhupāda, though you have made our good fortune, we must be able to acknowledge the different ways Kṛṣṇa’s mercy comes into our lives, and we must accept that mercy and not disempower ourselves due to material negativity or false humility. We must see the blessings in all circumstances and believe in the power of possibilities instead of the power of our problems or unexpected reverses.

This is especially true during this time of fratricidal fighting among different camps of devotees, court battles, the unstable world situation, challenges to our faith, or, for your grand-disciples, the untimely loss of their guru. Now and forever we must know where our shelter is! The material world is perishable and constantly changing. The one constant is our relationship with the Supreme Lord and His representatives.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us find our spiritual center in you and Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, so we can weather any storm. Let us internalize the teachings and realize their meaning practically, so we can face the challenges of life in a way that enhances our Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In short, dear master, please bless us to make rapid spiritual advancement, to be pure devotees decorated with all twenty-six qualities of a pure devotee, and to be the Lord’s instrument for benefiting the world through preaching to others, reaching out to them and teaching them to become devotees. And may you be pleased with our lives so we may by all means return back home, back to Godhead.

Thank you for giving us the greatest treasure, Kṛṣṇa consciousness! By your mercy may we become expert miners extracting all the nectar given by Lord Caitanya so the world may be overflooded with love of God, *kṛṣṇa-prema*!

*Yours servants in ISKCON Baltimore, Maryland, USA.*  
(written by Karṇāmṛta dāsa)

## Belo Horizonte

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Dear spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please pour over our heads your blessings, which even demigods are anxious to obtain. Your Divine Grace has manifested all the qualities of a pure devotee of the Lord. It is possible to find many great souls among yogis and *jñānīs,* but it’s very rare to find one truly great soul, a pure devotee of the Lord, one fully surrendered to the Lord. A great devotee has no other desire than that of serving Kṛṣṇa as His personal servant, friend, father, mother, or lover. Since you are such a great devotee, you are a bona fide representative of the Supreme Lord.

We owe a huge debt to Your Divine Grace, and the only way we can begin to pay it is by distributing your books, which are always full of nectar. We pray that we may always take part in this mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

We are eternally thankful and ask to remain always your servants, life after life

*Your aspiring servants in the ISKCON temple in Belo Horizonte, Brazil.*  
(written by Advaita Caraṇa dāsa and Mukunda Mādhava dāsa)

## Bhakti-latā Purī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

Thank you for being the captain at the helm of this ship of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. How fortunate we are, the crew of this ship, ISKCON!!

Much sincerity and surrender are required to manifest the link between humanity and God. You showed both to the highest degree. You did not get bewildered by the power Kṛṣṇa gave you, nor did you become intoxicated by having at your disposal such a wide variety of resources. You taught us how to become more attached to Kṛṣṇa through both failure (easier) and success (usually harder). We feel perfectly safe in the good ship ISKCON with you as the captain, and we know that the constant challenges you place before us are for our ultimate purification.

You are perfectly alive; otherwise, what we do and say in front of your picture would become an empty ritual. Is it just a healthy habit with no spiritual substance, like walking barefoot in the morning dew? No, we are sure you hear our prayers and accept our sincere offerings.

I still hope—and you know that in my case hope hurts—to serve you some day, or at least not to offer any hindrance to your mission.

Thank you very much for everything.

*Your eternally indebted servants at Bhakti-latā Purī, the ISKCON farm community in Argentina.*  
(written by Daityeśa dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your beautiful lotus feet. All glories to you, our beloved spiritual father and grandfather.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we at Bhaktivedanta Manor have one thing to say to you: Thank you.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for taking the trouble to come to the West and preach. You were living the ideal life for a *sādhu*, tucked away in your two rooms at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, one of the holiest places on the planet.

The six Gosvāmīs themselves would meet for discussion at the Rādhā-Dāmodara *mandira*. And there you were perfectly placed. As you glanced out of your kitchen window, your eyes would fall on Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *samādhi*. What better sight can meet the eyes? Walking out of your door, you were faced with the beautiful altar of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara and all the other Deities that grace their Temple. What better association can one get? And all around you, Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma*, Kṛṣṇa’s chosen place of pastimes. What more could any devotee wish for? You had everything—the perfection of life.

But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were a devotee with a mission. Instructed in 1922 by your beloved Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, to preach in English, you spent the interim years preparing yourself.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for taking your spiritual master’s instructions so seriously and making them the focus of your life.

May we all follow in your footsteps and abandon an easy life for one of preaching.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for empowering three householder couples—or, to be more accurate, three no-householding couples—to leave everything behind and travel to a foreign country, with no money, to start the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement in the UK.

Only you could inspire such commitment and surrender in the American youth. Because you told them they could do it, they went and did it.

May we also empower one another to do great things for your movement, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for introducing the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* to the streets of the UK. By hearing this chanting, George Harrison himself, one of the most famous people on the planet, became inspired. He produced a record of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* on the Beatles Apple label. It reached number two on the British hit parade and was a worldwide hit. The devotees were even on television pop shows chanting. Thus the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa was heard everywhere.

George Harrison continued to be inspired and gave us Bhaktivedanta Manor.

May we continue this *harināma saṅkīrtana* in the UK streets and inspire more people to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for installing Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda at Bhaktivedanta Manor. In 1973 you came whizzing in in a helicopter that landed right on the front lawn. Apparently helicopters were not your preferred mode of transport, but we tried to please you. And you certainly pleased us on Janmāṣṭamī when, after installing Their Lordships Rādhā-Gokulānanda, you invited all of us right into the Deity room for a big *kīrtana*.
