Rādhā-Gokulānanda were the Delhi Pandal Deities and were meant to go to Scotland. You gave them to us. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for always pleasing us.

May we continue in our attempts to please you (even if we sometimes fail).

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us Ratha-yātrā. You really taught us how to party. In 1973 you danced all the way from Marble Arch to Trafalgar Square! In fact you danced so much that the police were frightened you’d start a riot and wanted you to sit down and be quiet. You didn’t comply. You really taught us how to have fun chanting and dancing.

May we continue to have fun in the transcendental way you showed us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for that journey you made in 1977. We have not forgotten that we were the last temple you visited before you left for your eternal abode. We tried to make you comfortable, knowing that to leave Vṛndāvana to visit the West was a very difficult journey for you. We know that you left Vṛndāvana at midnight lying on a couple of mattresses in the back of your Ambassador car. It must have been a terrible journey on those bumpy Indian roads. But you did it for us, and that we will always appreciate. We have your palanquin here in your rooms to daily remind us of the lengths you went to for preaching. Even though you were too weak to even walk, you were strong enough to preach.

May we always remember your determination to preach and follow in your footsteps.

We know that here at Bhaktivedanta Manor we have a relatively easy life. We live in an opulent country with no material shortages. Religious diversity has become a part of British life. There is no real stigma to being a Hare Kṛṣṇa devotee. It’s safe to walk in the streets wearing devotee clothes and *tilaka*. On the Saturday night *harināma,* people of all ages join in as we pass. We once saw how the fire brigade, in their fire engine, sirens blaring, all raised their arms in the air as they rushed passed us. Once, down in the London underground, our arrival was announced over the tanoy system, informing passengers that the evening entertainment would be provided by the Hare Kṛṣṇas.

Police treat us as part of the scenery. In fact, this year they escorted as we marched down London’s prestigious Oxford Street on Gaura Pūrṇimā pulling the Deities of Nitāi-Gaurasundara on a swan chariot. Quite a change from the old days when we risked arrest for obstruction.

Life at Bhaktivedanta Manor has evolved considerably since your last visit here twenty-five years ago. What used to be a large and spacious Tudor Manor House that easily housed all the devotees and their projects has turned into a rather small and cramped mock-Tudor Manor House. Obviously, the building is still the same, so it really illustrates just how much we have expanded. Now devotees live scattered across the local area, and it seems like a million programs all clamor for space.

We pray that our expansion program has pleased you. For we know that we owe everything to you. Your success has been our success. Without your preaching, we dread to think where we would be today. It is a very humbling thought when one considers the debt we all owe you.

Armed with this knowledge, may we attempt the impossible task of repaying you.

Finally, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have one last but certainly not least thank you. Thank you for giving us Tribhuvanātha Prabhu. It would seem he was practically everyone’s favorite devotee. His losing his battle with cancer was a very sobering experience for all of us. He may have lost his battle with cancer, but he certainly didn’t lose the battle with Māyā. If anyone took your preaching mood to heart, it was Tribhuvanātha. He taught us all by his example how a devotee gives the holy name at every opportunity. Even his funeral turned into a wonderful bittersweet preaching event that brought the whole *yātrā* together.

Certainly it could be no coincidence that Śrīmān George Harrison left his body the day we put Tribhuvanātha’s ashes in the Yamunā. We could speculate wildly and guess that you’ve taken two of your favorite preachers for a new adventure. A new planet, a different time, and who knows, may be even a new Manor House! Whatever the reality, we devotees at the Manor know that we owe both of these great souls a debt for the service they performed not only for you but for our Bhaktivedanta Manor.

Our final prayer is that we can make a difference to someone’s life and bring them closer to Kṛṣṇa, whether it be through Tribhuvanātha’s festival program or any of the many other preaching programs that have their home at Bhaktivedanta Manor.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor, UK.*

## Budapest

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to you!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Once upon a time there was a transcendental architect. He was not an ordinary human being, because God Himself sent him to fulfill an important mission. He was interested exclusively in construction from his childhood. When the time was ripe, he wanted to study, but not according to his own ideas. He sought out a master, whom he soon found. He quickly attracted his master’s attention with his humble and attentive mood, and because he proved to be an excellent servant, he was able to receive the knowledge in perfection. He learned to stick the strong bricks of the principles with the mortar of love in order to make his helpers in the construction work dedicated to their profession, and to lay the foundation always on a solid basis.

After receiving the final instructions from his master, he started the serious work. Not sparing energy, laboring for the welfare of all, he conceived the plan for a big house where everyone could live and find a real home. A lot of time passed, because every detail of the plan was decided upon only after thorough study and deliberation. As he answered the appealing words of his love for God, his doubts were often cleared by the Supreme Lord Himself. After completing the plans, the transcendental architect began his work. He looked for firm ground, as he was taught. He persevered, but his attempts remained futile. His colleagues tried to deter him from his plan because they thought it beyond human capacity. But he remained unshaken. He said everything was already there; it was only time that separated him from it.

He was always thinking of his master with love, and so he performed his duty without any distraction or personal motivation, and he never shirked the tasks before him.

One day he traveled to a far-off place according to his master’s desire. Hoping he would find a better opportunity to succeed, he left everything behind. Although the other masters called his act madness, he did not give up. He arrived in an alien world alone, far from home. His unlimited simplicity, determination, devotion, and knowledge made him very attractive. He was willing to explain his plan to everyone, and to accept any help from anyone. Soon his austerities resulted in success, and he started realizing his plan.

The house is still being built and enlarged. The master himself is no longer present, but he has passed all the instructions to his qualified disciples. He found the solid basis. He laid the bricks of the principles and stuck them together with the mortar of love and devotion, and his helpers became the dedicated followers of his profession. He had fulfilled the desire of his master and the Lord.

The tale goes on and breaks up into many story lines. Because his good students continue the construction work, with the determination and faith in God learned from their master, they also conceive wonderful plans.

One of them, proving his ability and faith in his master, started a work in a place where it was doubtful that he could find a solid basis for the foundation. He had only a handful of helpers, but the power received from him and the help of others empowered them to do great activities. This person repeats two principles: that we should be determined, and we should never forget to pray to his master if we want to proceed with the construction. He planned the Palace of the Soul, from which only time separates us.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, all glories to your transcendental plan, to the house where everyone will find a real home. Please, on this wonderful day, bless us so that we can construct the Palace of the Soul!

*Your insignificant servants at the ISKCON temple in Budapest, Hungary.*

## Buenos Aires

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.**

Worship of the spiritual master on the day of his appearance in this world is nothing new. It’s been going on since time immemorial. It’s offered to that person who is able to present perfectly Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s point of view. He who is able to do this is allowed to sit on the *vyāsāsana*, Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s sitting place. He is guru. One meaning of *guru* is “heavy.” He doesn’t go even one inch away from what he heard from the *sādhus,* his predecessors, and from the *śāstra,* the scriptures. Besides this, he has to possess impeccable behavior.

Such transcendental qualities are found in our beloved spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇu-pāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedānta Svāmī Mahārāja Śrīla Prabhupāda. He presented the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in the same way that the past *ācāryas* of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *sampradāya* did.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day we pray to devote ourselves to please Śrīla Prabhupāda, to spread his mission, to help others understand the compassion, humility, and determination of this great soul who walked among us. We pray to be faithful to him and to be empowered to follow all his teachings and instructions. We pray to keep the desire for getting all the blessings that His Divine Grace is pouring on us. As members of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s family, we pray to help one another keep his legacy alive. We pray that every one of us and the whole world will surrender unto his lotus feet.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants in Buenos Aires, Argentina.*

## Cebu

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Thank you for all the wonderful gifts and blessings you have given us.

You have given us Hare Kṛṣṇa Paradise, where we can live while practicing devotional service.

You have given us Śrī Śrī Dayāl Nitāi Gaura Hari, the most blissful forms of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda, whom we can worship and serve with unconditional love and devotion.

You have given us Tamāl Krishna Goswami and Giridhārī Swami to be our spiritual masters, *dīkṣā* and *śikṣā*. They will patiently guide us until we perfect our present existence and develop love for Kṛṣṇa.

You have given us the holy names, which we regularly chant during *japa* and sing loudly in varied transcendental melodies during ecstatic *kīrtana*. The holy names, the means to achieve *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

You have given us *prasādam,* which not only satisfies our palates but also sanctifies us, body and soul.

You have given us your books, which will be the law books of mankind for the next ten thousand years. Your commentated translations of the *Bhagavad-gītā*, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and related Vedic literatures have given us pure and unadulterated transcendental knowledge. This knowledge not only immediately destroys all *anarthas* within our hearts and defeats Māyā when she attacks our minds in gross and subtle ways, but also allows us to get nearer to the Supreme Lord by reading His pastimes.

You have given us a family of devotees who are there with us through thick and thin.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what more could we ask for? You have given us everything we need to go back home, back to Godhead. Now it is up to us to embrace these treasures and take advantage of all of them to cultivate our Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is only one request we would like to make. Kindly situate us at the dust of your divine lotus feet and let us remain there eternally.

Happy Appearance Day. Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!!

*Your humble servants at Hare Krishna Paradise, Cebu, Philippines.*

## Christchurch

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I will do my best to report in from this small island outpost. Please tolerate the crackling static of this attempt to get through. Like a broken code message, a primitive inarticulate effort of Morse code carrying just the essence of information. Just to keep you up with how we are doing in the war against nescience. As if you don’t already know. You sit as “The General” on your *vyāsāsana* in the temple’s inner sanctum, our commander in chief, always at the campaign table. We are all your boys and girls. Some are on the front lines, waging face-to-face combat with books and *harināma,* others are on the big guns—Deity worship, Food for Life, and festival planning. Most of us are fighting in the trenches of *gṛhastha* life. In this way we are slowly but surely moving forward.

It is now early morning, the best time to communicate—all lines are open, a time to be honest. I feel it is a good time to go deeper and open up. Strictly speaking, I should just keep this broadcast formal and official, but there is this pushing to reveal this confidential information. To tell you of the real glorious struggle, the inner conflict. The boiling down of the milk. The most wonderful growth. The rest of the troops don’t know I’m on the wireless. I am alone with you. It is safe to make heartfelt confessions to you, our loving father.

Those of us who have traveled the full journey in this small South Pacific *yātrā* have seen the beginnings and built up to the appearance of Nitāi-Gauracandra, Their glorious installation. The original small embracing close family of loving devotees depended on the absolute mercy of your sober *mūrti* form. Yes, we experienced fantastic years of pure growth. We purchased land, constructed temple facilities, had a small but beautiful *gurukula,* and farmed the land. Thousands of plates of *prasādam* were freely distributed in the parks. *Harināma* manifested its full-blown exuberance, spilling over the city walls and carrying us to every town and village in New Zealand. The Padayātrā years, the glorious countrywide festivals. Books were distributed in their tens of thousands. We visited schools, taught cooking at the universities. We didn’t fight so much—we were too busy. Lakṣmī resided in every corner of your 83 Bealey Avenue household. The Sunday Feasts consisted of great opulence—not only the succulent *prasādam* but also the home-grown plays, exhibitions of spontaneous creativity, and outbursts of raucous laughter that captivated the senses of the conditioned souls. The freedom of slapstick humor, performing artists living on the edge, the avant garde of the 80’s, the Ratha-yātrās of the 90’s—whatever happened in America manifested in New Zealand twenty years later.

Your preaching extravaganza soared to a zenith. Some of your direct sons—*sannyāsīs* who came here—even said we were the best. We were proud of our spiritual reputation. You never personally visited here, so we just missed your manifested pastimes. But we will always remember and cherish those vital early years, when your movement took root in this part of the world. That time was like a big ship that came through, a huge ocean liner. We didn’t see it, it had just past. But just behind it was this powerful wake, evidence of an awesome moving vessel. The boiling turbulence of the powerful prop churned up the whole ocean.

And just as with the original ocean-churning by the demons and the demigods, many wonderful things came out. Our little *yātrā* was caught up in the momentum. It has taken twenty years to settle down to the realization that you had passed through and were now gone. The spinning eddies of that powerful movement have dispersed again into the main body of water. We could almost smell the mustard oil on your freshly massaged body, the ink was still wet from your signing off “Your ever well-wisher, A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami.” Now the reality was that we would know you only in your *vāṇī* form. It is described that service in separation is more intimate, pleasing, and intense. As Kṛṣṇa departed from this planet accompanied by knowledge, religion, etc., yet arranged to leave behind the *Bhāgavata Purāṇa* for guidance, you departed from here but left your translations of the *Bhāgavatam* and other books, which are now arising like the sun to dissipate the darkness of ignorance.

Here we have seen your movement do nothing but grow from strength to strength. It has never ceased, the churning has just begun. Yes, we are experiencing some temporary setbacks, the growing pains of ISKCON that are being felt worldwide. On the surface there has definitely arisen an oil slick of *kālakūṭa* poison that may appear able to destroy everything. The powerful determination and purity of your original disciples have grasped material energy by the neck, and those disciples are pulling backwards and forwards with the demons, their actions motivated by your personal association. One senior disciple of yours once mentioned how, along with the birth of Lord Caitanya, there came a dark force that had the potency to ruin everything. That statement was bewildering for some time, but as the years past, seemingly insurmountable difficulties arose and we began to witness the awesome presence of the material energy. It countered our attacks with weapons that could bring this movement to its knees: the breakdown of the family, divorce among the *gṛhasthas,* the exploitation of children, our failure to establish *gurukula* and a rural agrarian foundation, untrained leadership, dysfunctional management, the guru issues—there have been fighting in the ranks, desertion and deception. These adversities, caustic in their essence, have eaten away our consolidation, eroded our commitment, and hardened the heart with impersonalism.

Understanding that not a blade of grass moves without the instigation of Kṛṣṇa, we are forced to address these matters with philosophical introspection. Six children appeared in Devakī’s womb before Kṛṣṇa’s birth. They were the personifications of lust, anger, greed, illusion, madness, and envy. They were all killed by Kaṁsa, and then Baladeva came to cleanse the womb before Kṛṣṇa’s appearance. Our formidable problems have forced us to fortify ourselves and address our weaknesses; they have acted like the effects of malefic harsh planets such as Saturn, Mars, and Rāhu. They have hacked away the negative debris. The sculptor spends his time ridding his work of unwanted material so that only the essence of beauty is left standing. It is the smoldering tip of red hot iron that hollows out the center of a chosen piece of wood. Without this there would be no music from the lute.

Cannot disease be a healing forerunner to perfect health? What may seem like a sharp knife in a murderer’s hands may in fact be the skillfully wielded scalpel in the hands of a surgeon.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you once said, “Don’t be surprised at who leaves, be surprised at who stays.” We should have some confidence that among those who have not deserted or stepped back, there is empowered strength. Their unity and will to overcome hold the *śakti* of Nīlakaṇṭha (Lord Śiva), who swallowed up all the poison *halāhala* and who withstood the crushing fall of Gaṅgā to Earth. Like him (*vaiṣṇavānāṁ yathā śambhuḥ*), those remaining faithful soldiers are your topmost devotees. Undoubtedly they will survive and conquer, to see all the subsequent auspicious and opulent manifestations of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy float to the surface.

We will see the cow Surabhi, the horse Uccaiḥśravā, the elephant Airāvata, the Kaustubha gem, the Pārijāta flower, and the Apsarās. Lakṣmī, the goddess of fortune, will find Viṣṇu, her husband, and Vāruṇī, the goddess of beverages, will provide nectar for the renewal of energy. The churning of more preaching will go on, and Dhanvantari will manifest, carrying a jug of healing nectar. The demons will want some of that also. There will always be poisonous snakes and spiders, but we must always remember: the dogs will bark, but the caravan will pass. Finally, just as the *asuras* are about to snatch the ambrosia, the beautiful female incarnation of Kṛṣṇa, Mohinī Mūrti, will bewilder their lusty intelligence and preserve the nectar, finally placing it safely in the hands of the devotees.

Yes, we have experienced a temporary lull in external growth. The sap has been conserved inside winter’s wood. The promise of spring shown by the bulging bud will be fulfilled without fail, manifesting exuberant growth from fallowed soil. The ugly grub stifled in its cocoon will soon glide free as a butterfly. With growth, the lizard casts off its dead skin. So step aside, old timers, there is a rising in the youth. Give your wisdom but don’t smother their freshness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, the time is ripe to allow the second half of your plan to emerge and evolve into the most wonderful renaissance. The golden egg sits in the scraggly nest of Kali-yuga’s sharp sticks.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy has continued to come to us. The neem, although bitter to the tongue, has a purifying effect on the blood. Your wonderful ISKCON movement is dynamic, not static but ecstatic. It is so exciting to stay with you, to be a small part of how you are unfolding the lotus of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s heart in this contaminated pond of the Western world. To be part of the special forces of such a valiant commander in chief, one must have the mettle of a transcendentalist. To stay with you all the way tests one’s strength of heart. We pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that we may always attentively listen submissively to your orders on how to proceed from here. Any small problems we may have are just like mosquitoes compared with the Garuḍa-like protection your ISKCON has provided.

As you promised, there are still massive shipments of *prasādam* arriving. The more we give out, the more there is. We are still receiving your letters in the form of your unlimited blessings.

*Your rough but loyal team operating down under, in Christchurch, New Zealand.*  
(written by Yaśodā-dulāl dāsa)

## Columbus

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you and your auspicious mission of spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement!

We here in Columbus cannot adequately express our gratitude for the profound inspirational instructions and personal example you have left for us. Despite the disciple’s inability to repay the spiritual master, you have instructed us that our life’s mission should be centered on such a goal. In an attempt to better serve your movement, we have developed a mission statement for the ISKCON Columbus Yatra:

It is the desire of the Columbus Kṛṣṇa House and associates to increase the propagation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and reflect in our activities the teachings of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. In doing so we want to maintain a well-organized spiritual atmosphere, where all feel welcomed, wanted, and part of a supportive devotional environment. We thus hope to achieve a balanced Kṛṣṇa conscious community.

We pray that we may utilize this mission statement to serve your mission in a more focused and deliberate manner.

*Your servants at the ISKCON center in Columbus, Ohio, USA.*

## Cote D’ivoire

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Kindly accept our prostrated respectful obeisances. All glories to you!

You revealed Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world, even to the most fallen people on this dark continent. We see how people are finding meaning in their lives, their cultures, and traditions by coming in contact with your books and your devotees.

To people who have lost their sense of identity on a continent where traditional values and spirituality are fast submerging in the ocean of materialism, where everything good in them has to be either discarded or, to be acceptable, dressed up in Western garb, suddenly your emissaries started appearing with their *dhotī* uniforms and their shaven heads with the sacred little tuft in the back and the white marks on the forehead. They looked vibrant, confident, and happy. They danced and danced. In their wild ecstatic dance they further demonstrated an atavistic principle. Indeed, there is a recurrence or renaissance taking place here due to the activities of your devotees.

The loose robes, the shaved heads dotted with tuft and divine marks—these are all-too-familiar signs of the African-Vedic culture. The people were attracted to the devotees, and their eloquence and sagacity astounded them. At last the people’s repressed consciousness, their muted beliefs in reincarnation, ancestral rites, respect for *devas,* and chastity, has finally found a means of expression.

Suddenly the Hare Kṛṣṇas are everywhere, in everyone’s consciousness. When their leader, your empowered son Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, appeared on television, all other philosophies became silent, and all glowworm-like evangelists parading as stars faded away in the brilliance of transcendental knowledge coming down through the perfect medium. The message is clear, a revolution is under way.

All the preaching at the schools and colleges, book distribution on the streets and offices, personal interaction with the devotees—it all leaves lasting impressions. The established order has begun to feel threatened. Their foreign masters, who profit from their ignorance, are jittery. They will not capitulate without a fight. All that glitters and shines in their wealthy kingdoms is built from the resources of this dark continent.

Too much light is now penetrating the land in the form of new awareness. True freedom is beginning to emerge. Self-realization, they discover, is not just a spiritual awareness; it can equally mean a total emancipation in all spheres of life.

The development of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Africa has become a dangerous signal to the powers that be. They will not lose their stronghold without a fight. What is their strategy? They will try by all means to stop the spread of this knowledge by intercepting our strongest weapons: the Kṛṣṇa conscious literatures. Many shipments of books to the region never make it to their destination. Preachers are being lured with promises of wealth and secure jobs. Churches are being funded as never before. While the battle rages on, the generals who are responsible for the publication and mass distribution of your books in the West are being targeted one by one.

Yes, there have been many casualties; we have even lost several generals. And yes, we have lost some of our early gains. Our bases are, however, still intact, though the frontline men are getting fewer. The enemy’s gains are of course temporary, as their advances are checked by the power of the holy name, the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. The holy name is a powerful refuge, a strong fort within which the righteous find shelter.

Our fervent prayer is that you please strengthen your generals with your spiritual potency so that they can keep the fort protected from Māyā’s onslaught. The battle is intense, and the enemy is formidable. We need your divine intervention to once again take the battle to the enemy’s camp, shatter their ranks, and bring their masters at your feet.

Under the guidance of our able generals, Bhakti-tīrtha Swami and Kavicandra Swami, the spiritual warriors here in Cote D’ivoire are fine-tuning our weapons, strategizing, and riveting our attention on our goals. Our foundation is built on the values you have given us:

**Preaching Is the Essence:**No activity is worthy unless it helps us spread the message to more and more people. Any activity or service that diminishes our ability to continue preaching vigorously is not worth it.

**Books Are the Basis:** Our preaching will be based only on your words and whatever *śāstras* support them. Your books will be our guide and companion at all times.

**Utility Is the Principle:**We will not waste time waiting for some facility to manifest but will do the needful. We shall follow in your footsteps by using what is available, and thus by contact with the gem of the spiritual process, all means of success will be given to us.

**Purity Is the Force:** We shall rely solely on our *sādhana,* your mercy, and the mercy of your representatives as our source of inspiration. Whether we feel weak or strong, we will always look to our *sādhana;* to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda; and to Kṛṣṇa for help.

*Your saṅkīrtana army and spiritual warriors in Cote D’ivoire, Africa.*  
(written by Varāha dāsa)

## Coventry

Dearest Grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on your Vyāsa-pūjā day!

Where do we begin to glorify a personality such as you?

Just now, a large number of devotees from this temple are going to the holy land of Bharat on pilgrimage. One of them commented: “Where would we be without Śrīla Prabhupāda? Because of him, there is an eagerness to go on a *yātrā;* because of him, there is excitement to go on *yātrā;* because of him, there is depth of understanding and appreciation of going on a *yātrā*. Without him, a *yātrā* would be fairly meaningless.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for revealing the spiritual beauty of the holy land of Bharat to the whole world.

Recently at an annual general meeting at our temple we noticed that there were people of many races there—English, Punjabi, Gujarati, Hungarian, Italian, Caribbean; that there were people of different colors there—white, black, brown; that there were people of different religions there—Christian, Hindu. It suddenly dawned on us that nowhere else could there be such a diverse group of people gathered together to commonly glorify Their Lordships. Nowhere else. And it is even more remarkable that this is going on in every country in the world, all because of you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for building a house in which the whole world can live.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books are spiritual living forces. Over the years we have come to realize that, although your books don’t change, for the sincere reader the depth of the realization of what’s being said is ever-increasing. Time and time again, when faced with difficult situations, a particular passage from a particular book will suddenly spring out, without warning, and give an answer or understanding to the problem. And what’s more, the sublime writings of previous *ācāryas,* especially His Divine Grace Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, heighten these feelings of appreciation even more.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for opening the treasure house of spiritual wisdom to the whole world, without which we would all be blind.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for everything.

*Your ever-grateful grandchildren and great-grandchildren at the ISKCON center in Coventry, UK.*

## Curitiba

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Today on this most auspicious occasion we pay our obeisances at your divine lotus feet and ask you to kindly accept our *puṣpāñjali*. We understand that the best way to satisfy you is through persevering in the struggle to introduce Kṛṣṇa consciousness to all the suffering living beings, without discrimination. In this way, by preaching about Kṛṣṇa and transmitting His instructions, we will purify our own hearts, and we will also have the great fortune of participating in Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

We ask you to please bless us with success in our efforts, and please, beloved spiritual grandfather, give us the necessary strength to accomplish your mission in these dark times we are living in.

*From your servants in ISKCON Curitiba, Brazil.*

## Czech Republic

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

This year I, Maṇidhara dāsa, was asked to write an offering to you on behalf of the devotees in the Czech *yātrā*. I thought that more appropriate than me writing an offering for the whole *yātrā,* I would solicit preaching reports from various devotees around the *yātrā*. I remember how much you liked to hear the *saṅkīrtana* results and the preaching reports of your disciples. Surely you will enjoy the same reports from your grand-disciples. What these reports reflect consists only of glimpses of your unlimited preaching potency. Every day these devotees witness how your books, your preaching, affects the lives of thousands of conditioned souls in this country and elsewhere. As they try to preserve and protect the transcendental inheritance they have received from you, they would like to bow down to you on this most auspicious day with a humble prayer that their offerings may be accepted by Your Divine Grace.

\* \* \*

First is an offering from the Prague temple, written by Narākṛti dāsa, the temple president:

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We, the members of the Prague temple, would like to offer you for your pleasure the results of the devotional service we have performed over the last year. Although it is just a fragment of what you expect from us, if you are pleased with that endeavor, we can consider our lives successful. In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.9.23your write:

A Vaiṣṇava is especially favored by the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Pleasing him or displeasing him directly affects the pleasure and displeasure of the Supreme Lord. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, in his eight stanzas of prayer to the spiritual master, has sung, *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādaḥ:* by pleasing the spiritual master, who is a pure Vaiṣṇava, one pleases the Personality of Godhead, but if one displeases the spiritual master one does not know where he is going.

While trying to fulfill your desires, we are daily confronted with the challenges and serious obstacles put before us by the material energy. The most serious are our uncontrolled minds and senses, which are always trying to again drive us into the lower modes of nature. What is amazing is that, despite our uncontrolled senses, we are still wanting to be known as advanced Vaiṣṇavas.

Fortunately, there is no way around your instructions, which are clear, pure, transparent, ever fresh, and always relevant. These instructions are as good today as they were while you were physically present on this planet. They are eternal and filled with spiritual potency. By being absorbed in them and practically following them, one experiences unlimited spiritual satisfaction. Although we may not be able to fulfill your every desire immediately, if we simply agree that following your every word is the best path and have faith in your guidance, Kṛṣṇa will give us His mercy and help us advance. However, if out of total madness we think we know better than you and consider your teachings to be influenced by some mundane ideas, we are doomed. In *A Second Chance* you write,

Thinking the spiritual master an ordinary person and envying him are causes of a devotee’s falling down. Devotional service requires training under the guidance of a spiritual master, and this guidance is received when one surrenders to the spiritual master, inquires from him, and renders service to him. But these are impossible for one who envies the spiritual master.

Therefore we wish to take full shelter at your lotus feet by taking your every word and your every desire on our head.

From the very beginning the basis of your Prague temple was the distribution of your transcendental literatures. Over the last ten years your dedicated followers in Prague have distributed more than one million pieces of literature in the Czech Republic. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us so we can increase this most important preaching activity. Your books are being continuously translated into Czech by the team of your BBT servants.

The Lords of the Prague temple, Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Navadvīpacandra, are taken care of and worshiped regularly by your team of steady *pūjārīs,* cooks, decorators, tailors, and gardeners. Every day the whole temple and the *āśramas* are cleansed and the Deities nicely decorated, as you desired.

Almost every week the preaching department performs public programs, with *bhajans,* lectures, devotional dance, *prasādam* distribution, and finally an ecstatic *kīrtana* with the audience.

Another preaching department—communications—is focuses on preaching to politicians, the media, academics, and students, thus trying to fulfill your desire to address and convince the intellectual class of men about Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Every new devotee in the temple is trained in our three-month *bhakta* and *bhaktin* program, followed by further training. So far three devotees are fully dedicated to training and educating new students.

Men are trained to become first-class *brahmacārī* devotees of the Lord and thus eventually first-class *gṛhasthas* and *sannyāsīs,* while women are to become chaste and faithful wives and active devotees in all devotional fields in the temple and in the preaching field.

During the spring, summer, and autumn your *harināma* party goes out every day, chanting in downtown Prague, where thousands of foreigners and Czechs hear the holy name and appreciate it.

Under the guidance of your dear servant and our GBC representative Bhaktivaibhava Swami, the multimedia studio is working day and night producing magazines, videos, multimedia presentations, and brochures about Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Kṛṣṇa consciousness is taking solid hold in the Czech Republic, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, continue to change the hearts of the conditioned souls.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for giving us the best movement, the best philosophy, the best food, and the best and most inspiring association, your surrendered and dear disciples.

\* \* \*

On behalf of the Czech farm project, known as Krsnuv Dvur (with Varṇāśrama Prabhu as its temple president), and its associated Govinda’s restaurant in Prague, Dharma-rūpa Prabhu writes:

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

This is the first time ever—and I am very thankful for the opportunity—that I was approached by devotees from part of our Czech *yātrā* to write few words glorifying you. I will try to express at least a few drops of the gratitude we all feel toward you for the chance you have given us to free ourselves from this labyrinth of matter, where blind men lead blind men while Kṛṣṇa’s illusory energy entices us with her wonderful magic display to try to enjoy here and thus condemn ourselves to suffer again and again the anguish of birth and death. I doubt that any of us can fully appreciate the depth of your relationship with Kṛṣṇa, out of which grows the compassionate desire to offer us a restitution of our original identity. Of course, I don’t think that it would be wise for us to try to understand your intimate relationship with Kṛṣṇa prematurely, with the passionate haste characteristic of us Westerners. Just to recognize your liberating purity is fully enough to start to understand your personality. When I remember the words of your beloved disciple Jayānanda Prabhu, I think that he has expressed the essence of the reason why we, ordinary foolish children of Kali-yuga, feel unceasing love for you and confidence in you, which we would never be able to feel for anybody else. Jayānanda Prabhu said, “I wasn’t much of a religionist, but I was attracted to Śrīla Prabhupāda. I knew he didn’t want to cheat me, so I wanted to work for him.”

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we want to work for you!

\* \* \*

From the flourishing Nāma-haṭṭa center in eastern Czech Republic, Aśvahari dāsa, the initiator of this project, writes:

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Your Nāma-haṭṭa congregation in Moravia is slowly coming together. Now you have two Nāma-haṭṭa centers here, where congregational members gather every weekend to learn about the message you brought us, chant *kīrtanas,* prepare food for the Deities, and eat and distribute *prasādam*. Every Saturday we go on *harināma* to spread the holy name to the people of this region. Our children are also chanting and accepting this way of life to various degrees. They have many ideas about their future engagement in your mission when they grow up. Their parents are helping them become Kṛṣṇa conscious, and the children are trying to become Kṛṣṇa conscious themselves. Regular public lectures in various cities are going on, along with Kṛṣṇa conscious festivals. This year we distributed about 1,500 mahā-big books and big books, about 700 cookbooks, 700 magazines, and about 13,000 pieces and packages of *prasādam*. We hope we have pleased you by our endeavors, and hopefully we can maintain what you have given us and develop many other projects for spreading the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement here in Moravia.

\* \* \*

And finally from Govinda’s restaurant in the center of Prague, with Lomāñcita dāsa as its main manager, Kasya Prabhu writes:

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you!

I am approaching you on behalf of your devotees at Govinda’s restaurant in Prague.

You are the most merciful preacher, who has come from sacred Vṛndāvana, been blessed by Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, and been empowered by Lord Caitanya. Thus you are our link to Kṛṣṇa’s society. That is our great good fortune—to have you as our *parama-guru*. We are unworthy of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and unqualified to perform pure devotional service: our only hope is your patience and tolerance. We are puffed up by our egos: the remedy is your transcendental anger. We are weak, lazy, whimsical, and unclean: the antidote consists of the rules and regulations given by you. We are misled and possessed of skewed intelligence: the weapon of your “chopping-technique” preaching will set us straight. All in all, you are the ornament on the necklace of devotees of Gaura-Nitāi.

All your devotees at Govinda’s are working hard to please Kṛṣṇa, in hopes of one day communing with Him. But we know it is not possible even by the hardest work to please Kṛṣṇa directly. But is possible for us to please you, and by your pleasure Kṛṣṇa is pleased. Therefore it is essential that we remain your servants for all the time. For your pleasure we would like to report that during last year this Govinda’s restaurant served hundreds of thousands guests, who received one hundred tons of Pañca-tattva *prasādam*, including dishes like rice, *sabji, dāl, rayta, catni, samosā*, *purīs, capātī, pakorās, kofta, śrīkhand, burfi, kṣīra,* and many more.

\* \* \*

All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Thank you for saving our lives.

*Your grateful servants in the Czech Republic.*

## Dallas

Dear ever well-wisher Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. We beg you to always keep us under the shelter of the dust of your lotus feet. For without the dust from the feet of pure Vaiṣṇavas like yourself, there is no hope of ever achieving the revival of our dormant love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Exhibiting in fullness all twenty-six qualities of a pure devotee, you traversed the earth giving life, eternal life, in the form of your instructions, which we enthusiastically drank with our ears. Indeed, your instructions are living, and we never grow tired of hearing, chanting, and remembering all the nectar you have spoken or written. Your words are the panacea, the elixir for solving all the problems of our struggle for existence.

You have certainlyrefreshed our faith in God, giving us His name, His address, His pastimes, His associates, and His paraphernalia. This is all very nice, but if we fail to maintain our faith in you and neglect to respect and worship you, we will once again be shackled by the qualities of ignorance. Therefore it is our conviction that we must maintain full faith in both you and Kṛṣṇa. If we keep to this path, all understanding of the material and spiritual natures will automatically be revealed.

Without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no possibility of our understanding Kṛṣṇa. All our so-called self-realization would be a hallucination. We would all be victims of mental concoctions covering our souls with conceptions of voidism and impersonalism. Today, on this very auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, we want very much to express our appreciation for all you have accomplished for the benefit of the entire world. We want to acknowledge you as the rising sun that has dissipated the darkness of night in our souls. We wish to always eulogize you as the greatest preacher of modern times.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We pray we will always endeavor to fulfill your mission, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission, of preaching Kṛṣṇa’s glories throughout the world to every town and village.

Thank you very much.

*Your servants in Dallas, Texas, USA.*

## Debrecen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet! All glories to you and to your divine mission, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness!

Eleven years have passed since the devotees arrived here from Budapest to establish and spread the teachings of Lord Caitanya in this city. They were young and inexperienced, but they trusted and believed in you, confident that by following you they would be able to deliver the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa to the inhabitants of this city.

Since then we have moved to many better and worse places, but we feel more and more the necessity of living near your heart. Kali-yuga embitters everyone’s life, takes away everyone’s zest for life, confuses and misleads people. But praise be to Kṛṣṇa, you are here for us, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Under the protection of your careful instructions, we all feel great hope rising in our hearts. Under the patronage of your shelter, we feel that our lives have a real and eternal aim. By reading and hearing your enchanting books and lectures, even unqualified, lowborn conditioned souls like us can reach spiritual perfection.

As a glorious member of the *guru-paramparā,* you have revolutionized the lives of millions of people. You have taught them not to worship themselves or other insignificant persons but to worship those who really deserve it. You always glorify your spiritual master, the previous *ācāryas,* the great saints and sages. There isn’t any egoism or pride in you. You follow them and show us the right way. With your teachings and behavior you are a living *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* for us. You teach us how to be attached to the Supreme Lord, how to love Him with all our heart and soul, although in our hearts there are mostly envy and selfishness.

Lord Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (10.10):

*teṣāṁ satata-yuktānāṁ bhajatāṁ prīti-pūrvakam*  
*dadāmi buddhi-yogaṁ taṁ yena mām upayānti te*

**“To those who are constantly devoted to serving Me with love, I give the understanding by which they can come to Me.”**

When we remember you and your instructions, following this instruction of Kṛṣṇa’s seems so simple. But when we forget you and in this way we neglect your divine instructions, the purification seems to be so hard and hopeless.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please guide us on the way of purification. Forgive us for being lazy, foolish, and offensive. Without you we remain the victims of Kali-yuga, the servants of our senses and minds. We pray that you will raise us by your mercy from this aimless, confused situation and convert us into obedient, humble servants with only one desire: to please Kṛṣṇa by any means, in other words, to serve your divine mission until the moment of our deaths.

We ask you to engage us in the world-saving movement of Lord Caitanya for the rest of our lives, so that we can always chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and distribute your books and the Lord’s divine delicious *prasādam* to the unhappy people.

*Your eternal servants in Debrecen, Hungary.*

## Detroit

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 1.20–21) Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī says:

*granthera ārambhe kari ‘maṅgalācaraṇa’*  
*guru, vaiṣṇava, bhagavān,—tinera smaraṇa*

**In the beginning of this narration, simply by remembering the spiritual master, the devotees of the Lord, and the Personality of Godhead, I have invoked their benedictions.**

*tinera smaraṇe haya vighna-vināśana*  
*anāyāse haya nija vāñchita-pūraṇa*

**Such remembrance destroys all difficulties and very easily enables one to fulfill his own desires.**

By translating this most valuable scripture, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given mankind the greatest gift, which can solve all the problems of the material world.

O savior of the universe, because of your unlimited mercy, all your servants at Devasadan Mandir are working together to spread your mission under the loving care and guidance of your GBCs and senior disciples. Your “doll museum,” which we lovingly call The Bhaktivedanta Theater, has been upgraded with the dedication and hard work of Śrīman Bhūśāya Prabhu, Śrīman Caitanya Rūpa Prabhu, Mother Mṛgākṣī Prabhu and her family, His Grace Amiyātmā Prabhu, His Grace Juṣya Prabhu, Bhakta John, and Bhakta Joy. Your dedicated Ratha-yātrā team, under the leadership of Śrīman Jagat Puruṣa Prabhu, Śrīman Mādhav dāsa Prabhu, and the most blessed Parasher Family, are bringing bliss to Detroit by arranging the most wonderful Ratha-yātrā and other festivals every year. Because of your special blessings for Devasadan Mandir, we have the most sincere team of *pūjārīs,* under the leadership of Śrīman Caitanya Rūpa Prabhu and Śrīman Giri Govardhan Prabhu. Being pleased with their dedicated service, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī have arranged an abundance of roses for their garlands and vases through Śrīman Mādhav dāsa Prabhu. The hard work of these devotees shows their faith in and love for you. Without that, how could these amazing things happen?

Our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are very fortunate to offer you the most beautiful book display, which we call Śrīla Prabhupāda Granth Mandir. This was the hard work of Śrīman Akhilānanda Prabhu and his family, with the support of Nikesh and Naimesh Patel. We pray that every temple can establish such a Granth Mandir. Just the presence of the Śrīla Prabhupāda Granth Mandir encourages our book distributors to distribute more and more of your books under the leadership of Śrīman Puruṣa Sūkta dāsa. All the visitors are automatically attracted to look at your books and inquire about them as they enter the temple. Even though we are facing many challenges in the affairs of the material world, by your blessings alone we have the faith to always feel protected at your lotus feet. Please give us the intelligence to always keep you as our life and soul so that we will never deviate from the path you have shown us.

O dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, please appeal to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī to always hold us tight; otherwise we have the tendency to run away from Their shelter.

*Your humble servants at Devasadan Mandir, Detroit, Michigan, USA.*  
(written by Jagad-Guru dāsa)

## Durban

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nāma-śreṣṭhaṁ manum api śacī-putram atra svarūpaṁ*  
*rūpaṁ tasyāgrajam uru-purīṁ māthurīṁ goṣṭhavāṭīm*  
*rādhā-kuṇḍaṁ giri-varam aho rādhikā-mādhavāśāṁ*  
*prāpto yasya prathita-kṛpayā śrī-guruṁ taṁ nato’smi*

**“I bow down to the beautiful lotus feet of my spiritual master, by whose causeless mercy I have obtained the supreme holy name, the divine mantra, the service of the son of Śacī-mātā, the association of Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara, Rūpa Gosvāmī, and his older brother Sanātana Gosvāmī, the supreme abode of Mathurā, the blissful abode of Vṛndāvana, the divine Rādhā-kuṇḍa and Govardhana Hill, and the desire within my heart for the loving service of Śrī Rādhikā and Mādhava in Vṛndāvana.” (*****Śrī Mukta-caritam,*** **by Śrīla Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the transcendental gifts you have offered us can permanently eradicate all material miseries and overcome any spiritual difficulties. And beyond just doing away with difficulties, those gifts enthuse and enliven us on the spiritual platform. They enable us to experience the highest nectar—transcendental loving service at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha.

Recently we have been confronted with challenges that sometimes seem insurmountable. As we look here and there for solutions, we end up perplexed. Time and again we go through this and eventually come to the same solution—the shelter of your lotus feet. That shelter is easily accessible for those who sincerely strive to purify their hearts through chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa in a spirit of humble servitude.

The essence of all your instructions was to chant Kṛṣṇa’s name. Let us all strive to do just that—together. In this way you will be supremely glorified.

*he guro jñāna-da dīna-bandho*  
*svānanda-dātaḥ karuṇaika-sindho*  
*vṛndāvanāsīna hitāvatāra*  
*prasīda rādhā-praṇaya-pracāra*

**“O spiritual master, O giver of divine wisdom, O friend of the fallen, you are the giver of your own bliss, and you are the only ocean of mercy. Although dwelling in Vṛndāvana, you have descended for the welfare of fallen souls like myself, and you are preaching the divine love of Rādhā for Kṛṣṇa. Please be kind upon me.”**

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants in the service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha in Durban, South Africa.*

## East Hartford

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

One can appreciate what you have done for this world by seeing all the temples you have established, and also by seeing all the devotees who were made by your mercy. But one who reads your books submissively can then appreciate the sacrifice you have made for this world. Your greatness can be understood to some extent by attentively hearing the purports in the books you have translated. One who sincerely reads your books with a submissive service attitude can obtain your mercy and make spiritual progress.

You have established a perpetual society that will continue to grow as long as your books are read by sincere souls. Every word you chose is absolutely perfect. One can certainly associate with you through your books. And by that association one can become purified, to the extent that one follows the directions you prescribe in your books.

I believe that an understanding of your greatness—as much as it is possible for any of us to attain—can be attained by associating with you through your books. Your purports guide the reader step by step in the proper direction. You have deliberately chosen every word so the real meaning of the verse can be understood. What a noble sacrifice you have made for this world! Every living entity in human society can benefit from reading your books.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for making such a sacrifice. May the inhabitants of this world perpetually distribute and read your books. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants at the ISKCON center in East Hartford, Connecticut, USA.*  
(written by Pyāri Mohan dāsa)

## Eger

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to you!

On this most auspicious day we’d like to thank you for everything you gave to us. Thank you for bringing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the West so we can also get the blessings of it.

It was not an ordinary thing. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvati had so many disciples, but you were the only one who could fulfill his desire. You said the secret was that you blindly followed his instructions. Your are the perfect example for us of how seriously one has to take the instructions of the spiritual master. You prepared yourself during your whole lifetime by always meditating on and working toward this one objective: spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English language.

Before you came to New York, nobody could have done anything similar. Lord Caitanya gave you the ability to do it. As Niṣkiñcana Kṛṣṇadāsa Bābājī said: you were the most confident servant of the holy name because you gave Him to everyone. And what could be greater service to the Lord than to relieve His headache due to the suffering of the conditioned living entities? The living entities suffer because they’ve forgotten Kṛṣṇa, but by hearing and chanting the holy names they can awaken their love for Him and become free of suffering.

You said that if we knew who you were we’d faint. But still, each of us and the whole of human society is thankful to you for the extraordinary thing you did. When we preach we can experience the difficulties of this and we can imagine how difficult your mission was. But because you were empowered directly by Lord Caitanya, what could be difficult for you? Still, out of humility you took the role of a pioneer-devotee. Your success was assured because, as you said, “I feel that the whole *paramparā* is standing behind me and helping me.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the determination and faith to follow your instructions. Then we can be sure of success in our effort to go home, back to Godhead.

*Your servants, the devotees in Eger, Hungary.*

## Ekaterinburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances unto the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

On this auspicious day of your divine appearance, please let me try to glorify your transcendental activities, although I feel so unworthy and unqualified, like that poor frog in the well who tried to measure the ocean.

By your great devotion you delivered the transcendental message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to the people of Kali-yuga, who are infected with materialism and Māyāvāda philosophy. You gave us Lord Caitanya’s panacea—*saṅkīrtana*, the congregational glorification of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s holy name. You said that it is the easiest method of self-realization, and now I understand that it really is easy, but only in the association of sincere devotees. It is impossible to practice it alone. *Saṅkīrtana* is an easy method for simple people. Without simplicity, it is impossible to stay in association of devotees. From the other side, one may develop simplicity and humility in the association of devotees. As I understood from your books and from my own experience, Kṛṣṇa cannot be satisfied with the results of our service if we don’t want to cooperate with other devotees. But He is very happy when we make mutual efforts to satisfy Him, even if the results of such service are not so great. So when we chant the holy names together, we feel bliss and happiness and joy. It is because of our mood of cooperation that in our Ekaterinburg *yātrā* the congregational chanting of the holy names of the Lord is so happily practiced. The mood of *saṅkīrtana* saturates all our usual activities, and this *saṅkīrtana* mood is the best fruit of any manager in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This is your nicest gift to the conditioned souls—the opportunity to spiritualize our activities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I bow down unto you again and again and thank you for all your mercy. May you be always glorified in all the three worlds.

*Your servants in Ekaterinburg, Russia.*  
(written by Gaveśvara Gopāl dāsa)

## Gītā-nāgarī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are my father, my guru, my guide.  
You give me shelter always. You are there  
in your books. I love the *Bhāgavatam* especially.  
Why don’t I take shelter there more?

Once I collected flowers from many houses  
(yes, I asked permission) and strung a garland  
for you. Once I made cookies, and you passed  
them out with your own hand. I gave you a string  
of bells, and with them, my heart. It leapt out,  
throwing itself at your feet for all past, present,  
and future. You know this is the truth.

Before meeting you, before coming in touch  
with your temples, your books, your devotees,  
I was as lost as a boat at sea. Your movement,  
Lord Caitanya’s movement, rescued me. How  
could I leave your service?

I don’t do much. But every day I try to do  
something devotional for you, my Guru Mahārāja, and Kṛṣṇa.  
What else is worth trying for? I still long to take part  
in some wonderful adventure, some noteworthy achievement,  
that will please you in a big way. But even if all I can do  
is look at some pictures—Jāhnavā *ghat*, friends on a Vṛndāvana lane—  
and try to pay attention to my *japa*, maybe you’ll still be pleased.

Like in Hawaii when you called your leading men  
(who were hot in some managerial debate) to look over  
the balcony at some *mātājī* chanting her rounds:  
“Just see—she is simply chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa.”  
I wasn’t there. I read it in the *Līlāmṛta* or *Nectar* book later.  
I still like to read and hear about you.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, when will I see you again?  
Again give my heart—again be forgiven for so many  
discrepancies. Again roar in *kīrtan*, genuine soul to soul,  
jump and catch a flower from your hand—when?

Until then, please keep me near your sincere followers.  
They are as numerous and as various as the moods of nature.  
Please, let me prove true. And one fine day, somewhere in  
the fullness of eternal time, serve our Lord  
and His Lady, in the camp of your followers,  
guided by Your Divine Grace.

*Your servants at Gītā-nāgarī dhāma, Pennsylvania, USA.*  
(written by Vegavatī-devī dāsī)

## Gītā-nāgarī Baru

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who remain a constant source of inspiration to all who come in contact with you.

Our devotees at Gītā-nāgarī Baru are not able to communicate very easily, being situated in a remote area on the island of Sumatra here in Indonesia. I am therefore writing on their behalf to inform you of our on-going activities there.

We are deeply indebted to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing us to help manifest in some small way your desire for establishing a *varṇāśrama* community. It is now two and half years since we acquired our 150 acres of land in the province of Lampung. During this period, by your mercy only, we have been able to begin a small village community, which is gradually unfolding more and more. Our devotees attend both the morning and evening programs, as you instructed us, and the rest of the day they are engaged mostly in simple farming activities.

Since last year, three new families have settled at Gītā-nāgarī Baru and four new homes have been constructed, bringing the total number of residents to forty-five. In the middle of January 2002 our first calf was born, and we named him Ekādaśī since he happened to take birth on Ekādaśī day. In the month of February two children were born, one on the day immediately following Śrī Jayadeva Gosvāmī’s disappearance day. So his name will be Jayadeva. The other one is a baby girl born on the day of Gaṅgā Sāgar Melā, so her name is Gaṅgā. This brings our total child population to fifteen, almost all under age five. Our *mātājīs* have set up a playground area for the children and weekly have special activities for them. We are now seriously discussing how we can provide these young children with proper education, since this was a most important area you stressed in your “Essay on Gita Nagari.”

Our project called “Śrī Viṣṇu Sahasranāma Vana” is progressing nicely by your mercy; it has been on-going now for the last three months. Taking advantage of the rainy season, we have already planted 1,000 fruit trees around the three-kilometer walk path that the devotees have cleared around our boundary land. Over 800 plaques and cement posts have so far been made. These will display the 1,000 names of Lord Viṣṇu in three languages—Sanskrit, Indonesian, and English. The project will allow devotees to walk around your property chanting the holy names, as one does on *parikramā* in Vṛndāvana. As devotees perform their *parikramā* they will automatically circumambulate Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-Gopāl, our presiding Deities at Gītā-Nāgarī Baru.

Every Saturday afternoon the residents of Gītā-Nāgarī Baru take you and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi on our bullock cart pulled by your servant, our bull Param Dharma, to perform *parikramā* around the temple. We are free to chant as loud as we want, since our closest neighbors are two kilometers away. In the evening on Saturday, after the *Bhagavad-gītā* class, our devotees hold their weekly *iṣṭagoṣṭhī* to discuss issues of concern within the community. And every Sunday the *mātājīs* get together to prepare your traditional Sunday Love Feast. In this way our devotees are keeping engaged and endeavoring to follow your good instructions and example.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have many, many things to learn as we slowly endeavor to develop this small community. We are far from being self-sufficient, but we are working in that direction. Please forgive our many mistakes and offenses. We are all your small and inexperienced children who need your constant care and guidance.

It is by your mercy and inspiration only that we are attempting to develop this project, which we hope one day will become dear to you. We therefore pray for your continued guidance and protection so that we may all advance in your devotional service. Our only desire is to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for we know that Lord Kṛṣṇa becomes very satisfied with those who please His pure devotee and showers His benedictions upon them.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for engaging us in your service.

*Your servants at the Gītā-nāgarī Baru farm community in Lampung, Sumatra, Indonesia.*  
(written by Bhakti Rāghava Swami)

## Kurukshetra

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my *daṇḍavat praṇāmas* at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

On your glorious Vyāsa-pūjā day, I ponder how much I am indebted to you for what you have given us. What you have done is all real *paropakāra*, which is even difficult for an average person to understand. Although the process you have given is feasible for everybody, only an intelligent person can understand what it is all about. By introducing the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you have established the ultimate business for all human beings. By making known the *yuga-avatāra*, Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu, you have bestowed eligibility on everyone to succeed in the business of life. By familiarizing the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* you have trapped everyone into the prime business of life. By introducing the concept of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*, you have made everyone hanker for Kṛṣṇa’s business. As I recognize and realize all this year after year, I fervently pray at the feet of Lord Gaurāṅga to keep my mind bathed in the nectar of your instructions in the form of your books, and to be guarded by your trusted followers. Your instructions being nondifferent from your *vapu* presence, I wish to follow them always and thus remain eternally in your association as your eternal follower.

*Your servants at the ISKCON center in Kurukshetra, India.*  
(written by Śukadeva Swami)

## Lisbon

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Last year in your Vyāsa-pūjā book one of the homages emphatically said that the feeling we should always maintain in our heart toward you is gratitude. After some reflection, one could ask, What does it really mean to be grateful?

To be grateful to you means to stay within this transcendental movement of yours—ISKCON—which has brought us from a situation of perpetual conflict to a situation of serenity, from a situation of ignorance to a situation of self-knowledge. It also means to remain always conscious of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and to remain conscious that our mission is to follow your example.

We will try to be regulated, calm, educated, poetic, truthful, and merciful. This is your mission—to show to the whole world that a Vaiṣṇava is a first-class gentleman, and that the most important rule is to give to others the mercy you have given us.

We are only a small *yātrā* in a small country, but we cherish the big desire to maintain your presence in our lives by maintaining the programs you have established. We are preaching at universities, distributing *prasādam* in our restaurant and in our Food for Life program, and chanting the holy names on *harināma*.

We are still not doing enough distribution of your books. But by your blessings, in 2002 we hope to again engage in mass distribution of your books in Portugal. This we know is the most important part of your mission—the distribution of transcendental literature.

Nevertheless, we go on serving you, knowing that we have much more to do. And if someone asks us “Who is Śrīla Prabhupāda?” we will give the same answer that you gave in regard to your Guru Mahārāja: “Śrīla Prabhupāda is a Vaikuṇṭha man.”

*Your humble servants in Lisbon, Portugal.*

## London (Soho)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances and please forgive all our offenses against you. All glories to you!

Toward the end of last year we established here in ISKCON London a Temple Leaders Council (TLC), consisting of fifteen devotees. One of our first projects was to select temple goals for this year. After a few meetings, we concluded *unanimously* that our number-one goal for 2002 would be to distribute double the amount of your books we had distributed in 2001. You told us in the mid-70s to “double it,” so even though we’re a quarter of a century late, we thought, “Why not at least *now* make that our goal?” Our duty and privilege is to glorify you, and we think there is no better way than to distribute your books. After all, you spent your whole life writing them (and preparing to write them); the least we can do is make sure they get distributed.

When you were physically here with us, you pushed us to distribute your books. Why should that change now that you have disappeared from our physical vision? Has your desire stopped? Is your order no longer valid simply because you have withdrawn your *vapu*? As you write of your own spiritual master¾both profoundly and poetically¾in dedicating to him your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, “He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.” Your instructions are similarly eternal, as must be our following them.

At the time of this writing, shortly after the appearance day of Lord Nityānanda, we have embarked on a one-month book distribution marathon leading up to Gaura Pūrṇimā¾a special, concentrated effort to distribute your books. This does not mean, however, that our temple authorities will whip us to extreme and unsustainable austerities, such as sleeping little or staying out late. Rather, they try to emphasize and exemplify strong *sādhana*, knowing that empowerment to distribute your books comes from hearing and chanting “with rapt attention” in the association of devotees. (And there is no need to force us; we *enjoy* distributing your books!)

Indeed, the spiritual strength to distribute your books comes from the books themselves, i.e., from studying them. Anyone who studies your books can quickly understand how profound is the knowledge in them and how crucial their distribution is for the salvation of human society—a goal about which no other philosophy or institution has a clue, either theoretically or practically.

We see several important benefits of distributing your books:

1\. Pleasing you and the entire disciplic succession, whose mercy will surely pour down upon us, freeing us from material slavery.

2\. Pleasing the Deities.

3\. Purifying the distributors, as well as the devotees who facilitate the distributors.

4\. Purifying and enlivening the whole environment in which the books are distributed.

5\. Spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

6\. Increasing the enthusiasm of all the devotees, giving them a higher taste of transcendental pleasure.

7\. Invoking in others appreciation for our movement’s practical missionary work.

8\. Bringing *lakṣmī* into the temple.

9\. Helping build Māyāpur City (the BBT sends to Māyāpur twenty-five percent of whatever we remit for books).

10\. Ensuring that everyone has meaningful engagement (anyone can help, somehow or other, to distribute your books, regardless of degree of qualification).

We have even heard that you said that distributing your books can prevent world war!

So, we have excellent reasons for emphasizing the distribution of your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Sometimes devotees say, though, that distributing your books is fanatical. They say that many apparently stalwart devotees have energetically distributed your books, but that most of them are no longer with us. They say we lost these devotees precisely because of their enthusiasm for distributing your books.

But that devotees left our association *after* having distributed your books does not mean they left *because of* having distributed your books. The living entity is always marginal and therefore always has freedom to choose. Devotees leave ISKCON for multifarious reasons that have nothing to do with a particular service. And enthusiasm for distributing your books cannot be a cause of falldown, since there has never been in our movement anyone as enthusiastic about distributing your books as you are!

You have told us that the active principle in spiritual life is to simply follow the order of the spiritual master. We therefore accept your instruction to practice strong *sādhana* while extensively distributing your books. (We’ve heard that you said that from Oxford Street we can reach the whole world.) And we therefore daily experience most of the benefits listed above.

We want to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you have saved our lives and the lives of countless others. Without your guidance we don’t know which way to turn or what move to make. We simply depend on you. So we’ll do what you did, and we’ll do what you told us to do. We’ll dedicate our lives to spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness by the best possible means, instructed and exemplified by you: giving to others the knowledge contained in your books, which means, first of all, getting them to accept and read your books. This knowledge has transformed our lives and will also transform theirs.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Thank you for giving us this auspicious opportunity to help you distribute your books! Please bless us to double our distribution this year ¾ and year after year after year!

*Your servants, the servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara, London, UK.*

## London (South)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is absolutely amazing that by the order of your spiritual master you came to the West at such an advanced age to save materialistic people like us.

Any activities performed for the satisfaction of the Lord under the direction of the spiritual master are spiritual.

*māṁ ca yo ’vyabhicāreṇa bhakti-yogena sevate*  
*sa guṇān samatītyaitān brahma-bhūyāya kalpate*

**“Anyone who engages in spiritual devotional service without motivation, rendering such service for the satisfaction of the Lord, is elevated immediately to the spiritual platform, and all his activities are spiritual.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **14.26, as quoted in** ***Caitanya-caritāmṛta*****,** ***Ādi*** **14.29)**

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“To one who has staunch faith in the words of the spiritual master and the words of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the secret of success in Vedic knowledge is revealed.” (*****Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad*** **6.23)**

You propagated the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement according to these principles, and therefore your preaching work is going on successfully.

Prahlāda Mahārāja tells Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (7.9.44):

My dear Lord, I see that there are many saintly persons indeed, but they are interested only in their own deliverance. Not caring for the big cities and towns, they go to the Himalayas or the forest to meditate with vows of silence. They are not interested in delivering others. As for me, however, I do not wish to be liberated alone, leaving aside all these poor fools and rascals. I know that without Kṛṣṇa consciousness, without taking shelter of Your lotus feet, one cannot be happy. Therefore I wish to bring them back to shelter at Your lotus feet.

You’re that kind of saintly person, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the kind who wants to be right in the midst of people. Out of compassion, you want to deliver to them, as many as possible, the sublime message of Lord Caitanya. Therefore you have opened up centers and temples in every town and village of the world.
