Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Thank you.

Never have these words been more appropriately addressed to anyone. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for rescuing us from the darkest ignorance. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for forcefully enlightening us with your torch of knowledge. And thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for not stopping there.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, surely it is your special mercy upon us that the enlightenment we have received from you is multi-dimensional. Not only have we been enabled to distinguish the path of light from the path of darkness in life, but you have also given us the association of your devotees and the responsibility to enlighten others, essential elements in our ability to sustain our enlightenment.

I had a wonderful realization about the depth of the enlightenment you have favored us with during a recent conversation with one of your early disciples. She was relating the story of how she achieved your mercy. She was searching, looking for answers, yet unable to find answers or make sense of her world. Then she met your devotees. At once everything was clear. Just as the verse describes, it was as if she was in a dark cave and a torch was suddenly brought before her eyes. She was enlightened. She could see what was wrong with her life and how to put herself on the right path, the path to happiness. How excited she was! She thought everyone could or should be seeing what she was seeing.

Enthusiastically she tried to share her enlightenment with her family and friends, but to her surprise, they just didn’t get it. They couldn’t see what she saw. They didn’t care about what she cared about. In fact, they didn’t seem to care about her anymore. She was crestfallen, but realized that the first aspect of true enlightenment is that it separates one from those in darkness.

*yā niśā sarva-bhūtānāṁ tasyāṁ jāgarti saṁyamī*  
*yasyāṁ jāgrati bhūtāni sā niśā paśyato muneḥ*

**“What is night for all beings is the time of awakening for the self-controlled, and the time of awakening for all beings is night for the introspective sage.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.69)**

Although now enlightened, she understood that she must begin a new search. Fortunately, because she received her enlightenment from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, she was now ready to receive your second installment of enlightenment, the association of your devotees.

Living in the association of your devotees, she found others who could see what she was seeing and could help her see the Absolute Truth more clearly. She found a family and friends who appreciated her and taught her to appreciate Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa and you, His pure representative.

*mac-cittā mad-gata-prāṇā bodhayantaḥ parasparam*  
*kathayantaś ca māṁ nityaṁ tuṣyanti ca ramanti ca*

**“The thoughts of my pure devotees dwell in Me, their lives are fully devoted to My service, and they derive great satisfaction and bliss from always enlightening one another and conversing about Me.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **10.9)**

Enlightened and living with other like-minded devotees, your disciple was certainly well situated. However, there is also a reason why she initially wanted others to see what she saw when she became enlightened. She had been enlightened by your mercy. One who receives your mercy is characterized by not being content with one’s own enlightenment but being eager to enlighten and benefit others. Being blessed by you, how could she not now desire the welfare of others?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is your third installment of enlightenment, the responsibility to enlighten others. Because your desire to enlighten others is directly linked to the order of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, it encompasses the entire world, and has made us bold enough to think that by your grace we can enlighten anyone anywhere.

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi-grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

**“In as many towns and villages as there are on the surface of the earth, My holy name will be preached.” (*****Caitanya-bhāgavata, Antya*** **4.126)**

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you for lifetimes of service in your merciful association.

Thank you for future installments of enlightenment we are sure you will bestow upon us.

*Your servants at New Ramaṇ Reti, Alachua, Florida, USA.*

## New Remuṇā

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It has been almost twenty-five years since you walked among us. Where has the time gone? You once said that Kṛṣṇa took over the world with only five devotees (the Pāṇḍavas) and that you could do the same with a handful of serious devotees. So how serious are we? How much do we appreciate the matchless gift you have given us? Why is it that our attempts to repay our debt to you are always inadequate?

On this blessed day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, it behooves each of us to renew his or her vows to you with great enthusiasm. Trying to maintain ourselves within this material world often fills our days and leaves little time for spiritual endeavors. We forget that devotional service is the only way to be truly happy in this world and the next. We forget that we are spirit souls, trapped within material bodies. We act as though we are bodies with spiritual appendages called souls. We must give up this false idea and follow your instructions.

When you decided to come to the West to distribute love of Kṛṣṇa, the world did not know its great fortune. Only a few were able to take advantage of your personal presence. You said that *vāṇī* (the instructions) are more important than *vapu* (the personal form). By distributing your books, we can ensure that you will always be among us and that everyone will have a chance to take advantage of your storehouse of transcendental knowledge. Also, introducing someone to a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa is the greatest act of compassion one living entity can do for another.

To become serious we have only to remember the great hardship you underwent to bring Kṛṣṇa consciousness to us. You completely neglected your own comfort, left the peace and tranquility of Vṛndāvana, and came to the middle of degradation. You did not know us materially, but spiritually you knew we were all spirit souls, part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa. You did this for us without any thought of personal gain but rather out of your sincere desire to please your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. If we can remember this, and if we can muster a desire to try to repay you, then our attempts at devotional service will seem easy in comparison.

In 1979 there were over one hundred devotees living here in New Remuṇā Dhāma, and the outside, “karmi” world was viewed as a hostile, terrible place. Now there are only fifteen to twenty devotees living in the temple, but there are innumerable householders in the area who keep Kṛṣṇa in the center of their activities and welcome devotees to their homes for “satsangs” and *prasādam*. We must all work as a team if we have any hopes of pleasing you. You said, “Your love for me shall be shown by how you cooperate to serve me.” Thus by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can gradually conquer the material world. Perhaps if you allow us to come back to this material world for several lifetimes we may be able to help in some small way to fulfill your mission of having everyone become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

On this day, the wonderful celebration of your appearance, we pray that we can become more sincere in our efforts to follow your instructions. We know that there is no way to ever repay our debt to you, but we also know that our success lies in the endeavor to do so.

Thank you for saving us.

*The devotees of New Remuṇā Dhāma, Toronto, Canada.*

## New Seva-kuñja

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this auspicious anniversary of your appearance!

*na karma-bandhanaṁ janma vaiṣṇavānāṁ ca vidyate*  
*viṣṇur anucaratvaṁ hi mokṣam āhur manīṣiṇaḥ*

**“A Vaiṣṇava does not take birth under the jurisdiction of karmic law. His birth and disappearance are transcendental. The wise have declared that the servants of Viṣṇu are eternally engaged in the liberated service of the Lord and hence are free from the laws of material nature.” (*****Padma Purāṇa,*** **quoted in the** ***Hari-bhakti-vilāsa,*** **10.113)**

As with all previous *ācāryas* and eternal associates of the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, your apparent coming and going in this land of illusion simply constituted a tool like any other you fully and expertly engaged in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa. You passed through the threshold of birth 106 years ago just to execute the desires of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Though you were then thinly veiled as a man of this world, today it is easy to see that you are the person empowered by the Supreme Lord to spread Vaiṣṇavism to every corner of the universe. Similarly, the idea of death is only another venue through which you have completely served and glorified Lord Kṛṣṇa while shedding light upon us by your perfect example.

As amazingly as you utilized your so-called birth and death for the service of the Lord, you also see all fallen souls as the dear servants of Lord Kṛṣṇa, ripe and ready to be engaged in His service.

*vaiṣṇavera nindya-karma nāhi pāḍe kāṇe*  
*sabe kṛṣṇa bhajana kare,—ei-mātra jāne*

**“He \[Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī\] would not listen to blasphemy of a Vaiṣṇava, nor would he listen to talk of a Vaiṣṇava’s misbehavior. He knew only that everyone was engaged in Kṛṣṇa’s service; he did not understand anything else.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya*** **13.133)**

And from the purport:

He saw only that everyone was engaged in Kṛṣṇa’s service. That is the position of a *mahā-bhāgavata*. Actually, even if one is serving Māyā, in a higher sense he is also a servant of Kṛṣṇa. Because Māyā is the servant of Kṛṣṇa, anyone serving Māyā serves Kṛṣṇa indirectly.

Those of us who have come under your care have been immediately put to use as instruments of your desire to please the Supreme Lord. Much like any expert craftsperson who knows how to make something out of practically nothing, you have so mercifully put us together to create the hub of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. We resist, we offend, and we sometimes doubt ourselves, but ultimately we have tasted divine devotional service through contact with you, and we are somehow developing the desire to have the desire to spread it to other lost souls, those whom you see only as Lord Kṛṣṇa’s misdirected servants. You are the original “Hare Kṛṣṇa person,” the *mahā-bhāgavata* for us to stand behind in all of our endeavors.

As the most perfect servant of your spiritual master, you have shown us the way of unflinching adherence to the instructions of guru as the ultimate means of success.

Some of them said that it is greatly fortunate for the Americans that I have started the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in America. If I have any credit in this connection, it does not belong to me personally, but it is due to my eternal spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda. \[*Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* Preface\]

Once, while landing at the Chicago airport, you noticed the name of the airport—O’Hare. You then made a simple suggestion that Tripurāri Mahārāja approach the airport authorities and ask them to change the name from O’Hare to “O Hare Kṛṣṇa Airport.” He took your words seriously and, in spite of being rejected by the airport officials, he acted boldly according to your instruction. We would like to keep this effort clearly in our minds. That is, to accept your instructions with all our hearts and to push on the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Chicago to the point that the name of the airport becomes changed automatically.

Just as you see every living being as a servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa, you see Lord Kṛṣṇa everywhere.

*yo māṁ paśyati sarvatra sarvaṁ ca mayi paśyati*  
*tasyāhaṁ na praṇaśyāmi sa ca me na praṇaśyati*

**“For one who sees Me everywhere and sees everything in Me, I am never lost, nor is he ever lost to Me.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **6.30)**

Lord Kṛṣṇa never leaves Vṛndāvana, and you never leave Lord Kṛṣṇa. You have brought Vṛndāvana wherever you go, transforming everything in your path. Please allow us to serve you today on this holiest of days, and every day, thinking only of your pure devotion to the Lord and your selfless desire to bring that Vṛndāvana to every living being.

*Your humble servants at New Seva-kuñja, Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī’s Mandir in Chicago, Illinois, USA.*

## New Śrīvas Aṅgam

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your Lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

In expressing our heartfelt gratitude to you as our guardian and savior in this great dangerous ocean of Kali-yuga, we would like to serve you a nice birthday feast. It has been cooked with love and devotion by the combined efforts of all the devotees, and we have tried especially to pick out some of your favorite preparations. Here we will mention a few of the many dishes.

We first serve you nicely cooked white *basmati* rice with yellow ghee on top. This is the basic food for all people from Bengal, their grain of life. You are our white bull of Dharma, who stands firmly on the four legs of religious principles, and our spiritual father, who supplies real life and sustenance. How much you have sacrificed for the deliverance of future generations and us!

The drink is *lassi,* which is cooling in the summer heat, just as you are like the cooling moon in subduing the passion of the material world.

The *sabjī,* rice, and dahl have been cooked on the three-tired cooker you always used to bring along on your travels and which you taught your servants to use. It is an example of the efficient practicality you brought to all aspects of life. Similarly, we are taking lessons from you in the art of practical spiritual life.

On one side of your plate there is a big silver bowl of *śāka,* which is Lord Caitanya’s favorite dish. It is made from the top shoots of nettles, which we have carefully collected in the garden. You also pick out the many different *anarthas* from our hearts with the risk of being burned, but you are not greatly affected.

The *kachaurīs* are fried in ghee and have a filling of mashed potatoes and spices. As your nickname, Kachaurī-mukhī, denotes, this is your special favorite, as you are to us. During childhood you were very pleased to fill the small pockets in your vest with *kachaurīs*. And as you were later on very pleased by all the different preaching efforts of your followers, we beg for the ability to assist you in fulfilling Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep us in your pocket always.

We pray that you will forever stay in the pockets of our hearts so that we will be able to get the satisfactory taste of *bhakti,* the nourishment of realization of Kṛṣṇa, and the detachment from worldly things, which comes from each morsel of your nectarean service. The chutney that goes with the *kachaurīs* is made from pineapple. And it is just like book distribution. Almost too hot and demanding to do and too sweet and relishable to resist.

We then bring in hot *purīs* right off the stove. In the evenings, after long days of preaching, you occasionally used to relish them with hot milk. One of the things that we especially admire you for is that you are such a warm personality. Even though having so many things to accomplish, you were always ready to spend your precious time with those who needed association and guidance. All people loved you, because you loved everyone.

For sweets we have *gulābjāmuns,* your ISKCON bullets. You would use them to shoot down our lingering attractions for Māyā. These sugar-laden balls converted hippies into happies, and even now, thirty-five years later, you have not run out of ammunition. There is no sweeter thing than being the target for your mercy.

In the end we bring in differently flavored Simply Wonderfuls, and they are like a Vedic *sūtra* describing your personality: Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are simply wonderful!!

Thank you for all that you have done for us,  
Your humble servants at New Śrīvas Aṅgam, Copenhagen, Denmark.

## New Vraja-dhāma

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to you! All glories to your divine appearance day!

In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.1.6 you write:

Śukadeva Gosvāmī had already explained how people enter hellish life, and he could also explain how they could be saved from it. Intelligent men must take advantage of these instructions. Unfortunately, however, the entire world is lacking Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and therefore people are suffering from the grossest ignorance and do not even believe in a life after this one. To convince them of their next life is very difficult because they have become almost mad in their pursuit of material enjoyment. Nevertheless, our duty, the duty of all sane men, is to save them. Mahārāja Parīkṣit is the representative of one who can save them.

For us, *you* are the representative of one who can save “them,” namely us. We do not know what hellish life is, although we experience it at every moment. As you explained, it is very difficult to convince us that we have another life after this one, and because we believe that this is our one and only life, we work hard to get the most happiness out of it. We are almost mad in our pursuit of material enjoyment because we are addicted to it. But the duty of a sane man is to save such fallen souls. Demons think. “Let me be happy; I am not interested in others’ welfare.” Devotees think, “Let everyone be happy” (*sarve sukhino bhavantu*). Real Vaiṣṇavas are *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī,* so compassionate that, although they themselves know no unhappiness, they feel unhappiness on account of others’ suffering.

You showed us how to save others. You suffered so much and gave your “blood” to save us, the fallen souls. You tolerated heart attacks, headaches, and all kinds of bodily discomfort from constant traveling, in this way showing us that preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not an easy thing and that we also have tolerate such difficulties. You are the representative of one who can save others.

There are many things in this world. Every year inventors find newer and newer ways to make our lives easier and more comfortable, and scientists produce more detailed knowledge of this material world. Many people in the develop countries have more money than they need. What else is needed? What is missing? You kindly gave the answer: the entire world is lacking Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This is what is really missing, but we do not even realize it.

You came to the Western countries, where preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the most difficult task. You started ISKCON with the idea that its members, if they would follow you, would help you fulfill Lord Caitanya’s preaching plan. You entrusted and empowered your disciples to take up this mission, and now they are empowering their disciples to follow your footsteps.

Please give us your blessings and let us, your grand-disciples in New Vraja-dhāma, be your faithful representatives so we can successfully discharge the duties you have explained and shown us.

*Your insignificant servants in New Vraja-dhāma, Hungary.*  
(written by Manorāma dāsa)

## New Vraja (Khasadeśa)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy the whole earth has been delivered, and we are unlimitedly grateful to you for everything you have brought to us. By your mercy, we have been delivered. Could anyone else give us the things you have given us? No. You are the only one.

When we look at the moon, we see the soft moonlight caressing the land, the river, the mountain, the forest, and the lawn. And just as the moonlight in the sky enlivens everything, so the nectarean milk of your books quenches the thirst in every heart. We all love you. We love you unlimitedly.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, because of your care, this barren land has begun to sprout. And because of your care, this land will be fruitful.

Your words have encouraged your grandchildren for all time. Even when we are beaten by the storm and scorched by the sun, we still keep high spirits. We have a hundred percent confidence in you, and we have a hundred percent confidence in your sons and daughters.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, from the very beginning you said that the grandfather takes better care of the children than the father. As your grandchildren, we feel confident of your care for us. Sometimes we even think that you are spoiling us with affection. It almost seems that your voice and your face appear to us from time to time when we are talking about you, as if we have always been together and never been separated.

Because of the love that your sons have given us, we feel our own love for you increasing day by day, and we feel you entering into our lives more and more. You give us wonderful guidance, and we feel greatly fortunate. We feel blissful and fortunate in our hearts to have such a great, wise, kind, and solemn grandfather.

We will work hard so that more people can share in this great fortune. We will work hard to bring everyone in this world to you. In this way, we will try to pay our debt to you, but we know that we can never repay the love you have given us.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, through the love that we have received from your sons, we can feel an intense love for you growing within us, and through the loving care of your sons, we feel your constant care for us. By the passing down of your instructions through your sons, we have been awakened from ignorance.

Although this is a barren land full of ignorance and darkness, there are blooming flowers, delicious *prasādam,* white and orange *dhotīs* and colorful saris dancing, *karatālas* ringing, lively *mṛdaṅgas,* rich harmony, the magnificent sound of conch shells, the sound of *japa,* and sweet happy *kīrtana*. Here we have the mercy of Māyāpur, the fragrance of Vṛndāvana, the sea breeze of Jagannātha Purī, the history of Mathurā, and the opulence of Dvārakā. We have beautiful Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and we have handsome Gaura-Nitāi. We have Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara. Deep in our hearts, we know that all of this comes from your concern for us.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can never leave you. We will never leave you. We love you unlimitedly.

*Your grandsons and granddaughters in New Vraja, Khasadeśa.*

## New Vrindavan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We sing the *Gurv-aṣṭaka* every morning before the Deities. While meditating on these verses, let us meditate on your life and how it epitomizes the essence and the meaning of this wonderful prayer.

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka .*

The Kali-yuga fires of material existence are blazing throughout the world. You, as *jagat-guru,* are our link to the ocean of mercy. Just as Lord Śiva received the waters of the Ganges on his head and allows it to flow to this earth for the purification of the conditioned souls, so you received the eternal message from the ocean of mercy, and then translated and distributed that message to relieve the suffering. You continually circled the earth, pouring the soothing waters of Kṛṣṇa’s words over countries, continents, hemispheres, and the entire earth. Just like those massive cloud formations sweeping around the globe, your efforts encircle the world, attempting to quell the blazing fire of material existence.

*mahāprabhoḥ kīrtana-nṛtya-gīta .*

You brought the eternal *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya out of the villages of India and onto the world stage. With faith in the holy name, Lord Caitanya’s mission, and your guru’s instruction, you began chanting, dancing, and feasting, not in Vṛndāvana, not in Navadvīpa, but in Tompkins Square Park, London, Nigeria, and all over the world. You exhibited the ecstasy of pure love of God, but your ecstatic symptoms usually appeared somewhat different than hair standing on end, quivering of the body, or flowing tears. Your ecstatic symptoms were manifest by transcending your age, traveling the globe fourteen times, endlessly counseling thousands of disciples, translating many hours a day in your late 70’s, erecting temples throughout the world, and defeating the mundane philosophies of the modern man. These were your ecstatic symptoms as you tirelessly pushed on the movement of Lord Caitanya, not enjoying Him but serving Him.

*śrī-vigrahārādhana-nitya-nānā .*

While you are our lionlike master, you humbly presented yourself before Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa as you installed Deities throughout the world. Your invitation to Kṛṣṇa to enter your ISKCON temples transformed rundown buildings into Vaikuṇṭha. You personally showed us the principles of Deity worship, the care and attention required, the devotion needed, to allow the Supreme Lord to manifest in your institution. Kṛṣṇa was compelled to reciprocate and manifested His most beautiful forms throughout Hare Kṛṣṇa temples. The daily worship you established was the guiding hope for creating piety in the visiting pilgrims. Kṛṣṇa is all-attractive, and you showed us how to act as the brahminical janitors for His *arcā-vigraha* performance.

*catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda …*

How could we have known the Vaikuṇṭha menu without your epicurean demonstrations? You are the gourmet guru of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. Whatever people may say about us, whatever the world may think, your *prasādam* and the magnanimous distribution of that glorious *prasādam* are universally praised and appreciated. As we struggle to explain the philosophy and persuade people to give up sinful activities, while we work hard to maintain the temples so that anyone can worship Kṛṣṇa, while we battle to maintain spiritual standards and establish brahminical principles, when all these efforts seem weak or failing, there is always the higher taste of *mahā-prasādam,* our secret weapon to demonstrate that giving up sinful life is pleasurable. And we, as your children, take great comfort in knowing that when you watch us honor *bhāgavata-prasādam,* you are satisfied.

*śrī-rādhikā-mādhavayor apāra .*

In recent history, Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes, qualities, names, and forms were esoteric and stayed within the borders of India. Relishing those pastimes, you were compelled and consumed with the eagerness and enthusiasm to distribute those pastimes to the world, to the entire universe. Early in the history of your movement, you shared your ecstasy and translated the Tenth Canto. Revealing the secrets of Rādhikā and Mādhava by the mercy and at the request of Lord Caitanya, you established both the means to and the end of *kṛṣṇa-līlā*.

*nikuñja-yūno rati-keli-siddhyai .*

You have invited us, your disciples from around the world, en masse, into the groves of Vṛndāvana. You trooped us to Govardhana Hill and Rādhā-kuṇḍa to honor Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal love affairs. You demonstrated what being expert in assisting the *gopīs* means. We never once saw you try to enjoy Kṛṣṇa. Nowhere in your books can we find any hint of how to enjoy Kṛṣṇa or Kṛṣṇa’s affairs with Rādhārāṇī and the *gopīs*. You showed us the ecstasy in serving Kṛṣṇa: service, service, service, *dāsa-dāsa-anudāsa*. The *gopīs* provide pleasure for Kṛṣṇa, and Kṛṣṇa derives no greater pleasure than watching you sacrifice to save the fallen conditioned souls from material existence. You marked the path to the groves of Vṛndāvana.

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair .*

You showed the way of the *ācārya*: *ahaitukī apratihatā*—unmotivated, uninterrupted confidential service. Thus it is our duty to honor you as much as the Supreme Lord. You have acted as a loving father toward us, a caring teacher, our dearmost friend. Please forgive us for treating you with familiarity. Childish disciples that we are, we forget that you are the representative of Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa. We made a clumsy attempt to build you a glorious residence. We hoped it would be a fitting place for you, the Lord’s representative, to reside. We called it a palace, but our feeble effort seems inadequate. We pray that you find some devotion in our *śūdra*-like attempt to build a memorial for the bona fide representative of Śrī Hari.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo .*

You have taught us about Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, Lord Caitanya’s benedictions, the six Gosvāmīs, the line of disciplic succession, but at the end of the day all that we have is our service to you. You are Kṛṣṇa’s manifested mercy. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not a procedure, it is not mechanical. Only through devotion to you will we get the understanding needed to return to Kṛṣṇa. You gave your life to save the world. You gave your life to save us. There is no greater path to receiving Kṛṣṇa’s mercy than trying to repay our debt to you. Without your grace, our activity is external.

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura asks us to meditate on these prayers every morning. So too should we meditate on you, the epitome of the bona fide spiritual master, the standard by which all other gurus may be judged.

*Your servants at New Vrindavan, West Virginia, USA.*

## New York (Brooklyn)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

*rādhā-sammukha-saṁsaktiṁ sakhī-saṅga-nivāsinīm*  
*tvām ahaṁ satataṁ vande mādhavāśraya-vigrahām*

**O spiritual master, I continually offer my obeisances unto you, who are always in the presence of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and very much devoted to Her. You always reside in the association of Her confidantes, the** ***gopīs,*** **and you are the abode of loving devotion to Kṛṣṇa.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the first verse of his “Manaḥ Śikṣā” Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura states:

*nitāi-pada-kamala, koṭi-candra-suśītala*  
*je chāyāy jagata jurāy*

**“The lotus feet of Lord Nityānanda are a shelter where one will get the soothing moonlight not only of one but of millions of moons.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the pure via medium of Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu’s soothing mercy. The true proof of your mercy’s being identical to Nityānanda Prabhu’s is the grand success of your preaching. Lord Nityānanda is famous everywhere as Patita Pāvana, the deliverer of the most fallen. By the desire of Lord Nityānanda, Your Divine Grace was directed to a vast field of most fallen conditioned *jīvas* to dispense freely and most effectively the mercy and love of God, especially Lord Nityānanda’s mercy.

In reference to your miraculous achievement of delivering thousands of the most fallen Jagāis and Mādhāis, you said:

I was not very much hopeful. I wrote one poetry, that “Kṛṣṇa, why You have brought me in this country? What can I do? How I shall convince them how they will understand the philosophy? So, but because You have brought me here, must be there is some purpose. So all right. You make me dance as You like.” That poetry, I \[wrote\] in Boston, Commonwealth Pier, on the sea. I came by ship. So I wrote that poetry, that I do not know what for I have come here, why Kṛṣṇa has brought me here. As soon as I shall say that there is no illicit sex, no meat-eating, no drinking, and no gambling, they’ll say, “You go home. Don’t talk.” I knew this. Still I attempted. But these boys kindly accepted. I never made any compromise. I said, “These are the first conditions to become Kṛṣṇa conscious: no illicit sex, no meat-eating, no intoxication, no gambling. Are you agreed?” They say yes, then come. If I would have made compromise, “Yes, whatever you like you can do.” No, I never did. Ask them. \[Interview with Professors O’Connell, Motilal, and Shivaram, 18 June 1976, Toronto, Canada\]

The act of transforming so many fallen conditioned souls—candidates qualified by the disqualification of addiction to mundane action—into recipients of Lord Nityānanda’s mercy, so expertly delivered by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, was performed to bless the unfortunate, suffering conditioned souls with the most blessed gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, love of Kṛṣṇa. This act of mercy is nothing short of a transcendental miracle, proof of your empowerment by Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu’s potency.

Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura continues in the first verse of “Manaḥ Śikṣā”:

*heno nitāi bine bhāi, rādhā-kṛṣṇa pāite nāi*  
*dṛḍha kori’ dharo nitāir pāy*

**“If the world wants to have real peace, it should take shelter of Lord Nityānanda. Unless one takes shelter under the shade of the lotus feet of Lord Nityānanda, it will be very difficult for him to approach Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. If one actually wants to enter into the dancing party of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, he must firmly catch hold of the lotus feet of Lord Nityānanda.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are famous throughout the universe, among the demigods and the pure devotee *ācāryas* in the chain of disciplic succession, for establishing the worship of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nityānanda and the all-attractive Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, especially throughout the Western world, where the true moonlight of Lord Nityānanda’s mercy had not illumined and soothed the dark stonelike hearts of the unfortunate, unqualified, suffering conditioned souls. This is the most fortunate purpose of your preaching. This is the most fortunate benediction you delivered to the suffering conditioned souls. By delivering Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu’s mercy, you have transformed the most unqualified residents of the gutters and sewers of material existence into the most qualified menial loving servants of Lord Kṛṣṇa and His confidential associates.

In the beautiful *Gurv-aṣṭakam* prayers, which define your glorious position and potency, it is stated:

*nikuñja-yūno rati-keli-siddhyai*  
*yā yālibhir yuktir apekṣaṇīyā*  
*tatrāti-dākṣyād ati-vallabhasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is very dear because he is expert in assisting the** ***gopīs,*** **who at different times make different tasteful arrangements for the perfection of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal loving affairs within the groves of Vṛndāvana. I offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day of Vyāsa-pūjā, we pray that you will please continue to bless us with the soothing mercy of Lord Nityānanda. Please don’t neglect us, your aspiring and insignificant menial servants. Even if we remain unqualified, we beg you to consider us most qualified to receive your wonderful mercy, which truly is as soothing as the moonlight of millions of moons. We pray to remain your menial servants birth after birth.

*aṁśo bhagavato ’smy ahaṁ sadā dāso ’smi sarvathā*  
*tat-kṛpāpekṣako nityaṁ tat-preṣṭha-sāt karomi svam*

**“I am a tiny part and parcel of the Supreme Lord, and I am always and in every way His eternal servant. As I am always hoping for His mercy and kindness, I offer myself to His most dear servitor, my spiritual master.”**

*Śrīla Prabhupāda’s insignificant, unfortunate, and unqualified children at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandira, Brooklyn, New York, New York, USA.*  
(written by Rāmabhadra dāsa)

## New York (26 Second Avenue)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you!

We offer you this wonderful Upaniṣadic mantra:

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as much as we are devoted to the Supreme Lord, so we must be devoted to you. And due to this devotion you will reveal to us the purports of devotional service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as part of your glories we mention some of the qualities of 26 Second Avenue, the small temple in New York City where you began ISKCON. Your growing and vital group there continues to be inspired by your presence. We are bonded together by service to you, deeply committed to glorious New York preaching. We pray to keep wastefulness, immaturity, and irresponsibility in check, replacing these disservices with maturity, professionalism, concern, and care.

We are excited that this past year you have sent us many new devotees to serve you. Our *brahmacārīs* regularly preach in several colleges, perform *saṅkīrtana* on the streets, and give daily study classes in the temple. The householders assist the *brahmacārīs,* arrange for festivals, and maintain the finances. We also host many *sannyāsīs* and senior devotees, who enliven us. Nevertheless, we sense that our community is in its beginning stages; we are headed for much more.

We feel your blessings in the presence of your disciples, grand-disciples, and other followers. We experience the ways in which your devotees serve you and develop affection for you, and we become enveloped with escalating love for you. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us such sweetness by the association of your pure, intelligent, affectionate devotees that we are greatly indebted to you, and we desire to serve you more ambitiously. Yet we will progress in measured steps in order that we may sustain a lasting, artful, and valuable service.

In spite of all you have given us, we sometimes feel unhappy in our New York lives, thinking to run after you and cry, *āmi to’ kāṅgala, ‘kṛṣṇa’ ‘kṛṣṇa’ boli’, dhāi tava pāche pāche*. Prabhupāda, we are your lowly servants; take us with you, take us with you. Yet, association with your devotees at 26 Second Avenue sends our miseries far away and again makes us happy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for providing us with the most delightful and rewarding service imaginable in the most difficult of places, New York City. Thank you even more for sending the most loving association in a city that is cold and unaffectionate.

We pray that you empower us to spread your glories and those of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa in the city that is the seat of world materialism. By your mercy we will connect this city of many zeros to the one Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servants at 26 Second Avenue, the Matchless Gifts Temple, New York, New York, USA.*

## Nizhniy Tagil

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

It is not easy for us to comprehend how great you are, what a special personality you are. We can only guess. But one thing is quite obvious: it was you who brought the torch of this transcendental knowledge of Kṛṣṇa to the West, where people in general had no interest in it; it was you who gave us shelter in your worldwide Society for Kṛṣṇa consciousness; it was you who delivered the pure nectar of *bhakti* to the thirsty contaminated hearts of many, many thousands of Westerners; it was you by whose mercy we were saved. Thank you, dear Prabhupāda, many, many times.

Without you, where were we destined to rot? What kind of ignorant activity were we destined to be performing now had we not gotten your mercy? But in order to spread the greatest mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, you sacrificed your life for us and many other devotees in the past, in the present, and in the future. Therefore you are our eternal guru and we are your eternal debtors. How can we repay this debt? Is it possible at all?!

We would like to help you in spreading your mission in our area. But what can we do ourselves? Even in these efforts we need your mercy. By ourselves we are completely helpless and useless. Our stubborn minds do not want to accept the elevated truths clearly explained in your unique books; they continue to cling to the illusion of sense gratification. Our minds still do not want to surrender to the process of *bhakti*. But we still hope that one day we shall get the proper qualification and shall become useful members of your movement. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to engage us; please continue to show us the way out of ignorance; please continue to lead us toward the highest goal.

Again and again we offer our grateful obeisances before you.

*Your servants at ISKCON Nizhniy Tagil, Russia.*

## Nueva Goloka Vṛndāvana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga!

All glories to ISKCON, your vehicle for expanding Lord Caitanya’s movement in many countries, thus saving all fallen souls like ourselves. When a person receives the seed of devotional service, he should take care of it in his heart and water it daily. But for Kali-yuga residents like ourselves that task is not always easy. The scriptures clearly say how many disqualifications we have. But to realize this may take some years, because we are so much in ignorance. The fact is that we struggle every day to practice spiritual life. But the all-merciful Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu has sent you, a fearless *sākṣād-dharitvena* soul directly representing Kṛṣṇa, to give us that mercy despite our disqualifications.

Your freely distributed mercy is keeping us alive even though we have had difficult times in our *yātrā*. Only you and your faithful followers can give us righteous instruction for our spiritual progress, so that we will be able to continue assisting you in fulfilling Lord Caitanya’s mission. Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!! We are trying to maintain regular Deity worship and regular temple programs in the service of beautiful Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gaurasundar.

All glories to you, Śrīla A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda! And all glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

*Your lowly servants at the Nueva Goloka Vṛndāvana farm community in Costa Rica.*

## Osaka

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have taught us how to worship the Deities. I have been worshiping *śālagrāma-śilā* by the mercy of my spiritual master. Even a fallen soul like me can taste the nectar of *pūjā* by your mercy. You have given us your mercy so that devotees can be purified. Even though I have no direct contact with your *vapu* form, I get your mercy through my spiritual master. Your instructions are passed down to us through disciplic succession. It is almost a miracle that I am getting knowledge of Lord Kṛṣṇa, for I was in the darkness of ignorance. In this Age of Kali, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is you who have shown us the way to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Recently a guest who first visited the center in Osaka three years ago came back to us. He has been vegetarian for these three years and never forgot what he learned three years ago. He was away from devotees but never gave up Kṛṣṇa consciousness because he was enlivened to keep going when he knew all the devotees in ISKCON were trying sincerely. I told him to study the *Bhagavad-gītā* and offer his food to the Lord. I realized that once you have learned anything about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, some day the devotional credit will fructify for sure.

We will keep going on for the pleasure of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the pleasure of our spiritual master, and for our own spiritual pleasure.

*Your insignificant servants in Osaka, Japan.*  
(written by Satsvarūpa dāsa)

## Palankaraya

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this most auspicious occasion of your appearance day, let us offer a small homage to your glories.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are insignificant fallen souls. It is only because of your causeless mercy that we have been given a chance to practice devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa—chanting His holy name, singing and dancing, honoring *prasādam,* and so on. Under the guidance of your bona fide representatives, we are now helping establish farm projects and *āśramas* here in central Kalimantan province. We really have no power to develop such projects, which you desired, yet by the mercy of Lord Kṛṣṇa things are gradually progressing. We acquired a few hectares of land, where we built a simple *āśrama* with a temple room, a building for *gṛhasthas,* a *gośālā,* and a kitchen. By your mercy we now have three cows and a bull. We hope that the bull will help us cultivate the land and develop our project toward self-sufficiency.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we realize that we have no qualification for hearing and chanting the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa or developing the many projects you desired. We are sound asleep in the lap of Māyā. But you are so merciful that you have sent your disciples to open our eyes from that sleep, lift us out of the darkness of ignorance, and guide us so that now we have a light in our life and can realize the nectar of devotional service unto Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Please be merciful to us. Please help us to always engage in devotional service. Without your mercy, we can do nothing.

Trying to become your humble servants,  
*Your devotees in Dhruva Ashram, in Palankaraya, Central Kalimantan Province, Indonesia.*

## Paris

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this auspicious day of your appearance in this world, please accept our most humble obeisances from all of us—disciples, admirers, followers, grand-disciples, in short, all the moving and nonmoving living entities from the Paris temple who have already surrendered unto your lotus feet or who will do so in the near future. The Paris temple is an ocean of peace, love, and devotion, where all the problems are getting solved because here, imperceptibly, people are finishing up their karma in this material world. All we need to perceive this reality is to have transcendental vision.

Twenty-nine years ago you mercifully installed Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Parisīśvara for the purification and good fortune of all living entities. You once said, “My temples are the embassies of Vaikuṇṭha, the spiritual world in this material nature, a place where there is no anxiety.” Your temples are also the embassies of the Vedic culture all over the world, because even India is Hindustan, the place of the Hindus, but here, people of all creeds, classes, colors, religions, and nationalities can associate and learn, by your grace, how to serve and worship the almighty Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. This is your kindness!

Their most beautiful Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Gaura-Nitāi are flooding us with Their most charming smiles. They love us, and They are particularly enchanted by the expertise and extreme competence of Their ambassador—you, Śrīla Prabhupāda—who work wonders, changing fallen *jīvas* into bona fide Vaiṣṇavas. What a great service you are rendering to Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Even we, the devotees living in your temples, poor *mlecchas* a short while ago, have become, by your mercy, the residents of the spiritual world, wearing the official spiritual-world outfits of *dhotīs* and saris. The *śikhās* of your male followers are, as you said, the flags of Goloka Vṛndāvana. Many people come here to obtain visas and all sorts of official papers. We deliver them the official documents they are looking for in the form of your books, *japa-mālās, prasādam,* transcendental music, and other paraphernalia by which they will be able to reach the spiritual world.

Poor people in the desert of materialism become rejuvenated and enlightened when they enter the spiritual realm of this temple, just as a fellow lost in the desert might receive water from the hands of a most merciful well-wishing friend. Simply because this temple exists, people all around the world are becoming very fortunate. We have a *śālagrāma-śilā* here, and we have heard it said that those who die within two kilometers of a place where a *śālagrāma-śilā* is being worshiped go to Vaikuṇṭha after death so fortunate!

This Paris temple is only a tiny part of your achievements!

So many of your temples have been established all over the world, so many books have been and are being distributed, and so many devotees are joining! Kali is indeed very afraid because of the presence of the *senapati-bhakta,* the commander in chief of Kṛṣṇa’s armies.

Indeed, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the true hero of this age! May we eternally surrender unto you.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and all glories to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa!

*Your humble servants in Paris, France.*  
(written by Rāja Dharma dāsa)

## Pecs

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Soon we will build a beautiful Vedic temple in our town. Thank you for giving us the chance to show your divine glories to the people in this sinful town.

We know that it is impossible for us to ever repay the debt we owe you for saving us from the darkness of hellish material, conditioned life. We often try to think of new ways to carry on the preaching you have started with the help of your disciples. We would like to do something big to influence a lot of people, even if it is in a small place in Hungary. If we cannot do much ourselves, at least we should try to lay down the foundation for the next generation. We find that we do not have the courage, skill, or any other qualities needed to do something big, as you did. So we just try to work hard and struggle to sacrifice our lives for your mission. In this way we try to do as much as we can to take part in your transcendental pastimes.

Please give us your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and give us the strength to serve and please the Vaiṣṇavas and Kṛṣṇa by our effort.

*Your insignificant servants in Pecs, Hungary.*  
(written by Braja Ballabhī Ballabha dāsa)

## Perth

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

On this holy day of your appearance, we, your followers here in Perth, Australia, would like to attempt to thank you for all the kindness you are constantly bestowing upon us. We pray to rededicate ourselves with more enthusiasm to serving your mission.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us, along with so many other lost souls, the opportunity to engage in the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In this material world everyone is lost, distressed, and suffering—futilely searching for happiness through so many useless and temporary endeavors. Through your transcendental books you are giving us the greatest gift. You are teaching us our real identity—that we are spirit souls, eternally the loving servants of Lord Kṛṣṇa—and that the only means for attaining genuine deep happiness is to engage wholeheartedly in the Lord’s service, beginning with hearing and chanting His holy names. Without your mercy and compassion, we too would be living shallow, futile, meaningless lives. We can never truly repay your kindness, so please accept us as your servants and as instruments in your mission.

Here in Perth we’re trying to follow your instructions to the best of our ability by looking for ways to increase the *saṅkīrtana*. Last year (2001) was wonderful, as for the first time we managed to hold a large Ratha-yātrā for the pleasure of Their Lordships Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā. Actually, by your mercy we managed to hold two separate festivals, which gave so many people the opportunity to take *darśana* of the Lord, to hear His holy names, and to honor His *prasādam*. Here in Australia people love festivals, especially when there is profuse distribution of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam,* and by your mercy the opportunities to distribute *prasādam* are constantly increasing. Lord Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam* is becoming quite famous and very popular!

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this holy day of your appearance we humbly pray that you may continue to bestow your mercy on each and every one of us, and that we may become worthy and grateful recipients to receive it. We pray to become your simple and pure-hearted followers, for without becoming qualified ourselves, how can we possibly give this knowledge to others?

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to your service!

*Your servants in Perth, Australia.*

## Rāḍhadesh

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The Rāḍhadesh-vāsīs feel honored and humbled to offer you prostrated obeisances on this most auspicious 106th anniversary of your appearance in this dark world.

Although you never visited Rāḍhadesh during your manifest pastimes, this community came into existence, and continues to exist, only because of you and your representatives. Once, one of your disciples saw the castle’s great potential as a facility to spread your mission. He consulted with you about it, and so eventually, under your guidance, it was purchased. Since that time this community of devotees has grown, fluctuated, weathered numerous storms—in short, gone through many changes. Still, there are several stalwart devoted followers of yours who have accepted Rāḍhadesh as their home and the vehicle for their practical service to you. Because of their dedication, Rāḍhadesh is not only surviving but thriving and evolving into a refuge for resident and visiting Vaiṣṇavas alike.

This year we are celebrating the twenty-fifth anniversary of the installation of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha and Lalitā-Viśākhā-devīs. Also, the Bhaktivedanta Library is being inaugurated, and the pilot year of the Bhaktivedanta Vaiṣṇava College is to begin in September. Soon the Guest House will comfortably accommodate many devotees and guests so that they can take advantage of the devotional programs, to begin or further nourish their practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Although our neighbors combined to publicize their objections to Rāḍhadesh’s expansion, we continued fearlessly, confident in your statement that any publicity about Lord Kṛṣṇa and His devotees will bring about favorable results. Now more local people visit the castle, boutique, and restaurant, and new openings in communication with the village authorities are developing. These are some of the highlights of the range of projects being developed here as services to your International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Many of your ISKCON leaders, who regularly grace Rāḍhadesh with their presence, heartily appreciate and encourage our endeavors, and although we are well aware of our shortcomings, we accept their blessings as indications of your satisfaction.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bestowing upon us such a sublime synthesis of philosophical ideology and its practical application. You perfectly present and represent Lord Kṛṣṇa’s instructions in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* as well as those of all the previous *ācāryas,* who have labored tirelessly to attract us to the real shelter and happiness of devotional service. May we continue to steadily serve your mission in the company of your faithful followers and thus make our human lives valuable at last. May all the endeavors of the Rāḍhadesh-vāsīs culminate in the blossoming of our pure attraction to the spotless glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s name, fame, form, qualities, and pastimes. We understand that this is the offering that satisfies you the most.

*Your aspiring servants in Rāḍhadesh Dhāma, Belgium.*

## Rio de Janeiro

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble and respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to you!

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, our hearts melt as we meditate on the compassionate nature of your activities for the welfare of suffering humanity. Perfect is your vision of how the world might be transformed through Kṛṣṇa consciousness, how people in general might finally become happy by putting Lord Kṛṣṇa at the center of their activities. Why is it perfect? Because you scientifically explained how to reorganize human society through a change in human beings’ consciousness. Your proposal was not that everybody should go back to dwelling in caves, but rather that everything manufactured by man should be positively used for the spiritual improvement of mankind instead its destruction. This is the specific reason why this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is destined to meet success.

We pray that we may deeply understand your divine instructions, which are so important and beneficial for us, as well as for the general mass of people. Our debt to you is eternal. The least we can do to begin to pay you back for all you have done for us is to seriously work so that the population of this sensual city of Rio de Janeiro may come to understand your transcendental message, take to the practice of chanting the *mahā-mantra,* and gradually make their lives perfect and happy.

*Your humble servants in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil.*

## St. Louis

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, without you there would be no holy name. Without you there would be no transcendental books. Without you there would be no temples.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, without our heads at your lotus feet there would be no hope.

*Your servants at ISKCON St. Louis, Missouri, USA.*  
(written by Mayeśa dāsa)

## Sao Paulo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances again and again. All glories to you!

On behalf of the São Paulo mandir we are writing this Vyāsa-pūjā offering. As strange as it may seem, we have personally never written Your Divine Grace an offering, nor have most of my Godbrothers now residing at the São Paulo mandir. We thus hereby first of all beg you to forgive our lack of devotion and gratitude; please look upon us with benevolent eyes, taking into consideration that most of us are not your direct disciples.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, a feeling of immense gratitude overwhelms us as we reflect on Your Divine Grace’s merciful and incalculable contribution to our lives. Your Divine Grace never came physically to our country, yet you are so present in your books, in your recorded lectures, in the preaching work of your followers, and in the love your sons and grandsons have for you.

As we write we are sitting before the always grave manifestation of Your Divine Grace—your *mūrti* in the temple. This is the only tangible physical form of yours that most of us have ever came in contact with. As I meditate upon this grave form of yours, I can’t stop from thinking, in the background, of your immense smile, a smile so potent and intense that it fills up the whole universe with the most tender transcendental feelings, warming and nourishing our barren hearts.

On this day when we are mostly grateful for your compassionate and heartfelt appearance in this world of ignorance and darkness, we can simply thank Your Divine Grace millions and millions of times.

We must say we feel completely unworthy and incapable of adequately praising Your Divine Grace, the divine disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura who has come to liberate the Western countries from the dangerous sharks and crocodiles of the material ocean of existence, such as voidism and impersonalism.

How blessed is that joyful day of Your Divine’s Grace appearance!!

Through your benediction we were, in spite of our lives of endless sin, able to know of your divine love for the Divine Couple, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. In this way Your Divine Grace has bestowed upon us the greatest of treasures, one that would otherwise be absolutely inaccessible to us: the knowledge of unconditional and uninterrupted pure devotional love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Yet we are still so immersed in the dense layers of ignorance that we stubbornly refuse to let go and allow ourselves to be washed away by the merciful rain shower of the Godhead’s servant’s *vijñāna* and drowned in the ocean of *premānanda* that you have come to guide us to.

Please bless our generation and future generations so that we and they may adequately represent the depth of the teachings Your Divine Grace has brought us. And allow us to always stand beneath the shade of the greatness of your love and of your lotus feet.

Jaya Jagat-Guru Patita-Pāvana Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Abhay Charanaravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda Kī Jaya!

Please accept our most humble obeisances once more.

*The simple servants of your humble servants at the Sao Paulo Mandir, Brazil.*

## Scotland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your ISKCON! All glories to your transcendental book distribution!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, throughout your time on this planet you worked so hard to translate and teach the science of self-realization as taught in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the *Bhagavad-gītā,* and all supplementary literatures. You left a vast library of knowledge, a wealth of direction and instruction for all candidates in spiritual life to follow. The spiritual master lives in his instruction, and the follower lives with him; therefore we are ever blessed with the chance to take your transcendental association through the pages of your books. Your purports are your ecstasies and our direction and beginnings in spiritual life. Everything necessary for perfecting this human form of life is given by your kindness and endeavor. Selflessly and compassionately you took upon yourself the order of your Guru Mahārāja to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the English language, and the effects have been truly amazing.

The culture of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is now taking hold all over the world, among people of every creed, color, and background. Rich, poor, black, white, educated, uneducated, male, female—all are being given the opportunity to learn the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. How satisfying it must be to see the ocean of love of God spread to the shores and lands of all places of the world! The associates of Lord Caitanya used to shout and dance in ecstasy upon hearing someone even unconsciously chant the holy name of the Lord, for they knew that this was the beginning of the end of their material life. With your understanding and purity of vision, you desired that everyone get that opportunity to end their material encagement, and thus you started this preaching movement to reach every town and village of the world. You are always pushing this preaching mood—“Double it!” “Triple it!”—more and more until, as much as humanly possible, our every ounce of energy is given to the task of uplifting the fallen souls. Once the fallen are uplifted, you take them onboard, fix them up, inspire them with your instruction, and enlist them in the preaching mission.

We are trying to take up that mood and make spreading your mission our first and foremost ambition. We would love to see Your Divine Grace happy and pleased with our endeavors to please you. Pleasing you is perfection itself.

Spreading the holy name around, seeing people chant and feel relieved, distributing your books, and watching as fallen souls become amazed with the clarity of your direction, we are enlivened to carry on day by day. By your kindness, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are engaged and connected to the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu, and if we continue on with this sacrifice, our otherwise useless lives will become perfect. You are touching the hearts of people all over the planet, and we are happy to be at your service if you are pleased to engage us. Thank you for the opportunity, because it is only by your mercy that we are here endeavoring to reach life’s ultimate goal of being re-united with our loving Lord in His devotional service.

We are hoping that in the years to come we will become more capable instruments in your lotus hands and that you will bless us with the ability to execute your order more perfectly. How wonderful it would be to hear Your Divine Grace say, “I am pleased with the endeavors of my children and grandchildren in Scotland, and may they receive the full blessings of Lord Caitanya so that they may be inspired to preach more and more.” We would regard this as perfection itself. We want to do what is right to please you, and to know that you are smiling in ecstasy would be all the inspiration we need to go mad and work harder and harder to spread the holy name and the glories of Your Divine Grace.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all that you have given us. We pray that you are pleased with us, and we desire your service more and more.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your insignificant servants in Scotland.*

## Seattle

**Just Let Me Walk with Prabhupāda**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O Vāsudeva, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, O Supreme God,  
I just want to be like Your servant Prabhupāda.  
He is the only one I know who truly loves me.  
He is the only one I know who shows me ecstasy.  
He is the only one I know who could love such a fallen soul.

Therefore, O Supreme God, just make me like Your servant Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
He is the only one I know who can look into my heart.  
He is the only one I know who has this special art  
of making me feel I have something to give,  
of making me feel I have a reason to live.

Therefore, to You I pray, my dear Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
make me dance just like Your servant Prabhupāda.  
Please make me engage in each soul’s only business,  
unalloyed, undivided service to You, O Supreme God.

Only because of Prabhupāda  
this wretch prays to be blessed.  
Only because of Prabhupāda  
I live on through my tests.

O Lord of the *gopīs,* only because of Prabhupāda  
I know Your greatest devotees,  
and to his feet I sob, with tears of intense bliss,  
feeling his association, I cry and cry,  
feeling his sacred station,  
his most sacred station of love for every soul,  
because he fully loves You, O Lord in control.

O Vāsudeva Kṛṣṇa, with Your inconceivable will,  
please take me to Prabhupāda, who makes me feel this thrill.  
The thrill of union with God’s greatest servant,  
O Vāsudeva Kṛṣṇa, I only pray for this mercy.

Let me walk with Prabhupāda and never leave his side,  
the only soul who loves me so, my only perfect guide.  
Let me walk with Prabhupāda, these tears I cannot hide.  
Let me walk with Prabhupāda, forever by Your side.

O Vāsudeva, O Vāsudeva, please answer this soul’s cry!  
May I be with Prabhupāda and his family on high,  
chanting and dancing in the party of Śrī Śrī Gour-Nitāi.  
Otherwise I shall die.

This Anubhāvānanda dās, by Prabhupāda’s mercy, does lovingly, helplessly cry,  
Just let me walk with Prabhupāda, forever by Your side.  
Never have I seen a person with such charming beauty  
as Your servant Prabhupāda, only doing his duty,  
surrendered soulfully unto You, the Supreme, most beautiful youth.

Let me keep him in my heart, so he may never part,  
only then may I bear this life, inside Your supreme heart,  
where Your dearest servants never ever depart.  
Let me walk with Prabhupāda and never leave his side,  
the only soul who loves me so, my eternal father and guide.  
O souls, just cry to Prabhupāda, and by his orders, do abide.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants in Seattle, Washington, USA.*  
(written by Anubhāvānanda dāsa)

## Silicon Valley

We offer our obeisances to the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is so very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa in this world, having taken complete shelter at His lotus feet.

What else can we offer to our Gurudeva on this Vyāsa-pūjā day other than our sincerest gratitude? Lest we think that mere gratitude is not a sufficient offering, let us examine the nature of that emotion, for the outpouring of gratitude toward the Lord and His devotees is itself the essence of spiritual life.

Indeed, out of gratitude, “Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is very affectionate toward His devotees, sells Himself to a devotee who offers Him merely a Tulasī leaf and a palmful of water.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 3.104) Out of gratitude Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura became extremely pleased with a seemingly unimportant *brahmacārī* who went to Navadvīpa and sold a few copies of his Guru Mahārāja’s paper, worth only a few paise. In her prayers, Queen Kunti exalts those devotees who continuously hear, chant, and repeat Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental activities or those who, out of gratitude, “take pleasure in others’ doing so.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.8.36)

And Śrīla Prabhupāda writes, “There is no sufficient means to repay the Personality of Godhead for His act of benediction; therefore, we can simply feel gratitude and pray to the Lord with folded hands.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.31.18)

Evoking gratitude for our Gurudeva, let us remember that because of our misguided faith in the guardianship of material nature we’ve been repeatedly placed in the wombs of mothers in millions of species of life. Each time there we’ve suffered bitterly and cried out to the Lord for His mercy upon us, promising that we’d surrender. But each time material nature was so powerful that we forgot our prayers upon being pushed out into the world. Seeing the smiling faces of our new parents and overwhelmed by Māyā, we forgot our intention to surrender to the Lord.

Life after life we’ve been frustrated by every imaginable reversal of fortune—losing kingdoms and loved ones to the force of time. We struggled on in ignorance, without a clue that we were suffering “only for want of seeing the charming smile of the Supreme Lord.” Our anguish has been so acute and prolonged that we’ve literally filled oceans with our tears of sorrow. If stacked atop one another, the skeletons and bones of our material bodies, acquired throughout our many lives, would block out the sun’s rays.

It is from this treacherous ocean of suffering that Śrīla Prabhupāda has rescued us and turned our faces toward the brilliant spiritual sky.

A magnanimous astrologer delivered a man from wretched poverty by divulging the location of an inestimable hidden treasure, showing him also how and where to dig it up. In the same way Śrīla Prabhupāda has redeemed us by revealing to us the true wealth of our lives, the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.

The more we realize the value of his gift, the more we will be moved by a profound sense of gratitude. This gratitude will lift our hands to service; it will inspire our tongues to cry out, “*Guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya!*—“Gurudeva, let me hear only the nectarean words from your lotus mouth so that my heart may be purified.” Finally our gratitude will transport us completely outside the realm of our own tiny needs and desires until we finally agree to sweet surrender at our Gurudeva’s lotus feet.

Rejoicing on this day of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance, let us reflect the good fortune he has brought us, becoming “daring and active” in our mission to go on preaching the glories of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa without restriction. *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants at ISKCON of Silicon Valley, California, USA.*  
(written by Vaiśeṣika dāsa)

## Solapur

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet on this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā. All glories to you!

You came to this world for a special and exceptional mission—to deliver the conditioned souls by spreading the glories of the Lord and nothing else. You are still spreading the glories of the Lord through your transcendental and wonderful books. You were talking about the glories of the Lord till your last breath.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura wrote,

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound!  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around!

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, who is considered to be the seventh Gosvāmī, is very reasonable in this matter. Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your books you have always described Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to be the most magnanimous personality. But then that makes you the supermost magnanimous personality. You have spread the *mahā-mantra* to every town and village of the world, fulfilling the desire of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Therefore you are the most merciful devotee of the Lord.

*Your eternal servants at ISKCON Solapur, India.*

## Sydney

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As the years go by, our understanding slowly increases regarding, well, just about everything. Not all equally relevant, to be sure, but little of it altogether useless. Vyāsa-pūjā day, however, is a special day, a day for reflecting upon the best of all things, that which enables us to claim ourselves, our thoughts, and our ideas as being not altogether without value. Today we reflect upon that which is above all else in meaning, relevance, and importance. That, without which everything else is rendered futile and, beyond all relativities, useless. What is the one ultimate thing without which nothing else really matters or signifies—or even really exists—and which, when added, gives life and purpose to even the meanest and the most inconsequential? For us the best of all things is Śrīla Prabhupāda, the spiritual master of all other masters, and the single principle by which, and from which, all other things draw meaning. He and his mercy are the best of all things.

Mercy may be a matter of general principle, a kind of encompassing, pervasive benevolence. But it can also be something very specific, very personal, very intimate. In the greater world of ISKCON and beyond, many, many living entities are even now receiving, knowingly or unknowingly, that mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda. For us, however, as Śrīla Prabhupāda’s personal sons and daughters, grandchildren, and, soon, great-grandchildren, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy assumes a more specific form, a more solid shape, something altogether more appreciable and tangible. It manifests in the unique and very personal nature of our own service relationship with him. That relationship, just like a person, takes birth, grows, and gradually matures into full expression, ultimately to be relished—eternally, endlessly satisfying, ever sweeter, ever more complete.

By definition everything depends upon something else—is thereby defined by it. Only in this way does any one thing have any meaning. The conditioned soul’s delusion of independence obscures, however, that which in truth defines him, effectively voiding his very existence. In such a futile state he becomes drowned, eclipsed, helpless, hopeless—any fitful lapse into vague awareness ushering in only bitter agonies of discontent. Then, as it is said, for reasons that reason knows not why, enters the guru: in our case, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Catching us up, firmly holding our reluctant hand, he drags us to the shore of solid truth, and there, sitting us down, retrains and re-establishes us in the nature of the eternal and the real. “Established thus, one never departs from the truth, and upon gaining this he thinks there is no greater gain.”

The spiritual master effectively forges our link with reality out of the very substance of his mercy. Through it we are connected to that which has actual meaning, that which, in truth, really exists. It is only through our connection with him that we ourselves take on meaning. Without him we do not really exist. We simply remain lost—in a world that itself is lost, devoid of either rhyme or reason. He, however, breathes life and meaning into our existence—and thus into everything that exists with us. As much as he is real, through our relationship with him, we then also become real.

What is the best thing we know? The best thing is Śrīla Prabhupāda. For by his mercy everything has been made good. That mercy then must be the best of all things. By its influence Śrīla Prabhupāda came, by its influence we were able to receive him. What more can be said? All that is left is to try and appreciate, glorify, and always remember that special grace, to pray for it, to pray to it, catch it up, treasure it, to understand it—ultimately to distribute it. By its influence everything is accomplished, restored, made whole, rendered sweet.

So, we are confident that as the years go by our understanding slowly does increase regarding, well, just about everything. Not all equally relevant, to be sure, but little of it altogether useless. On this particular day however, Vyāsa-pūjā, let us reflect only upon the very source and substance of that confidence, let us reflect only upon the very best of all things: Śrīla Prabhupāda and his mercy. For upon this surely all other things depend. Therefore we must concentrate ourselves here. Though reluctant still, by the mercy, again, of this special day, our thoughts are being dragged to this point. Oh, how fortunate are we, here today, on the best of all days, to be able to appreciate the best of all things: Śrīla Prabhupāda and Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy.

*From Śrīla Prabhupāda’s humble servants of ISKCON Sydney, Australia.*

## Szeged

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances! All glories to your divine lotus feet!

Every year devotees try to praise you with nice words on this very special day, your appearance day. Sincere thoughts, deep self-realizations connected to you—to your deeds, teachings, and the activities of your secret personality—are spoken out.
