But can we ever pay you back for all that you have done for us and for humanity? To try to answer this question, we first have to understand what you have given us. Through your unselfish love you have given us the final goal of life. This love, so rare in this world, is the reason you could attract the hearts of so many impersonalistic young people. You knew what real happiness is. But you never wanted to keep that knowledge for yourself, and thus you let us know about that real happiness. Thus you showed yourself to be a bona fide spiritual master, an ocean of mercy.

When a drowning person is saved, he becomes indebted to his savior eternally. Likewise, you saved us and gave us back our lives. And what could be more precious than life? After all, no one can purchase back even one second of life, no matter how much money he possesses. You have given us not only life, but our true lives.

We cannot thank you for this simply by saying nice words expressing sublime thoughts. What is it worth if we say we love you but do nothing for you? What is the value of it if we say thank you and that is all? We have to enter the battle for the conditioned souls; you are our general and we are your soldiers, and we have to join you in your fight against the evil influences of Kali-yuga. Our only chance is if we help you in your mission to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the entire world. By becoming one with your desire and acting according to that, we could begin to pay back our debt to some extent.

Just as every cave does not contain a ruby, every elephant does not bear a genuine pearl on his head, and every forest does not contain sandalwood trees, so every birth does not afford one the chance to meet a bona fide spiritual master. \[Cāṇakya Paṇḍita\]

The fact that we have met you or that we have become your disciples means that we are very fortunate, though we may not understand how lucky we are.

We humbly ask you for your blessings so we may serve you and your sincere followers. In Hungary, Szeged is a very little town, as little as a grain of dust in the desert of the material world. However, because of your great desire, in even a small place like this live devotees who shine like diamonds in the darkness of nescience.

*Your devotees in Szeged, Hungary.*  
(written by Jayaśrī-devī dāsī)

## Tallinn

Our dearmost master and guide, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet! All glories to you!

We are happy to be once again together at your lotus feet, attempting to speak some words of glorification and gratitude. Thank you for giving us this shelter. As years pass, we start to consider more and more what you actually mean to all of us, your followers. Yet it remains inconceivable that we, the most fallen, have received a most extraordinary chance to attain that which is most exalted. There is no other conclusion we can come to than the one of having received it by your unlimited and causeless compassion.

Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, our foremost predecessor *ācārya,* states in his *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu,* while listing the practices of *sādhana-bhakti*, that the most powerful items are the chanting of the holy name, associating with devotees, hearing the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* worshiping the Deity of the Lord, and residing in a holy place of the Lord’s pastimes. Rūpa Gosvāmī states that these five processes are so potent that even a small attachment for any one of them can arouse devotional ecstasy even in a neophyte. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us all these processes: you gave us the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* you gave us communities where we could come together and hear and chant *kṛṣṇa-kathā,* you translated for us the *amala-purāṇa, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* you introduced us to the practice of serving Śrī-vigraha, and you opened up the *dhāmas* of Śrī Navadvīpa and Śrī Vṛndāvana to us, creating facilities for us to stay there. As we can see, you did all this in an effort to enhance our devotional progress. This is your unprecedented blessing upon the fallen souls in the bewildered societies of Kali-yuga.

One can attempt to perform devotional service for millions of lifetimes, but one’s effort will always remain futile unless one has received the blessings of an empowered spiritual master. Kṛṣṇa is very difficult to attain, but by the mercy and good wishes of someone dear to Him, one can become eligible to approach Him (*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*).

It can thus be ascertained that our qualification for attaining any spiritual standing or advancement lies first of all in being firmly attached to the shelter of your lotus feet. We might not be progressing as quickly as you might want us to, we might not be as successful in our preaching efforts as you might hope, we might not be as broad a shelter for your devotees as you taught us to be, but yet by always remaining loyal to your lotus feet we can still hope to invoke your best wishes and protection. But if we stray from your lotus feet, we will inevitably be lost.

We pray that you will bless us to continuously increase and deepen our realization of our eternal indebtedness to you, and that you will guide us by giving us the intelligence to make our tiny gifts pleasing to you. And most of all, please allow us to always remain in the blissful company of your sincere followers.

*Your most insignificant beggars at ISKCON Tallinn, Estonia.*

## Tampa Bay

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you and your loving service to your spiritual master, Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī.

We are so indebted to you for coming to America and liberating us from birth and death. We had no qualification to meet you, so it was your sheer kindness and compassion upon us that we met you and were able to hear the liberating sound of your voice.

Now, in your service, we are dedicating and rededicating our lives to distributing that divine sound, which liberates all living entities and which you so mercifully showered upon us.

As you were so determined to give the world a chance to go back to Godhead, we are firmly obligated to do the same.

If we neglect the order of the spiritual master, we have no spiritual life, we have no connection with God. As you often pressed upon us, following regulations and worship in the temple is insufficient to please the spiritual master. We must have a deep compassion for all living entities and spend time in our lives helping people be Kṛṣṇa conscious. You showed us the perfect example.

To preach means to go into the material world and tolerate the offenses of others while distributing knowledge. Everyone can do this by giving time, intelligence, and money. You encouraged householders to try to give fifty percent of their income to ISKCON. In one letter you requested all devotees, including administrators such as GBCs and temple presidents, to go out on the street and distribute books for at least one hour every day. This was intended to purify ISKCON of politics, gossip, and the tendency to lose touch with our goal: to distribute knowledge and convert everyone to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

As you pleaded with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to always focus steadily on the goal of distributing knowledge and making devotees, we are struggling to maintain that conviction. From you position in the spiritual world, please give us a special boost so we may move faster in our preaching work.

Māyā imposed many, many obstacles to block your preaching, all of which you miraculously overcame. And, of course, Māyā is still hindering ISKCON. The most destructive Māyā you warned us of was disunity among ourselves. That destruction is caused by the first offense against the holy name, which is to criticize the devotees who have dedicated their lives to propagating the holy name.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you respected everyone’s unique ability to preach in their own way, serving you, and you diffused many, many personal offenses we committed among ourselves in our preaching work. You showed us how we could be unified and change the world.

Now in the same way we can unify all Vaiṣṇavas, all gurus, all devotees by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa all together and distributing the holy name to save the people of Kali-yuga. If we can all work together to achieve this goal, we can powerfully and quickly usher in the golden age of Lord Caitanya within Kali-yuga.

Our fault is that by criticizing one another we are criticizing Vaiṣṇavas who have dedicated their lives to propagating the holy names, and thus we are committing the first offense and nullifying the potency of our chanting.

To be united and change the world, we must appreciate and respect all Vaiṣṇavas and their bona fide preaching work, and we must immediately reject all criticism and faultfinding based on envy. How can we be united in propagating the holy name while we are offending the holy name? We must have friendship and respect and no criticism.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your divine appearance was the dawn of the new Vaikuṇṭha day. We still very much feel your presence in your books, but we need a special intervention to keep ISKCON on the cutting edge of liberating planet Earth.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your servants in Tampa Bay, Florida, USA.*  
(written by Vivasvān dāsa)

## Tauranga

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

First and foremost, let me prostrate myself again and again at your lotus feet in respect and thank you for everything you have done and are continuing to do for us. You came to the West and, giving your pure association, started a firestorm of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in but a few years.

If we tried to walk even a few inches in your shoes, which incidentally none of us could possibly fill, we’d appreciate more the struggles you underwent for all of us. Your implicit faith in Guru and Gaurāṅga enabled you to make your dream into the reality we are all now part of.

Philosopher, motivational teacher, material and spiritual counselor, guide, and friend—you are so many things to thousands of devotees. Unmotivated in your giving Kṛṣṇa, through yourself, to all who are ready and able to take Him and to the degree they / we are able, you are willing to give.

Genuinely you are our ever well-wisher (*suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānāṁ*). Your tolerance knows no bounds, yet your uncompromising presentation of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in all your writings stands along with the commentaries on the *Vedānta-sūtra* by the *pūrva-ācāryas*—The Bhaktivedanta Sūtras.

As anyone who reads your books will know, repeatedly we go over the same purports, yet in them we find an increasing depth in their meaning. Similarly, your lectures, though played hundreds of times, are ever fresh, all the time unfolding as though kept secure with a *samput* (coded encryption) that is released when we’re ready to understand it.

Your teachings are available to one and all irrespective of nationality, race, gender, or any other material impediment. There are no limitations in receiving “the full package” based on any material status such as birth, wealth, or education—none whatsoever.

Speaking for myself, I know that by my coming in contact with your movement my life has dramatically changed for the better. Friends, even relatives, could see the positive changes from hippy to happy. At my birth-father’s funeral (1986) an aunt who had taunted me as a *brahmacārī,* and even stopped my mother, her younger sister, from coming to visit me at Bury Place and Bhaktivedanta Manor (late 1970s), also had a change of heart. She approached and whispered in my ear, “This thing you’re in has made a man of you.” I thought of you, and under my breath said “*Jaya* Prabhupāda!”

Where would we be without you, without everything that you have given us? I gravely dread to think. If you’d thought only of your own situation and stayed in Vṛndāvana, where would we be now? Every devotee has his or her own story of how Lord Kṛṣṇa came into their lives. The stories are precious, packed full of realizations, every single one of them worth hearing. Some have received Kṛṣṇa through direct contact with you, some through “a carrier”—devotees, friends, or even seeming strangers. Looking further, we see His hand reaching out through yours. *Ebe jaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana* aren’t just some words of a nice song; these are your qualities, being sung by the grateful, all of them glorifying you. For all of us then, as now, there wasn’t another option. You did it, the glory is yours. And to you we dedicate our lives.

When we note the objectives you planned to achieve, we see that one goal was to make an association of like-minded devotees—a *sādhu-saṅgha* that in due course would be endowed with knowledge gained from you, would realize your teachings gained from experience, would appreciate them in communion, and would be eager to share their realizations with others.

In the ecstasy of a rip-roaring *kīrtana,* the intensity of a *bhajan*, the celebration of a festival, being captured by the details of a drama or story or remembered “gem,” those elements around us are transformed into spiritualized elements of a transcendental world. Your world. An iron rod placed in fire becomes firelike, and you’ve set our world on fire, showing practically how to use what we come by on a day-to-day basis in developing Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You have made life childlike again. Discovery, excitement, appreciation, and sharing are offshoots of what we taste. You’ve made Kṛṣṇa consciousness uncomplicated and fun to be part of. We feel satisfaction in the understanding that you have given us everything we need.

By your mercy we find ourselves in a small community of devotees, each and every one of whom, I suggest, is as dedicated, loyal, and true to you in his or her own way as any devotee one would find anywhere.

Nourished by your mercy, by your teachings, by your association, by your devotees, by your *prasādam*, and through various other means, the members of this community live and thrive.

I humbly beg that you protect us all from all manner of material influences by keeping us always under your shelter. May we each be satisfied and absorbed in our attempts to repay our debt to you, although we all know it’s something that can never be repaid.

As a community we are confident in the availability of your mercy, which comes in many forms. Please allow us to recognize it, to store it for times of extra need, and to distribute it well.

*The community of aspiring devotees in Tauranga, on the Western Bay of Plenty, in and around Katikati, New Zealand.*  
(written by Jaya Tīrtha Charan dāsa)

## Tokyo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

We are still reading the offerings in your Vyāsa-pūjā book for the year 2001. And now it is time to prepare the offering for the year 2002. Yes, time does certainly fly by. And this year has been quite eventful: the world was shocked on 9 / 11, and also your dear disciple and friend Bhakta George has left our association. (Is he with you now?) We would like to take this opportunity to say “Thank you” for leaving Vṛndāvana and coming to the West to give us the most wonderful gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Not a day goes by when we cannot but appreciate the causeless mercy you are kindly bestowing upon us. We think there will be many “troubled waters” ahead—after all it is Kali-yuga. Even your ISKCON seems to be shaking. You could have easily stayed in Vṛndāvana. We’re sure you would have passed the remaining years of your life there peacefully chanting, reading, going on *parikramā*. Why take so much botheration, especially in old age? You were sixty-nine years old, your health was deteriorating, you had no money and no means of transport. And what about the “P” form? But against all odds you thought, “Let me try.” Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for trying. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for not giving up on us. Thank you for giving us your transcendental instructions. As George said, “Everything else can wait, but the search for God cannot wait.” We think he understood your message—the most important message for the whole world in these troubled times.

*From your tiny servants in Tokyo, Japan.*

## Vijayawada

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

By your unlimited blessings, we have inaugurated a temple in the historic city of Vijayawada, on the banks of the Krishna River, and installed Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara Deities under the divine guidance of His Holiness Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Mahārāja. All the devotees, life members, and donors are extremely inspired by this new temple. The temple is quite close to the historic Lord Nṛsiṁha temple in Mangalgiri. Thousands of devotees participated in the four-day installation celebration, from February 15 to 18, 2002. The temple inauguration took place on the auspicious Vasanta Pañcamī day as an offering for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace. The well-known Tridandi Nārāyaṇa China Jeeyar Swami of the Rāmānuja *āśrama* participated, along with the Gurukula School *brāhmaṇas*. Many other important local leaders, well-wishers, and townspeople also attended and were very happy. About 12,000 plates of *prasādam* were distributed.

On this most auspicious day we seek your divine blessings so that we can continue to serve your divine mission.

*Your most fallen servants at ISKCON Vijayawada, India.*  
(written by Murlimohan dāsa)

## Villaggio Hare Krsna

Dear Guru Mahārāja Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

By your mercy, this year I have the opportunity to offer for your pleasure this homage glorifying your wonderful qualities of *sādhu-bhūṣaṇam*.

It is very astonishing how in India your dear disciples are achieving wonderful things, since they are very attached to your lotus feet. In the month of January, at Kurukṣetra, a Ratha-yātrā was started that continued for one month, until February 25, before reaching Vṛndāvana. This is a historical achievement: never before had there been a month-long Ratha-yātrā. Also, on February 17 a very nice temple glorifying your wonderful preaching efforts was opened in Surat, Gujarat. Yaśomatīnandana Prabhu engaged everyone in your service for the opening of the marvelous temple. During December, the Glory of India Temple in New Delhi distributed many thousands of your *Bhagavad-gītās* in honor of Gītā Jayantī. All this is due to the power contained in your instructions. The secret is to follow your invaluable instructions in your wonderful books, and then everything will follow.

Also in the month of December, though it was extremely cold—more so than in other winters—the *saṅkīrtana* devotees still distributed your fantastic books for your pleasure. I thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your compassion is so great and your loving nature so overwhelming that they are not possible to measure. On this very auspicious day I pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I can continue to glorify you by distributing your majestic books, since you possess many, many wonderful qualities that are too numerous to describe.

Regarding the festival in Pune, which also took place this year, this is the sixteenth time this wonderful *sādhu-saṅgha* gathered together. The devotees were all full of ecstasy as they recalled the time you were present and blessed many, many conditioned souls. Thank you again for your compassion and infinite mercy on all of us. You are an ocean of compassion, with an incredible amount of love for me and for everyone. All glory to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the most excellent spiritual master in the history of the preaching culture.

*Your devotees at Villaggio Hare Krsna, Italy.*  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Villa Varṣāṇā

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances under your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

All glories to the footprints of those lotus feet that you have engraved on the earth of our souls! All glories to the temple for the whole world that you have built with your divine lotus palms! All glories to the compassionate glance that you have cast upon disenchanted humanity with your penetrating lotus eyes! All glories to the vibration of transcendence flowing from your divine lotus mouth to inundate the ears with “all the truth regarding Vaiṣṇava philosophy”!

You have left an indelible mark on the history of humankind. History is now divided into two periods: before and after His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

The process of transformation of consciousness and purification of heart that you have set into motion has been greatly activated by your very self. That is the nature of your immense potency.

You have brought the type of consciousness you have called “Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” You have brought the faith, you have brought the trust and the concern, you have brought the compassion, the mercy, purity, gentleness, you have brought the strength and humility. You have brought light where there was only darkness. You have brought the existence of high consciousness.

You have brought the real beauty, you have brought *Kṛṣṇa*!

You have brought Kṛṣṇa with Rādhā, Balarāma, Caitanya, the *avadhūta* Nitāi, the Gosvāmīs. You have brought the *mahā-mantra*! You have brought *kīrtana* and *prasādam*. You have brought Vṛndāvana, Yamunā, Tulasī, and Śrī Mūrti.

You have brought the *Gītā,* the *amala-purāṇa, caitanya-līlā*, the *paramparā*. You have brought the Pāṇḍavas, the Kumāras, Nārada, Bali, Dhruva, Prahlāda, and Lord Nṛsiṁha. You have brought devotional service! You have brought so many things into our empty lives. “Thank you” is a such little word. How can we repay our debt?

You have struck us with wonder, divine swan! You are the personification of the twenty-six qualities of a Vaiṣṇava.

Now that you have gone out of our limited vision, please reappear in your *vāṇī* form and penetrate the thick layers of our crippled minds, to make us *sādhus,* to make us your ideal reflections, all of us.

You have made us Godbrothers, Godsisters. Let us realize the deep meaning of this. Teach us within how to treat the Vaiṣṇavas with tenderness, how to treat the innocent with softness. Teach us how to become the servants of all without selfishness or desire for greatness, in the integral sense and with fair spirit. Teach us how to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness directly and indirectly to create the favorable conditions around us for the cultivation of Vaiṣṇavism.

Don’t let us lose a single one of those “little ones” who happen to pass by one of your temples to get Lord Caitanya’s grace.

Teach us to fight the inward battle against the devil ego and defeat and kill him. Denouncing the *māyā* civilization, you are the chief commander of the army of spiritual warriors fighting the modern War of Kurukṣetra. You have shown the fallacy of sense gratification and put the emphasis on self-realization.

Empower us to become your true representatives, individually and collectively, to unite in diversity with the cement of respect and honesty in relationships and cooperative service.

Let it happen here and now, in this temple and in all other temples, in this zone and in all other zones, here and now all over the world, here and now before the night of Kali comes down.

Please save us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, save us once more, once for ever, all of us! All of us offer our obeisances under your lotus feet again and again.

His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

*Your humble servants at Villa Varṣāṇā, the ISKCON temple in Malaga, Spain.*  
(written by Brajishma dāsa)

## Vṛndā-kuṇḍa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most sacred day, along with all your followers around the world, we humbly prostrate ourselves at your divine lotus feet. Those of us who are fortunate enough to serve your mission here in Vraja especially want to thank you for your unlimited compassion of spreading the glories of this transcendental land of Lord Kṛṣṇa throughout the whole world. After spreading those glories, you then returned and increased the glories of Vraja by establishing the most beautiful and glorious temple of Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma.

When that temple was first built, some people used to ask why you built a temple so far away from town, out in the wilderness. Who would go there? But now we see every day so many thousands of pilgrims from all over India and from all parts of the world coming daily to take *darśana* of the most enchanting Deities of Gaura-Nitāi, Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, and Rādhā-Śyāmsundara. On some days the temple is so completely crowded that we like to think you must be smiling down from your place in Goloka Vṛndāvana to see all the people who have come. You must be proudly pointing out to your dear friend, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, how many souls have come to hear the ecstatic chanting of the holy name in your palacelike temple.

You not only spread the glories of Vṛndāvana, but also the glories of Tulasī Devī, the sacred plant most dear to your Lord. In fact, Vṛndāvana bears one of the names of Tulasī Devī, Vṛndā. Thus we see that the holy place where Vṛndā Devī eternally resides has come to your wonderful ISKCON movement.

Recently, the historic Kurukṣetra-to-Vṛndāvana Ratha-yātrā also came to Vṛndā-kuṇḍa. The devotees there were supremely ecstatic to see Lord Jagannātha’s flower-decorated *ratha* coming down the road. Then the Deities were taken from the *ratha* and placed in our temporary temple. Meanwhile you remained high on your *vyāsāsana* on the *ratha* while it was being turned around. Suddenly we saw you so mercifully glancing over the project and seeing the elaborately hand-carved sandstone temple that is under construction there.

When we saw your kind glance, we were reminded of the seven purposes for which you established this ISKCON movement. The fifth is: “To erect for the members and for society at large a holy place of transcendental pastimes dedicated to the Personality of Kṛṣṇa.”

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! On your holy Vyāsa-pūjā day, we want to thank you from the bottom of our hearts for so kindly engaging us in fulfilling one of your purposes of establishing ISKCON. By your mercy, this exquisite temple will be inaugurated this year and increase your glories as the transcendental ambassador of Goloka Vṛndāvana.

*Your humble servants at Vṛndā-kuṇḍa, Vṛndāvana, India.*

## Vṛndāvana

**The Opulences of the Supreme Spiritual Master, Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, who are capable of delivering unlimited fallen souls! All glories to your divine instructions, which can free the world from the clutches of illusion!

The Supreme Lord is full in all opulences, and you are the representative of those opulences. You are as good as the Supersoul seated in our hearts. You are the beginning, the middle, and the end of our existence. Of lights you are like the sun, for you illuminate the world with transcendental knowledge, and among the stars you are like the moon, for you relieve the world with your cooling instructions. Of the *Vedas,* you give the most essential wisdom in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and of demigods you are like Indra, for you wield the thunderbolt of transcendental knowledge. Of the senses you are like the mind, for you guide our acts, and you are our living force. Of Rudras you are like Lord Śiva, the greatest Vaiṣṇava, who is especially merciful to the most fallen souls. Of the Yakṣas you are like the lord of wealth, Kuvera, for you possess the greatest treasure, wisdom; of Vasus you are like Agni, for you can burn the seed of material desire from our hearts. Of mountains you are like Mount Meru, for you are most lofty in your spiritual height. Of priests you are the chief, for you can transform *mlecchas* into *brāhmaṇas*. Of generals you are like Kārttikeya, for you successfully lead the forces of devotees against Kali’s attack. Of bodies of water you are like the ocean, for you are a sea of transcendental love of Godhead. Of great sages you are like Bhṛgu, for you are a most powerful sage who can identify the Supreme. Of vibrations you are like the *praṇava, oṁkāra,* from whom all transcendental sound vibrations emanate, for you have delivered the *mahā-mantra,* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Of immovable things you are like the Himālayas, for you never swerve from the path of devotion. Of trees you are like the banyan tree, for you spread the *bhakti-vṛkṣa* throughout this world. Of sages you are Śrī Nārada’s direct representative. You are as gifted as the Gāndharva Citraratha, for you have brought to the world devotional *bhajans*. You are like Kapiladeva among perfected living beings, for you have presented His teachings exactly. You have appeared from the ocean of nectar of devotion, and among learned men you are their leader. Of weapons you carry the thunderbolt of absolute knowledge, and like a *surabhi* cow you yield unlimited nectarean instructions that are like sweet milk. You have inspired the procreation of societies of devotees and have caused the birth of new spiritual love for the Supreme.

Of the many-hooded Nāgas, Ananta is the one you represent, for He is an expansion of the original guru, Śrī Balarāma, and chants the Lord’s glories with His thousands of tongues. You are like Varuṇa, for you preside over the spiritual ocean of *bhakti* and the devotee-aquatics who reside there. Of dispensers of law, the *mahājana* Yama is who you resemble, he who uplifts the miscreants of the world through correction. You are like Prahlāda Mahārāja, a great devotee from childhood, and of subduers of bogus philosophers you are as formidable as eternal time.

Among beasts you are like the powerful lion, a *siṁha-guru,* and among birds you are like Garuḍa, for you act as a conveyance for the Lord’s eternal message. Of purifiers you are like the wind, which can purify even the most impure without becoming contaminated. Of wielders of weapons you are as mighty as Lord Rāma, who can defeat the demons in this age. Of fishes you are as fearful as the shark for the pseudo practitioners of *bhakti*. You are the personification of the spiritual science of the self, the giver of conclusive truth.

You are like the powerful Gaṅgā, purifier of the three worlds. You are a representative of Lord Brahmā and a personification of all glorious qualities. You are giver of the Gāyatrī mantra, and you bring delight to everyone by harvesting devotion in their hearts. Your instructions are as pleasant as flower-bearing spring, and your personality is splendid. You are the best of cheats, for you can trick anyone into performing devotional service. You are always victorious in devotion and enjoy the adventures of a traveling *sannyāsī*. You are the strength of the devotees and a friend to the Lord, as dear as Arjuna, for through you Śrī Kṛṣṇa delivered the *Bhagavad-gītā* to the world.

You are a representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, for you presented the *Vedas*, and you are a representative of Vāsudeva, for you revealed His transcendental glories. You know how to punish and be affectionate simultaneously, and you have become victorious by advocating morality. You are the example of true silence, for you speak only *kṛṣṇa-kathā*. You are the giver of spiritual wisdom and the generating seed of spiritual existences; there is no follower of yours who can exist without your grace. There is no end to your causeless mercy, and this is a mere indication of your glories.

Whatever acts of devotion are performed by your devotees spring from but a spark of your devotion. With your transcendental instructions you maintain the entire ISKCON society. We offer our humble obeisances unto your lotus feet and meditate upon your glories on this auspicious day of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

*Your servants at ISKCON Vṛndāvana, India.*

## Yogyakarta

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to your lotus feet!

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

As a fallen soul, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am very grateful for your kind charity. Without your gift of the *paramparā* system, I could never have understood about this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Now, in some small way I am trying to follow your teachings.

Please give me your mercy so that this unworthy soul can continue to perform devotional service. By these insignificant activities I hope to become purified and reduce the results of my sinful activities. I know this is very difficult for me, but I believe that through the directions and guidance of your disciples I will be able to increase my *bhakti* in running this small preaching center here in Yogyakarta, Indonesia.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for engaging me in your service.

*Your servants in Yogyakarta, Indonesia.*  
(written by Śubhānanda Caitanya dāsa)

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Brazil)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humblest obeisances at your lotus feet, shelter for all mankind. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your books are the life and soul of all civilized, intelligent humans. Unfortunately, many of them don’t know this. Thus it is our crucial duty to eradicate this mistake by continuously publishing and distributing your books far and wide. Kṛṣṇa explains in the *Gītā* that out of thousands of men, only one will be interested in perfecting his life, and out of those, only a rare few actually understand Him. Just see what an awesome task we have taken up! If we see how many literatures have been distributed to date (over 460 million!) and how many actual devotees of Kṛṣṇa there are (a few million at best), then we can see that for every devotee made, hundreds of literatures were distributed. Thus if we wish to make only 10% the world population into devotees, we’ll need to distribute *128 billion pieces of literature*! And that’s in the space of a single generation! So we certainly have our work cut out for us.

However, as any devotee knows, none of this happens without your direct and all-powerful mercy. Not a page gets published, not a book gets distributed, without your mercy. Thus, it is with great humbleness and desire to please you that we here beg for your mercy so that we may play our part in this most wonderful pastime. Your mercy is always available. Actually, there is no limit to what can be done by any human who has received your mercy—such is your power. However, it depends on the recipient to know how to accept it. But we are so fallen that we do not even know how to accept your mercy. As such, we are begging not only that you shower us with your mercy but that you empower us to realize this great goal despite our inability to so much as accept your mercy.

*Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Brazil.*  
(written by Giridhārī dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your most auspicious appearance day!

On this most auspicious occasion, we, your humble servants working in your BBT group, humbly beg for your mercy so that we can say something to properly glorify you from our minute realization through doing this specific service.

You are the *jagad-guru* of the whole world.

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

The whole world is burning up in the all-devouring fire of material existence, *saṁsāra*. Out of your great compassion, you, as the direct representative of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, have descended to deliver the most unfortunate souls in this Kali-yuga. Your words, in the form of your volumes of books, are like the soothing rain pouring from heavy clouds, which is the sole yet very powerful means to extinguish the huge fire in the forest of this material existence.

As the bona fide spiritual master situated in the disciplic succession descending from the original Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you are the personification of the *Vedas*. The knowledge in the form of the *Vedas,* spoken from the lotus mouth of Kṛṣṇa, is directly passed down through the chain of great authorities such as Brahmā, Nārada, Vyāsadeva, and Śukadeva Gosvāmī. And finally this knowledge, exactly in its original form, was given to you by your spiritual master. Specifically empowered and favored by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, knowing the purpose and essence of the *Vedas,* considering the extremely serious situation of this Kali-yuga, and clear about the most efficient way to deliver people in this most unfortunate age, you extracted the essence and the conclusion of this knowledge and presented it in the most simple and easy-to-understand form. You strictly followed Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instruction: *yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*. Absorbed in such pure consciousness, you presented the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* not only in their original form, without the least deviation, but never failing to directly point out their essence and conclusion, that is, the devotional service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa: *man-manā bhava mad-bhakto mad-yājī māṁ namaskuru*. You teach every person to directly approach Kṛṣṇa, offer obeisances to Him, think of Him, pray to Him, worship Him, and thus become His devotee. And finally, *sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja:* everyone should completely surrender to Kṛṣṇa only. Therefore, the knowledge you present is the most pure, without any tinge of the material energy, namely karma (fruitive activity, which the materialists are attached to) or *jñāna* (philosophical speculation, which the impersonalists are attached to).

You are a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa, a *mahā-bhāgavata* and *uttama-adhikārī*. You deliver us a clear understanding of the Supreme Lord, His various energies, the living entities, and the constitutional relationship between the Lord and the living entities. Anyone who hears from you submissively, with faith, can give up all misunderstandings manufactured by his crooked mind under the influence of Māyā, the illusory material energy, and thus begin to render devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. You describe pure *kṛṣṇa-kathā* as talks about Kṛṣṇa’s name, form, qualities, and pastimes, etc. Anyone who hears *kṛṣṇa-kathā* from you constantly and submissively, with faith, will have his heart totally cleansed. By taking up the process you recommend in your books, especially the nine devotional activities headed by hearing of, chanting about, and remembering the Lord, one can attain the final goal of life, reinstatement in one’s original relationship with Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we, who are most unqualified both in spiritual understanding and in our command of translation techniques, humbly pray for your mercy, so that we may focus on your lotus feet, gradually understand a little bit more about your exalted position and glories, totally depend on your mercy when serving your words, which are nondifferent from yourself, surrender our mind and intelligence in your service, and thus fulfill your desire to do it expertly yet without any deviation.

*Your humble servants at the Khasa division of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.*

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to you!

We here at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust have accepted as our life and soul the mission you gave us: to publish your books and distribute them, as much as possible. We are happy to report that one of your dearmost book distribution programs—the BBT Library Party—has been revived in a new form. Now named the Prabhupāda Rasāmṛta Trust, the new party is led by Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa Prabhu and Viśvaretaḥ Prabhu. Once again, full sets of your books are being distributed to university libraries all over North America! Here are a few of the wonderful letters university administrators have sent Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa (they know him as Mr. Walthall) in appreciation of your books (They refer to “gifts” and “donations” because the *lakṣmī* for the sets is collected separately.):

September 13, 2001

Dear Mr. Walthall,

On behalf of St. Mary’s College of Maryland Library, I would like to thank the Trust for the generous gift of the Srila Prabhupada Collection, including the eighteen volumes of the Srimad Bhagavatam. The books are very beautifully produced, and will be of interest to students in many academic departments. The faculty member who specializes in Vedic Philosophy is particularly grateful for the gift, as he can now direct students to a broader range of primary Vedic literature, which until now has been represented in our collection by excerpts and commentaries. Thank you for your generous contribution to the intellectual life of our college. We look forward to seeing the books well-used by generations of students and faculty.

Yours truly,

K.N.,  
St. Mary’s College of Maryland

\* \* \*

November 14, 2001

Dear Mr. Walthall:

I am writing to express our appreciation for your donation of an excellent collection on the culture of India. The 26 volumes, including the Bhagavat Purana, by Srila Prabhupada, A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami are valuable additions to the religious, philosophical, and cultural collections of the Humanities, Social Science, and Education Library. Libraries at Purdue will benefit from this donation. With it, and thanks to you, we will be more capable of achieving our mission and serving the students and faculty of the Purdue community.

Sincerely,  
S.S.,  
Purdue University

\* \* \*

September 20, 2001

Dear Mr. Walthall:

I would like to thank you and the Prabhupada Rasamrta Trust for your generous donation of the 18-volume set of the Srimad Bhagavatam, with the text in Sanskrit and English, and extensive commentaries and illustrations; and of a number of related works by A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada. These volumes will enhance the Libraries’ collections in Indian religion, philosophy, and culture.

Sincerely,  
M.W.,  
Fordham University

\* \* \*

June 4, 2001

Dear Mr. Walthall,

Thank you for your donation of the Bhagavat Purana Collection. Our students and faculty will value these new translations and commentaries. They will become the centerpiece of our collection on Indian philosophy and religion.

Sincerely yours,  
S.M.,  
Colby College

\* \* \*

November 30, 2001

Dear Mr. Walthall,

I would like to thank you on behalf of the LSU Libraries for the splendid collection provided by the Trust, for the 18-volume Srimad Bhagavatam written by Srila Prabhupada, A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami and the accompanying texts. This is a very scholarly edition that does an excellent job in elucidating the difficult text of the Bhagavad-gita; it should be invaluable to Western scholars. We are delighted to have the opportunity to add A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada’s works to our collection and are very grateful for your generous donation.

Sincerely,  
W.A.,  
Louisiana State University

\* \* \*

January 16, 2002

Dear Mr. Walthall,

Thank you for your gift—we will use it well. With the support and encouragement of benefactors like you, we are able to serve the ever-changing needs of a dynamic university and create a library collection with breadth, depth, and distinction.

Sincerely,  
M.R.C.G., Chancellor  
University of California at Santa Cruz

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we trust you would be very pleased with how your books are being enthusiastically welcomed in the academic community. Please let us facilitate the widespread distribution of your transcendental literatures eternally.

*Your servants at the North American offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, in Los Angeles, California, USA.*

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please allow me to bow down respectfully at your merciful lotus feet and take shelter of them.

The huge material cosmos is impregnated with innumerable fallen spirit souls who are perpetually struggling and suffering in various forms of life in different types of imperfect bodies. The darkness of ignorance is so intense and covering that the souls do not remember who they are. Hardly anyone asks the essential questions: “Why am I here? What is the purpose of my existence? Who am I? What am I?”

In ignorance the living entity accepts his fallible body to be the self, and consequently he indulges in gratifying the senses of that body and believes that to be happiness. Then he experiences constant fear due to identification with a temporary body. That fear makes him lament. Never is there even one drop of genuine bliss, love, happiness, or relief for a conditioned soul.

That was my past, my state of existence and consciousness, before I came in contact with you. It is one of the greatest miracles for a soul entrapped in a material body to come in contact with a true spiritual master, a deliverer of factual and essential knowledge.

Years ago, when I lived in utmost anxiety and total frustration, a wonderful book was put into my hands. Prior to that fateful event, I knew I was suffering but I didn’t know why. I had been looking for God, for the truth behind all other truths, but I didn’t know the way. Nor did I know who I was. Like a patient who has lost his memory after a severe accident, I had entirely lost all knowledge about my true identity.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it was as if you were informing me that in actuality I was a beloved daughter of a great king and my father was longing to see me again.

The book in my hands felt heavily charged with uncommon knowledge, while my heart felt lighter and lighter. Then you answered all the questions that arose in my mind, even the ones I had not properly formulated yet. The more I read, the more my anxieties and fear of dying vanished. You gave me an astonishing view of the material cosmos and my position in it. The books you have brought to us are full of descriptions of ancient cultures, history, human psychology, human relations, technology, warfare, metaphysics, and, most importantly, the topmost transcendental secrets and techniques.

All of a sudden everything was available: the ever-expanding spiritual world, with all its wonders, and especially Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself, along with His associates and exalted devotees. Everything was there, right in that book.

You explained so nicely how I could revive my relationship with God and return to Him. You offered access to the Supreme Person without the slightest hesitation or consideration of the mostly degraded situation of us Westerners. You widened my vision unlimitedly. You opened my blind eyes. All of a sudden there was the chance to see things as they are. I fell in love with that First Canto. It became my greatest treasure, a worshipable Deity.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I do not know if I have ever sincerely thanked you for all this. Please let me do it now. Thank you a million times for all your hard work and sacrifices. And thanks for not considering us unqualified for receiving Vedic knowledge.

When you dictated the books into your Grundig tape recorder, you didn’t do this with a few highly intelligent scholars in mind. You considered that people have different intellectual capacities. Sometimes you wrote in a highly sophisticated way, and sometimes you wrote in a style that even a small child can understand. I really appreciate that. When it gets too complicated for my small brain, I just continue reading until the next passage that you wrote for simpletons like myself.

Your explanations are sublime and brilliant, and the examples and analogies are very helpful. Indeed, your reader benefits from every contact with your writings.

As you yourself so aptly wrote to the German devotees in May 1977:

Whoever gets a book is benefited. If he reads the book he is benefited still more, or if he gives the book to someone else for reading, both he and the other person is benefited. Even if one does not read the book but simply holds it and sees it, he is benefited. If he simply gives small donation towards the work of Kṛṣṇa consciousness he is benefited. And anyone who distributes these transcendental literatures, he is also benefited.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we often regret that we never met you in person. But you are so much present in your writings that we strongly feel your personal presence and loving association when reading your translations and purports.

Please ask Kṛṣṇa not to forget His servants at the North European BBT. We are little spiders in your global publishing mission, but together with all the other devotees around the world, we’ll continue building that bridge to the spiritual world that you masterminded and began to build. We won’t stop until everyone who wants to has crossed the vast ocean of suffering and arrived at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. There we will surely meet.

*Your aspiring servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Northern Europe.*

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

The most significant development for *Back to Godhead* in the past year was that BTG and the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust launched an ambitious project to increase the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness through the Internet. Although this medium was unavailable for use in your mission during your time with us, we’re confident that you are pleased to see us taking advantage of it for Kṛṣṇa’s service. If the printing press is the *bṛhad-mṛdaṅga,* the World Wide Web must be the *bṛhad-bṛhad-mṛdaṅga*.

You were always eager to give value to the inventions of the nondevotees by using their materialistic progress to defeat their material illusion. You challenged us to use our intelligence to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to always be on the lookout, as you were, for novel ways to inject the antidote for Kali-yuga into the tortured minds of the misled masses. Through the Internet we can reach the masses in every country of the world in seconds, opening up a broad new avenue for increasing the distribution of your books and *Back to Godhead*. Our focus, as always, is to bring people to the shelter or your books and magazine.

We older members of the BTG staff wish to thank you for sending us bright, enthusiastic members of ISKCON’s second generation to help us distribute your books by producing a world-class web site. Their inspired service to you keeps the project moving forward.

Thank you for allowing us to take part in your great mission of mercy. We are starting to see the unlimited potential for its worldwide influence.

*Your servants at Back to Godhead and Krishna.com.*

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

This year will mark the 25th anniversary of your disappearance from this planet. It marks service in separation for twice as long as the time that ISKCON was blessed to have your personal *darśana*. As time goes by we appreciate just how great your achievements are. This year we have begun to compile a book displaying the Deities of various temples. As this project unfolds, we are astonished at the devotion and dedication of your followers in establishing and maintaining the standards of Deity worship throughout the world.

If this were your only accomplishment, it would be considered a monumental endeavor. That Westerners could adopt such complex rules and regulations and perform the necessary rituals, from waking the Deities at 4 a.m. in the morning to putting Them to rest in the evening and all the *pūjā* that goes on in between, is surely one of the great phenomena of religious history.

However, you did not just introduce some religious rituals; you also presented a philosophy, translated and created commentaries on the Vedic scriptures, and, in establishing the temples and *āśramas* of ISKCON, created a “transcendental lifestyle” for the whole world. The basis of this lifestyle is surrender to your instructions—to chant the holy name, to follow the regulative principles, and to preach the philosophy worldwide. This process, in the words of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* is everlasting and joyfully performed. Every day is a festival of the beauty of the Deity, the *prasādam* offered to the Deity and honored by the devotees, and the chanting and dancing both before the Deity and in public on the *harināma* parties.

Your achievements reflect your surrender to Kṛṣṇa and the reciprocation of the Lord with His devotee. We can only lament our lack of appreciation for these qualities and our reluctance to follow your instructions. You often described the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness as “simple but sublime.” We are so intent on appearing advanced and superior that we overlook the key quality of a devotee—humility. That humility is something we can grasp when we see your example and measure this against our own failings. We have everything to be humble about, and yet we allow our false ego to dictate against this.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day please give us the blessing to take your instructions to heart and cultivate the humility that will make this possible.

We can only beg to remain,

*Your humble servants at The Bhaktivedanta Archives.*

## Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Loving Grace! All glories to your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā!

Your Divine Grace once asked your disciple Upendra Prabhu if he was studying your books. He replied that he was reading them daily. Upon hearing his reply, you emphatically stated that he should study your books and not just read them. You then stated that your desire was that we should know the meaning of every word. Inspired by this and other, similar instructions, we at the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture (BCEC) have by our meager attempts continued to serve by holding structured courses for devotees and new members. Last year, by your mercy and the mercy of the rest of the *paramparā,* the courses were conducted in Ashcroft (British Columbia), Cape Town, Guyana, Durban, Houston, Johannesburg, Manila, Mauritius, Māyāpur, Nairobi, Pretoria, Puerto Rico, Toronto, Trinidad, and Vancouver. Since its inception in 1991, 6,179 students have graduated in various courses based on your books.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being the expert commander of our ship. By giving us the favorable winds—the transcendental literatures—you have afforded us with an opportunity to sail over the turbulent sea of this material world. As an erudite scholar, avid practitioner, pure devotee, and the founder / *ācārya* of ISKCON, you can comfortably be described without a doubt as a direct link to the previous *ācāryas* in disciplic succession. As such, you have conveyed to us the thoughts and moods of the Gosvāmīs so we may understand and enter the pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Although on many occasions Your Divine Grace mentioned that Kṛṣṇa dictated your purports, you also wanted us to present your purports in our own words. You mentioned that to become expert preachers all we have to do is cram your purports. On this auspicious day, we pray that you may bless us so that we may be able to absorb and digest your purports and thereby be able to present and live the teachings given in your Bhaktivedanta purports.

By your kind mercy you gave to us and the generations to come your very own self by giving us your books. As His Holiness Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja explained, “Śrīla Prabhupāda once said to me, ‘My books are better than me, because the best of me is in the books. I sit there, and every word that comes out is the very best of me.’” (from a class given in Belgium, 1 August 1989). Even though as grand-disciples we feel unfortunate that we missed your physical association, we can still feel close to Your Divine Grace by studying your books.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving yourself to us so completely in your books. You said, “Whatever I know I have explained in my books. Beyond that I have no knowledge.” (Conversation, 26 June 1975). Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are confident that by your continued blessings and guidance we will definitely be able to regain our eternal service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

*The members of the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture, Johannesburg, South Africa.*  
(written by Mādrī-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you were with us you showed us the true meaning of leadership. You were kind, bold, influential, humble, humorous, gentle, balanced, hard-working, adaptable, respectful, determined, an excellent communicator (both as a speaker and a writer), and above all, fully conscious of Kṛṣṇa.

You felt comfortable both in an Indian village and a Western metropolis. You could converse meaningfully with a simple worker and a head of state. You could quote with ease both the *Bhagavad-gītā* and Shakespeare. You knew business, math, the gentle art of persuasion, philosophy, and four languages. You cared for individuals and touched the hearts of all you met.

We at the BGIS are awed by your abilities and character, and we hope that this, your school, continues to strive to represent your qualities in our teachings, our teachers, and our students.

*Your servants, the staff and students at the Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School in Vṛndāvana, India.*

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Berkeley)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your Divine Grace has given us many instructions, but we should not become bewildered, thinking them impossible to fulfill. Instead, we should always meditate on them, and Your Divine Grace will reveal how they can be fulfilled. You will send all the men and *lakṣmī*. This we have seen practically, time after time.

For example, Your Divine Grace said:

Philosophy and science should be engaged to establish the glory of the Lord. Advanced people are eager to understand the Absolute Truth through the medium of science, and therefore a great scientist should endeavor to prove the existence of the Lord on a scientific basis. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.22purport\]

Your Divine Grace always looks to see the effort that we put into our service, but we have to depend on you for the results. Your Divine Grace has pointed to a possible approach to this endeavor in response to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.26.34:

This verse is the potential basis of great scientific research work, for it explains how subtle forms are generated from the ethereal element, what their characteristics and actions are, and how the tangible elements, namely air, fire, water, and earth, are manifested from the subtle form.

Philosophers and scientists in the West have agonized over the relationship between mind and body, and because they are ignorant of your purports, they think that the mind somehow arises from the body. Biologists strive for a scientific theory for the origin of form. (Darwin gives only a historical account.) In this purport (to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.26.34) Your Divine Grace speaks of the influence of subtle forms.

In our attempts to fulfill your instructions regarding the development of the Bhaktivedanta Institute, we have begun to get a glimpse of your own eternally glorious position as the best of the scientists and philosophers the world has ever seen, since we have begun to see that the above purport will prove to be the guiding light for modern science for the next 10,000 years, the duration of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Our worshiping of Kṛṣṇa, that is our internal affair. The external affair—we need to establish this theory. Otherwise they’ll be leading this society. Misleading. They are misleading, not leading, misleading. So we have to stop this misleading. Make program how to do it. Because it is truth, you will come triumphant. There is no doubt about it. It is truth. Now you have to know how to present the truth. That is your business. We are not presenting something theories, concocted by my brain. No. This is the fact. Rudimentarily we have got evidences, but it has to be presented by the modern ways. \[Morning Walk, 3 May 1973, Los Angeles\]

Your Divine Grace has given us many realizations of how to bring the scientists to your lotus feet. To develop these realizations fully, we must be in a pure state of consciousness: purity is the force. On this most auspicious day, we beg for a drop of your own unlimited purity to sustain us in our service in the Bhaktivedanta Institute.

*Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute, Berkeley, California, USA.*

## College Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party (TSKP)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

After hearing a lecture you gave on Lord Nityānanda’s appearance day glorifying Him as *baladeva-tattva*, the source of spiritual strength, we are feeling a deeper appreciation for what is *guru-tattva*. The guru is he who bestows spiritual strength and who alone can deliver the shelter of *nitāi-pada-kamala* and thus the service of Lord Caitanya. As you stated,

Formerly there was no university. The university was in the cottage—Vyāsadeva. Vyāsadeva was writing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and all the *Purāṇas* in a cottage. The university was there. Who can produce such literature as Vyāsadeva has given? From any angle of vision, from literary point of view, from philosophical point of view—everything, so perfect, every literature, *Mahābhārata, Purāṇas,* and *Vedānta*. Veda-vyāsa, he has given. So there was no need of university. It required clear brain. That was to be done by the brahminical qualifications, *śamo damo titikṣā ārjava, jñānaṁ-vijñānam āstikyaṁ brahma-karma svabhāva* . Where is that education? This education, technical education, how you can very nicely hammer, this will not solve the problem. So if we want real solution of the problems, then our duty is first of all to take the shelter of *nitāi-pada-kamala*. Then we’ll be happy, and we’ll get moonshine, and our all fatigueness will be subsided. \[Lecture on Lord Nityānanda’s Appearance Day, Bhubaneswar, 2 February 1977\]

In our travels to the universities it is obvious that these institutions give the students at best some material strength. But as Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura says, *ahaṅkāre matta hoiyā, nitāi-pada pāsariyā, asatyere satya kori māni*. They are very puffed up with their mundane education and they lack humility. They have forgotten their relationship with Nityānanda and are thus captured by Māyā. They accept untruth as truth and truth as untruth.

Whereas the acquisition of material strength gives one the feeling of independence and false pride, the acquisition of spiritual strength gives one the realization that one’s real happy life is one of complete dependence on Kṛṣṇa. Your Divine Grace, as the authorized agent of Nityānanda or Balarāma, gives the spiritual strength by which one can realize this.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, just as Lord Nityānanda begged door to door for all to take the name of Kṛṣṇa, you also personally distributed the divine grace of Lord Caitanya in the form of the holy name to countless souls as you traversed the globe over a dozen times, requesting all to accept this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and distribute it to others. You requested us to do the same, so on your order only we travel to the universities.

But who are we to enter these training camps of Māyā to communicate that the real goal is to realize *jīvera ‘svarūpa’ haya—kṛṣṇera ‘nitya-dāsa’*, that our real happiness is in being eternal dependent servants of Kṛṣṇa? And how are we to cut through the pride and independence of the misguided souls found in America’s top educational institutions, those souls who have accepted as their guides similarly blind persons?

The strength to attempt such a thing can be had only by one who has received the shade of the lotus feet of Lord Nityānanda. And it is only by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we can receive even one ray of the moonshine from His feet. There is no other way.

On this auspicious day of your appearance, we, who are burning in the fire of material existence, desire to have the cooling shade of your lotus feet. Only by receiving your mercy can we hope to do something that may attract the merciful glance of Gaurāṅga and Nityānanda, without which our fortunate human birth is wasted.

Always begging the shelter of your cooling lotus feet, we remain

*The members of College TSKP.*

## Festival of India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. Hare Kṛṣṇa!

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-devamayo guruḥ*

**“‘One should understand the spiritual master to be as good as I am,’ said the Blessed Lord. ‘Nobody should be jealous of the spiritual master or think of him as an ordinary man, because the spiritual master is the sum total of all demigods.’” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.17.27, as quoted in** ***The Science of Self-realization*****)**

In February 1936, when speaking in honor of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī, you stated: “He has nothing to do with the affairs of this mundane world. He does not descend here to meddle with the affairs of temporary necessities but to deliver the fallen, conditioned souls.”It is clear that you are such a spiritual master. Everything you did—all the projects you started, the temples you opened, your translations and Bhaktivedanta Purports—were simply “to deliver the fallen, conditioned souls.”

*anāsaktasya viṣayān yathārham upayuñjataḥ*  
*nirbandhaḥ kṛṣṇa-sambandhe yuktaṁ vairāgyam ucyate*

*prāpañcikatayā buddhyā hari-sambandhi-vastunaḥ*  
*mumukṣubhiḥ parityāgo vairāgyaṁ phalgu kathyate*

**“When one is not attached to anything, but at the same time accepts everything in relation to Kṛṣṇa, one is rightly situated above possessiveness. On the other hand, one who rejects everything without knowledge of its relationship to Kṛṣṇa is not as complete in his renunciation.” (Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī,** ***Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu*** **2.255–256)**

Here again, these *ślokas* remind us only of you. You are the true real renunciant, not attached to anything but fully accepting and engaging everything in Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s service, and all “to deliver the fallen, conditioned souls.”

*āpane ācare keha, nā kare pracāra*  
*pracāra karena keha, nā karena ācāra*

*‘ācāra’, ‘pracāra’, nāmera karaha ‘dui’ kārya*  
*tumi sarva-guru, tumi jagatera ārya*

**“Some behave very well but do not preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whereas others preach but do not behave properly. You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore you are the spiritual master of the entire world, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world.” (Sanātana Gosvāmī speaking to Haridāsa Ṭhākura,** ***Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya*** **4.102–103)**

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have inspired and continue to inspire innumerable conditioned souls to take up the practice of devotional service all over the world. When we hear Lord Nityānanda Prabhu’s pastimes with Jagāi and Mādhāi, we think of the thousands of Jagāis and Mādhāis to whom you have given and are still giving the seed of *bhakti*.

Remembering any one of your numerous transcendental qualities astounds us. We can directly perceive your transcendental association in your books, and especially at festivals in the *kīrtana, kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* and the words of your devotees when they are delivering your message.

Begging to become your sincere disciples,  
*Your servants with your Festival of India.*

## Global Varṇāśrama Educational, Social, and Cultural Organization

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We are pleased to inform you that in the last six months a group of devotees has been endeavoring to help promote your long-cherished desire of establishing *varṇāśrama* communities. The Varṇāśrama Educational Steering Committee (VESCO) was established solely for that purpose. Recently we set up a website fully dedicated to the cause of *varṇāśrama*. For your pleasure we here offer the Mission Statement and Mission Goals of this committee, with the hope that you will be pleased to bless us in this humble endeavor so we may serve you and your community of devotees all over the world.

It will only be by the combined efforts of all your sincere devotees that we can one day become successful in implementing many *varṇāśrama* communities. Our main business in these rural communities will be to protect Lord Kṛṣṇa’s cows, to grow our own food, and to progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is only by your mercy that we can achieve these goals. We therefore seek your mercy and pray that we can continue to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

**Mission Statement**

To help promote and establish the universal concepts and principles of *varṇāśrama-dharma* for the benefit of all individuals in society. This can best be achieved within an agrarian-based social structure, with special emphasis on family, culture, education, and devotion.

**Mission Goals**

1\. To establish local, regional, national, and continental Varṇāśrama Educational Steering Committees for the purpose of promoting *varṇāśrama-dharma*.

2\. To help set up models or micro-units of *varṇāśrama* communities (villages), where the principles of *varṇāśrama-dharma* and Vaiṣṇava *dharma* will be promoted through *varṇāśrama* colleges.

3\. To locate and secure suitable land for the purpose of establishing such *varṇāśrama* communities and for promoting the ideology of *varṇāśrama-dharma*.

4\. To demonstrate in a practical way how the principles of *varṇa* (categories of occupational engagement) and *āśrama* (categories of spiritual practice) are universal principles, the essence of which are found in all the major religious faiths, such as Islam, Hinduism, Christianity, Buddhism, Judaism, etc.

5\. To recruit and train qualified teachers in the various arts, sciences, and other fields of knowledge for the purpose of teaching at the *varṇāśrama* colleges and communities.

6\. To demonstrate the principles of self-sufficiency and localized economy based on the natural categories of the four *varṇas* and four *āśramas*.

7\. To systematically and methodically conduct seminars and workshops that will provide training and education for individuals in all *varṇas* and *āśramas*.

8\. To compile, publish, and distribute literature (pamphlets, booklets, books, magazines) for the sake of informing and educating people in general about *varṇāśrama-dharma*.

9\. To establish curricula and define other educational guidelines for the *varṇāśrama* colleges.

10\. To demonstrate a more natural and simple lifestyle based on the principles of God consciousness, keeping the traditional family unit as the nucleus and basis for healthy, peaceful, and progressive communities.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and please continue to engage us in your service.

*Your servants at the Global Varṇāśrama Educational, Social, and Cultural Organization (GLOVESCO).*  
(written by Bhakti Rāghava Swami)

## Hare Kṛṣṇa Center (Stockholm)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances.

All the devotees at Hare Kṛṣṇa Center (Stockholm) would like to offer our gratitude and thanks to Your Divine Grace for giving us an opportunity to serve Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Their mission. Developing a service attitude toward the instructions of the spiritual master will lead to success in our spiritual life and help us fulfill the mission of human life.

**Ignorance Is Darkness, Knowledge Is Light**

You opened our eyes to see the light,  
knowledge of Kṛṣṇa shining bright.

Held fast by Māyā’s spider web,  
eternal freedom to us you led.

No qualifications, the most fallen souls,  
yet your mercy takes us to higher goals.

By following your instructions, we have found,  
the tight knot of karma is slowly unbound.

This forest of illusion, a most dangerous place,  
but easily crossed by your compassionate grace.

To free the poor lost bewildered souls,  
to the Western world the truth you unfold.

We humbly fall at your lotus feet,  
the mercy of Kṛṣṇa we do seek.

Suffered so long in our selfish ways,  
confused and tired we walk in a daze.

By Kṛṣṇa’s grace we find the spiritual guide.  
By the Guru’s mercy he sits us by his side.

We humbly serve you because we know you care.  
A pure devotee’s association is very rare!

Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi *kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

*Your servants at the Hare Kṛṣṇa Center, Stockholm, Sweden.*  
(written by Advaita Ācārya dāsa and Arcyā-devī dāsī)

## Hare Kṛṣṇa School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Prabhupāda-śikṣā-sāra  
Seeking the Essence of Prabhupāda’s Teachings

Should our focus be on establishing *varṇāśrama,* a social structure to accommodate the whole world? Or perhaps on resurrecting your *gurukula* system of educating our children, to teach them sense control, tolerance, and simplicity by practicing it ourselves? Should we more actively support book distribution, placing time bombs in every home, office, library, and motel room in the world? Certainly we could become more attentive to our Deity worship and focus once again on punctuality and cleanliness. Or should we be preaching with more conviction and vigor on your behalf, defeating impersonalism and recruiting new members? Or perhaps we should take more care of the cows, work the bullocks, plough the land, and grow vegetables and grains so as to become more and more independent and self-sufficient? We could definitely strengthen our commitment to our family responsibilities and create stability and security for our dependents.

Shouldn’t we support the *brahmacārīs* and *brahmacārinīs* by providing facility for systematic training in the *āśrama,* offering shelter, direction, and guidance? Shouldn’t we be studying your books more carefully, developing a taste for the pastimes and activities of the Supreme Personality of Godhead? Or should we perhaps become more qualified academically, so as to smash the atheistic scholars and establish the supreme position of Kṛṣṇa? Should we be acquiring skills to communicate openly and honestly with each other?

Still others say we should live for an extended time in the holy *dhāma* and familiarize ourselves with the places of the Lord’s pastimes, or at least we should visit the *dhāma* on a regular basis. And there are others who say we should increase our enthusiasm for *harināma-saṅkīrtana* and distributing the holy name far and wide for the benefit of suffering humanity. Others say we should sing the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *bhajans* with more devotion and increase our meditation on the instructions of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *acaryas*. Still others say we should promote the Sunday Love Feasts and offer the conditioned souls the opportunity to relish *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* and insure their higher birth in the next life. And then others say we should perform more dramas of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes for our own benefit and the benefit of those who come to see such performances. Or should we not absorb ourselves in organizing big festivals like Ratha-yātrā and Janmāṣṭamī, or in constructing palatial temples for the glorification of Kṛṣṇa?

Of all the things we could and should be doing to strengthen your wonderful movement, the most important elements remain chanting sixteen rounds a day without offense, attending *maṅgala-ārati,* and associating with devotees.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray for the sincerity and simplicity to be able to serve this most essential of all instructions: to chant sixteen rounds a day, attend *maṅgala-ārati,* and always keep the association of the devotees. With the strength each individual derives from attentive hearing and chanting sixteen rounds a day, we can collectively achieve extraordinary results and build a house in which the whole world can live in peace and harmony.

*Your servants at the Hare Kṛṣṇa School in Auckland, New Zealand.*  
(written by Prāṇa dāsa)

## International Society for Cow Protection (ISCOWP)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You left your rooms at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara Mandir in Śrī Vṛndāvana *dhāma* and crossed the turbulent oceans of this planet to reach America, the land of cow-killers and drug-crazed youth. On your journey to us you suffered heart attacks on board the good ship *Jaladuta*. Your first year in America was austere and fraught with various frustrations. But your faith in the order of your Guru Mahārāja and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa never faltered.

Daily you persevered. Daily you translated the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*—word by word, verse by verse, page by page, chapter by chapter, canto by canto, so that we would be able to have the mercy of Your Divine Grace and ultimately the mercy of your Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja. He told you to distribute these holy *śāstras* in English to the English-speaking world. That you have done, as well as to people speaking many other languages.
